# Oxagen `apps/app`: rev1 architecture

> Current presentation scope: [ADR-130](../../docs/adr/ADR-130-spend-and-operator-feedback-ui.md) applies the canonical mockup layout while excluding DoD, witness, proof, and credit/trust scores. Its spend and correction flows take precedence over the historical page descriptions below.

> **Status**
>
> - Law for `apps/app` rev1. Every rev1 PR into `app-rebuild` conforms to this document; the invariants in §4 are enforced mechanically and the worklist in `architecture.worklist.json` is the order of work.
> - Adopted 2026-09-13.
> - Supersedes plan §4.5 (fixture adapter) and §4.12 (per-page×state e2e) where §9 says so; where this document and the plan or the spec disagree, this document wins.
> - Each open question in `architecture.worklist.json` carries the architect's recommendation. The recommendation is adopted unless the maintainer overrides it.

Paths are relative to `apps/app/` unless they start with `packages/`, `apps/`, `tools/`, `docs/`, `.github/`, `.claude/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, or are one of the repo-root files `turbo.json`, `eslint.tenancy-seams.mjs`, `capabilities.manifest.json`. ---

## 0. The call, in twelve lines

1. **Rev1 ships six pages with real UI:** Fleet, Run, Organization (People, API keys), **Billing**, **Skills** and **Audit**. It keeps the sign-in flows, organization creation, the invitation page, the CLI consent page and a thin shell. Agents, Tools, Steering and Spend exist as routes and each renders **one** shared `NotRecorded` state until its gap lane lands (§8, Gap lanes). Ontology is not built (decision log, 2026-09-14): the `ontology.*` / `graph.*` capabilities stay on the API and MCP. Skills ships the resolution surface (decision log, 2026-09-18; ADR-090, which reverses the 2026-09-15 narrowing): `/{org}/{ws}/skills` governs which skills a workspace's `.oxagen/skills.toml` lets an agent find, which it may load, what that cost and what was held back — Catalog, Search, In the loop, Reflection and Versions — with the observed-inventory read (`list_skills` over `tacho.sessions.skills_available`) kept as its own section beside the resolved catalog (#3098). Audit ships as what is built today on the new skin (decision log, 2026-09-15; #3097): `/{org}/audit` reads the organization's control-plane events through `query_audit_log` and signs their export through `export_audit_events`, the recorder keeps the write-once archive at seal, the legacy `/{org}/security/audit` route lands on the page, and `/{org}/security/**` and `/{org}/access/**` otherwise redirect to `/{org}`. The in-app agent stays: the shell's assistant flyout, notifications and ⌘K are rev1 shell features built by the #2968 lane on `stella-serve` per ADR-053, not cut.
2. **Billing sells governed action units (GAUs), never dollars of usage.** A subscription carries a bucket of GAUs per period; more GAUs are bought in unit quantities at the customer's contracted per-GAU rate, which the page prints. An org is prepaid by default: at exhaustion the recorder charges the saved payment method for a configurable number of blocks (auto top-up), and only when that cannot run is the next governed action refused. An org a platform operator has approved for invoice billing is never capped: overage is invoiced at period end, or as soon as `invoice_gau_max` GAUs accrue. On the GAU meter, money comes from two sources only: the contracted rate (and the block total it implies) and invoices (§3.9). The page prints them in the summary tiles, This month, Invoices, the price list and the Change plan dialog (INV-25). In-app AI usage is the second meter, in usage credits (1 credit = $0.01): the page prints the credit balance and sells credit top-ups for it (§1.4, §3.9, §9 2026-09-15).
3. **The contracted rate has one source of truth per customer:** a negotiated `billing.contract_terms` row when one is effective, otherwise the published figures on the `billing.plans` row of the org's entitled subscription. Nothing copies the tier into the org at creation, so a plan change is reflected on the next read (§3.9).
4. **Exactly one rev1 write is a governed action: `resolve_approval`.** Every console read the app makes declares `noBillingGate: true` (ADR-052 exclusion 2), so a page load or an SSE poll never meters or locks a customer out (§1.5, INV-28).
5. **The runtime fixture adapter is deleted.** Dev and e2e run against the real stack, seeded through package APIs. Nothing fixture-shaped exists under `src/`.
6. **An unbacked slice has no port method, no view model, no adapter code and no bespoke component.** A whole unbacked page is one row in `src/data/unrecorded.ts`; an unbacked in-page slice renders nothing at all, with one named exception (§3.6).
7. **One read path (`kernelRead`) and one write path (`kernelWrite`) into the kernel, both in `src/server/kernel.ts`.** The kernel enters the tenant scope itself; the app never calls `runInTenantScope`. Only `src/server/tenancy-lookups.ts` touches a database, for the reads that happen before a tenant scope exists. A ctx that was not minted by `src/server/viewer.ts` is refused at runtime by the shared core (§3.1, INV-02).
8. **New kernel contracts:** `list_runs`, `get_run`, `list_approvals`, org-scope `list_members`, `list_api_keys`, `authorize_cli`, `get_contract_rate`, `get_gau_bucket`, `purchase_gau_bucket`, `set_auto_topup`, `list_invoices`, and the platform-operator `set_org_billing_terms`; `get_action_usage` is retired with the dollar model it reports. The app does not read tables around a contract (CLAUDE.md:245).
9. **The billing backend moves from dollar-derived credits to GAU buckets** in its own worklist phase (D2), governed by a new ADR-055 that refines ADR-052 and a spec amendment that supersedes MC spec §12.1, A.8 and the App. E billing rows. The recorder debits the bucket, runs the auto top-up and checks the invoice threshold in one place; an hourly job closes each org's month and resumes settlements Stripe never answered; one settlement ledger records every charge (§3.9). Tokens and retention are not on the page (§1.4).
10. **Three kernel defects are fixed:** #2906 (`resolve_approval` matches only the row uuid), tacho ingest never writes `initiating_principal_id`, and the member handlers throw untyped `Error` strings. **Roles are checked in handlers** (`assertOrgRole`, §3.2): the kernel's IAM check allows every capability for a non-enterprise organization (`packages/iam/src/check-iam.ts:157-182`), so a contract's `defaultRoles` is documentation for Free, Build and Scale orgs. Maintainer decision 3 of 2026-09-15 turns every feature on for every tier, IAM included: WL-55 removes the tier gate, and the handler checks stay (§9).
11. **Every invariant has a mechanical check:** a lint rule, a nominal type, or an architecture test with a baseline that may only shrink.
12. **The e2e suite is exactly three tests in three files: `login`, `pay`, `page-load`.** Everything else is a unit or component test. The suite runs on a production build against seeded CI services. No other Playwright spec exists anywhere in the tree at cutover (INV-20).

---

## 1. Rev1 scope

### 1.1 The rule

A slice gets real UI in rev1 only if **both** hold:

- (a) it belongs to a page the maintainer named for rev1 — Fleet, Run, Organization, Billing, and Skills and Audit from 2026-09-15 — or the shell, sign-in, invitation, organization-creation or CLI-consent flows need it to reach one of those pages, or it is a kept slice of Agents, Tools, Steering, Spend, Run or the shell that its gap lane builds (§8, Gap lanes: #2952, #2953, #2955, #2956, #2957, #2958, #2961, #2962, #2963, #2964, #2967, #2968, #2970, #3098, as thinned by their scope notes of 2026-09-14); and
- (b) it reads and writes only through a kernel contract that exists today or lands in worklist phase D or D2.

A whole page outside the five renders `<NotRecorded section="…"/>` and nothing more until its gap lane lands. An in-page slice with no backing renders nothing (§3.6). A gap lane moves a slice into the first group, adding the contract (its backend half), then the port method, the view model, the adapter, the section and its tests at once (its app half). A slice the review cut — Run › Context, Tools › policy simulation, Cedar versions and the assurance suite, Spend › reconciliation, agent scores and percentiles, SSO/SCIM, data-plane UI (model funding is back, decision log 2026-09-18), two-person mandates, steering effect, retirement and promotion thresholds, Audit beyond the deprecated events viewer and its signed export — has no lane, no route, no `UNRECORDED` row and no placeholder: it is not in this release.

Why the rule is this strict:

- **70 of 83 port methods have no production caller today.** Only `features/shell/load.ts:25-30`, `features/onboarding/reads.ts`, `features/auth/invitations.ts:22` and `server/stream-feeds.ts:43` call `dataSource()`.
- **About 8.9k lines of live adapter code are unbound.**
- **Many reads marked "wired" can never return a row.** `mappers/runs.ts:318-322`, `mappers/approvals.ts:139-147`, `mappers/org.ts:302-306` set a required field to null.

### 1.2 Pages, sections, titles

Every route that renders a page has a catalog title key. The root layout sets `metadata.title = { default: t("app.name"), template: "%s · Oxagen" }` (today `layout.tsx:8-11` sets one constant title for every route). Each `page.tsx` exports `generateMetadata` returning `{ title: t(<key>) }` and renders `<PageHeader title={t(<key>)}>` from the same key, so the document title and the `h1` cannot drift. The page-load smoke asserts both (§6.3) from the oracle in `e2e/routes.ts`.

| Route | Page | Smoke oracle (`e2e/routes.ts`) | Real UI in rev1 (contract) | Renders `NotRecorded` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/login` `/signup` `/verify` `/two-factor` `/forgot-password` `/reset-password` | Sign-in flows | title `pages.login` … `pages.resetPassword`; anonymous | as merged (Better Auth) | — |
| `/invite/[token]` | Invitation | visited as `/invite/e2e-no-such-token`, anonymous, title `pages.invitationNotFound` (the found state's `h1` is `pages.invitation`; both states export the matching `generateMetadata`; the anonymous-found, signed-in-found, expired and wrong-email states are component tests) | public route (`proxy.ts:22-24`; the token is the capability). `readInvitation(token)` (§3.1) reads it with no session and `decideInvitation` (`features/auth/invitation.ts`) yields one of: not found; anonymous with a valid token → the invitation and "sign in as the invited address"; signed in as another address → wrong email; expired; invitee → view, accept, decline (`accept_member_invite`, `decline_member_invite` with `InviteeCtx`) | — |
| `/new-organization` | Create organization | title `pages.newOrganization`; owner | form → `create_org` (org, owner membership, IAM, first workspace) → Fleet | — |
| `/cli/authorize` | CLI consent | title `pages.cliAuthorize`; owner; query = a valid PKCE set built in `routes.ts` (`redirect_uri=http://127.0.0.1:53682/callback`, `state`, `code_challenge` = base64url(sha256(verifier)), `code_challenge_method=S256`; the required set is `cli-authorize.ts:53-67`) | `requireUser()` → picker from `pretenant.orgs` and `pretenant.workspaces(orgSlug)` (§3.3) → consent → `authorize_cli` → loopback redirect (`LoopbackUri`) | — |
| `/cli/complete` | CLI login complete | title `pages.cliComplete`; **anonymous** | the last leg of `oxagen login`: the CLI's loopback listener 302s the browser here once the token exchange succeeds (`apps/cli/src/auth/loopback-login.ts`, `cliLoginCompleteUrl`), so the flow ends on the app origin rather than on `127.0.0.1:<port>` (#3076). The page resolves no viewer and reads nothing, and is public in `proxy.ts`: the browser that finished the exchange may hold no app cookie, so a gate would end a successful production sign-in on `/login` and a 404 for a signed-in one (#3091). It renders one `ok` outcome panel under its `PageHeader`; there is no other state, because the CLI redirects here only after the token is in the terminal and renders its own error locally when it is not | — |
| `/github/setup` | Callback (`route.ts`, not a page) | not in the smoke (no `page.tsx`) | auth gate, then `/` | — |
| `/` | Root | `expect: { redirectTo: "/e2e-org/core" }`; owner | `requireUser()` → `pretenant.orgs` → first org by membership → `pretenant.workspaces(orgSlug)` → its first workspace → `redirectTo(routes.fleet(...))`; no org → `/new-organization` (`features/shell/landing.ts`, §3.3) | — |
| *(org layout)* | Shell | — | sidebar (9 links in the mockup's order: Fleet, Agent IAM, Tools, Skills, Steering, Spend; Organization, Billing, Audit — Skills joined with #3098 and Audit with #3097; Run has no sidebar entry, it opens from the Fleet runs table; no Ontology); org and workspace switchers (`shell.context`: `list_orgs` + `list_workspaces`); user menu (sign out, theme); ⌘K over static routes; the **phone shell** — a fixed five-slot thumb bar (Fleet, Agents, Tools, Spend, More, with a count only where something waits on a person), a More sheet carrying Steering, Skills, Organization, Billing and Audit, dialogs as bottom sheets with a drag handle and a full-width footer button, list tables as labelled cards, a scrim behind the drawer, 44 px targets, 16 px inputs and safe-area insets (Mockups `origin/main` `mc.html:745-830`, `12826-12900`; `pages/<page>-<state>-mobile.html`). The thumb bar's Agents, Tools and Spend slots point at `NotRecorded` pages for the whole of rev1 and are kept deliberately, because the bar is the phone's only navigation and a four-slot bar would change again at every lane; the mockup's `audit` More-sheet entry returns with the page (decision log, 2026-09-15). Built by the #2968 lane (ADR-053, `stella-serve`), after WL-33: the assistant flyout (it stays in the sidebar, and the Mockups bottom-dock branch is not adopted, maintainer decision 9 of 2026-09-15) with turns recorded as runs and an honest engine-down state, notifications, ⌘K as `search_tools` over the app's own routes and records, nav counts, the Account dialog | Until the #2968 lane lands, not rendered at all: notifications, assistant flyout, Account dialog, nav counts, recent runs (WL-06 deletes the fixture-backed versions; the lane rebuilds them on live backing) |
| `/{org}/{ws}` | **Fleet** | title `pages.fleet`; owner | **Stat strip**, two tiles only: Live runs ("of N agents in this workspace") and Waiting on a human (with the oldest wait off the live approval clock) — both counted from `list_runs` and `list_approvals` with no new contract. The mockup's other two tiles, Spend today and Cache hit rate (the mockup's Fleet stat strip), are the cost rollup's and land with #2962's app half: `features/spend/fleet-tiles.tsx` reads `get_spend` at the model level over today (`spend.fleet`) and prints today's spend with its basis and cache_read ÷ (input_uncached + cache_read) over the day's model calls, each "not recorded" where the rollup has no figure, and draws no tile when the read fails; WL-34 places its two tiles beside them (G2962-app, #3065, maintainer decision 13 of 2026-09-15) · **Approvals** (`list_approvals`; `resolve_approval`; the auto-approval eligibility line on each card is #2970's, §8) · **Runs** table, cursor-paged (`list_runs`). The agent identity in a row is drawn **without the two scores**: the mockup's `agentCard` (`mc.html:11583-11596`) is one component in three layouts with a Trust and a Spend pill on every one, and the pills and their percentile bands are cut (#2969 closed), so rev1's row renders avatar, key and harness label and no `scorePair`. `list_runs` **excludes in-app-agent runs** from its first version: the assistant is Oxagen's, not the tenant's, so its turns are recorded as runs but never listed as the customer's (Mockups `71bc546`; the mockup keeps them in a separate `ASST_RUNS` list that Fleet's `RUNS.filter` at `mc.html:3990` never reads) · empty state with the `oxagen agent enroll` instruction · **the onboarding gate** above the strip while the organization's gate is open (#2967, ADR-065): the three-step rail from `get_onboarding_state`, the provisional-workspace banner with **Bind &lt;repo&gt;** while no main repository is bound, and the first-run banner naming the run the first frame opened. A refused gate read draws nothing, so Fleet's own sections report their own failures | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/runs/{run}` | **Run** | title `pages.run`; owner; the seeded run id | **Header** (`get_run`) · **Frames** for ledger runs (frame page + SSE resume) · **Approvals on this run** (`list_approvals {runId}` over `agent.approval_requests.run_id`, set from `ctx.agentRun`; WL-35, maintainer decision 8 of 2026-09-15) · **Halt** for wrapped runs (`dispatch_tacho_command` `kill`; disabled on ledger runs with the `run.halt.ledgerReason` copy until WL-61's ledger ingest contract gives a ledger run a revocable run token, maintainer decision 11 of 2026-09-15). Gap lanes, witness first: #2955 proof/witness tab (the flip, verdicts, proven spend), then #2952 recorder and replay, #2953 controls (pause, resume, cancel, steer); no Context tab (#2954 closed). **Every tab the mockup draws has an owner** (`runTabs`, `mc.html:4783-4790`): Transcript → #2952 · Governed actions/Player → #2952 · Proof → #2955 · Cost → #2962 (it needs `get_run_cost`) · Policy → #2958 (it is the auto-approval and rule half, with the eligibility line #2970 adds) · Chain and seal → #2951 · Context → cut. A **witness run** (`R.witnessFor`) renders its own four-tab set — Ladder, Frames, Cost, Chain and seal (`mc.html:4776-4781`) — on this same `/runs/{run}` route, so #2955 adds a variant and no route | `run.frames_wrapped` → G6 (the one in-page row, §3.6) |
| `/{org}/{ws}/agents` | Agents (Agent IAM) | title `pages.agents` | lane #2956 (+ #2957 mandates): identities list and agent detail — identity, credential broker (token exchange; the agent holds no credentials), roles, toolbelt, mandates; no scores or percentiles (#2969 closed). **The two lanes add exactly three routes**, so INV-20's count moves by a stated amount rather than by whatever a lane happens to build: #2956 adds `/{org}/{ws}/agents/{agent}` (agent detail, `pAgent`) and `/{org}/{ws}/agents/{agent}/source` (the `.oxagen/agents/<slug>.toml` definition editor with its own loading, error and denied states, `pAgentSource`, added in Mockups `bcc7049`) — 19 → 21 `page.tsx` files and `e2e/routes.ts` entries; #2957 adds `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}` (`pMandate`) — 21 → 22. Each route arrives with its `page.tsx`, its `REV1_ROUTES` entry, its title key and its `page-load` step in the lane's own PR. #2957's app half added the **Mandates** tab on agent detail (`list_mandates` narrowed to the agent, with the "No mandate" state that says a call carrying a consequence is denied before dispatch) and the **Request a mandate** dialog (`request_mandate`, which records a draft for the accountable role); the `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}` route landed on 2026-09-19 and has its own row below; the mandate id in this page's Mandates table links through to it. **Session 3 of the Mission Control build made the detail page write** (#2956, #2953, and the budget question in `docs/mission-control/BUILD-CHUNKS.md` §3), adding no route: **Identity** offers **Assign a role** and a per-row **Revoke** to an org Owner or Admin on an identity that is not retired (`assign_agent_role`, `revoke_agent_role`), and the picker offers only the roles `list_iam_roles` reports with `kind: "agent"`, which is the set the handler accepts, so it cannot name a role the write would refuse; **Enrollment** offers **Enroll a host** (`create_enrollment_token`, the token, its expiry and the `oxagen agent enroll` command shown once) and a per-row **Revoke** confirmed by hostname with an optional reason (`revoke_tacho_enrollment`); **Toolbelt** renders each entry's input schema, its origin (`declared` from the contract, `imported` from the registry version) and its SHA-256 digest over the canonical schema, which is not the manifest checksum `list_tool_versions` publishes under the same field name, with the schema travelling as its digest alone past `BELT_SCHEMA_BYTE_LIMIT` (16 KiB) and `NotRecorded` for an MCP server whose tools were never imported; **Budgets** is a seventh tab, read-only over `get_spend_budget`, showing the organization and workspace ceilings this agent runs under with the basis of each money figure and one `NotBacked` line saying no per-agent ceiling exists, because the contract's scope is `org` or `workspace` and nothing narrower. Agent-scope budgets wait on an ADR about the scopes `billing.budgets` accepts | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}` | **Mandate** | title `pages.mandate`; owner; a granted mandate's public id | lane #2957 (`pMandate`, ADR-059): one mandate's delegated authority. **Header** — eyebrow, the id in the mono face, the status as a dot and a word, and the two governed writes, **Change limits** (the one gold action) and **Revoke**, each behind a dialog and each gated in its handler by `assertConsequenceRole`. **Four tiles** — Per call, Per period, Settled, Remaining — one figure and one basis line each, every measure listed through `MandateAuthorityList`; the figures are the ledger's own accounting (INV-10) and no search narrows them. **The ledger** — When · Call · Amount · State · External id · Receipt, with a search, a facet on the movement state and a pager, all over the rows `get_mandate` returned, because that contract takes a bound and no cursor or filter. **The grant** and **Reconciliation**. Flat rather than nested under the agent: the mandate id names the record on its own and `get_mandate` takes it alone, so the agent comes from the record and the header links to it. Two columns state what is not recorded rather than filling in — *Call* names the measure the movement drew, because the ledger records the call by a raw uuid nothing resolves to a tool version, and *Receipt* is empty and Reconciliation declines to claim the ledger balances, because receipt frames have no read (G8). The route carries no sidebar item of its own: `features/shell/nav.ts` lights **Agents** for it, the same way a run with no Runs item lights Fleet. No `e2e/routes.ts` row yet; the 2026-09-19 known issue says why | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}` | **Register an agent** | title `pages.register`; owner; `{step}` = `name` | lane #2967 (ADR-065 decision 1): the gate returns as a rail over the real pages, and the register flow is this route's three steps. **name** (`register_agent`: slug, display name, harness, description → the identity, its delegated principal and one long-lived credential shown once) → **wrap** (the path the recorded harness takes: `create_enrollment_token` mints the single-use token for a hook-based harness, shown once with its `oxagen agent enroll --token …` command; an SDK agent is wrapped in its own process with the credential registration issued) → **run** (`get_first_frame`, long-polled with the `waitMs` budget `get_run` takes; the page re-reads only while a host is enrolled, and **Bind &lt;repo&gt;** over `bind_main_repository` closes the provisional window). `advance_onboarding` moves the gate between its wrap and run steps when this workspace carries it; `unlocked` is the ingest's alone, so no step completes on a timer. The identity is carried as `?agent=`, so the flow has three addresses and no catch-all. **The lane adds exactly one route** — +1 `page.tsx` file and +1 `e2e/routes.ts` entry, on the WL-33 baseline of 19; with every lane landed the total is 26 (§1.2 route-count paragraph). The mockup's three wrap tabs (Claude Code, Codex CLI, SDK agent) are not drawn: `register_agent`'s harness enum carries no `codex` member and fixes the harness at registration, so the step shows the one path the recorded harness takes rather than three tabs the contract cannot switch between (§3.6) | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/tools` | Tools | title `pages.tools` | lane #2958: **Registry** (`list_tool_versions`, `import_tools`, `set_tool_classification`): the active tool versions with their safety classification, the kill switch that stops each one today, the schema’s origin and digest and its 30-day calls, the consequence-tag chips the contract filters on, the labels/API-names toggle, the tool dialog and its reclassification form, and Import tools from an MCP server, which picks its server from the roster · **Tool servers** (`list_mcp_servers`, `register_mcp_server`), under the registry: every registered server with its transport, endpoint, recorded health and pin count, and Register a server · **Connections** (`list_connections`, `get_connection`, `create_connection`, `list_credential_grants`): the workspace’s connections with their connector, auth, health and status, a drawer over one of them, Add a connection, and beneath them the credential grants log — grant, tool server, run, connection, scope, TTL, state. Owner, Reviewed and Next review render the not-recorded state: `list_connections` carries no field for any of them · **Kill switches** (`list_kill_switches`, `set_kill_switch`): every level, the deny generation, a card per recorded switch, and the switch dialog that states the blast radius before confirming · lane #2957: **Mandates ledger** (`list_mandates`, `grant_mandate`): every mandate the workspace has recorded — granted, requested, expired and revoked — with its grant, the tool patterns it covers, its per-call and per-period limits named with the window each is counted over, and what the ledger records as settled, reserved and remaining, by measure — the tab the accountable office reads. Each row's mandate id links to `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}`, the same link the Agents tab's mandates table carries. A tab, a consequence tag, the names toggle and a cursor are query values, so the two lanes add **+0** routes between them. **Not drawn, because no capability carries them:** the mockup’s ten-category “what it acts on” taxonomy (derived there from a hard-coded name table) and the On belts column; the grants log’s Tool version and Agent columns; a blast radius counted in tool versions, agents and runs in flight; a display name for the operator, workspace or org a switch names and for whoever flipped it. **Not built, because no store exists:** Policy versions (G2) and the observed-schema proposals banner and its approve dialog | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/skills[/<tab>]` | Skills | title `pages.skills`; owner | lane #3098 (ADR-090, decision log 2026-09-18), which reverses the 2026-09-15 narrowing this row previously carried. Five tabs on one catch-all route: **Catalog** (in scope / held back / if-all-loaded / actually-loaded tiles, and each in-scope skill as `id@version` with source, kind, tokens, digest prefix, citations, proof rate and a decision badge — held-back rows carry why, and a held skill is not a low-ranked result); **Search**, the console answered by `preview_skill_search` — the human capability, not the agent's `search_skills` — against the config version a run pinned, showing hits and each withholding **by name and reason**, which is the projection a person gets and the agent never does. It writes no run frame: `skills.searched` belongs to an agent's resolution inside a run, and this tab resolves outside one; **In the loop**, the interjection seat with what it may and may not do; **Reflection**, quarantined and research-only; **Versions**, the rendered `.oxagen/skills.toml` and its git history, each version a pull request. The observed-inventory read (`list_skills` over `tacho.sessions.skills_available`) keeps its own section beside the resolved catalog — it answers what the harness actually had, which resolution does not. Dialogs `skenable`, `skcfg`, `skill`, `skadd` and the creation wizard. Nav count of skills waiting on a person. When `skills.enabled` is false the route renders the gate, not an empty page. Loaded, empty, loading, error (**Try again**), denied and the shared `pending_approval`. **The lane adds one route** (a `[[...tab]]` catch-all, so tabs cost no extra `page.tsx`), with its title key (+1); its `REV1_ROUTES` entry and `page-load` step land with `e2e/routes.ts` (WL-47). Depends on #3241: the config is read through the repository binding | — |
| `/{org}/{ws}/steering` | Steering | title `pages.steering` | lane #2961: published records → proposals → Context PR state machine; no effect metrics, retirement candidates or promotion thresholds | `steering` until the lane lands |
| `/{org}/{ws}/spend` | Spend | title `pages.spend` | lane #2962 (app half landed): the cost rollup for the month to date by operator, by agent with the models beside it, and by tool (`get_spend`), wasted spend by cause (`list_waste`), budgets (`get_spend_budget`) and one operator's, agent's or tool's drill (`get_spend_drill`), plus Fleet's Spend today and Cache hit rate tiles (Fleet row); the tab and the drill are query values on this route, so the lane adds no route. Beside the tabs on every view: **Set a budget** (`set_spend_budget`: the organization's ceiling or this workspace's, monthly or rolling, enforced or not; the handler admits an org Owner, Admin or Billing member, or a workspace Owner or Admin for that workspace's own, INV-29) and **Export report** (`export_statement`: one month's statement as CSV). The mockup's agent and operator budget scopes, per-run and daily periods and soft mode have no contract field and are not offered; the signed PDF and emailed delivery wait on the audit-exports lane (ADR-060 §6). The drill's **Export this view** is not built: `export_statement` answers a whole workspace's month and takes no key, so the per-key report (spec §12.9 operator and agent views) waits on a contract that takes one, and #2962 stays open for it. Lane #2963 (app half landed) adds the **Findings** tab, which the page opens on: `list_findings` over the open findings with the savings identified, their share of the window's priced spend and that saving annualised, a composition strip (`role="img"` with its legend, the tail past eight rolled into one entry) and a card per finding carrying its kind, level, subject, why, fix and what it cites; **Evidence** opens `get_finding_evidence` on `?tab=findings&finding=fnd_…` with the cited runs, the calls the counterfactual covers and what each side cost; **Fix** records the change through `record_finding_fix` or closes the finding through `dismiss_finding`, both `kernelWrite` and both org Owner or Admin in the handler (INV-29). Each finding's share of the listed total is computed through `ratioOfMicros` in `data/contracts/money.ts`, and the legend's tail entry sums those shares; every other figure is the findings job's, printed as it arrived, with `null` printed as not recorded (INV-09, INV-10). The findings cross-links the mockup draws on the drill, Run › Cost and the agent budgets tab are not built: `list_findings` takes no subject, so a per-key list waits on a contract that does. No reconciliation tab | — |
| `/{org}` | **Organization › People** | title `pages.people` | members and pending invitations (`list_members {scope:"org"}`); change role; remove. **The #2964 lane** has added the two kept sections the mockup's `pOrganization` draws: **Roles** (the role and permission catalogue) at `/{org}/roles`, the route WL-33 deleted and the lane re-added with its `page.tsx` and title key (+1 on the WL-33 baseline of 19, composed with the other lanes' routes in the route-count paragraph below this table; its `REV1_ROUTES` entry and `page-load` step land with `e2e/routes.ts` in WL-47, which has not landed), and **Workspaces** (list, create, edit, archive) as a section of `/{org}`, adding no route. Invitations stay a section of this page, not a tab. The lane's model-funding, data-plane and SSO/SCIM panels are cut (2026-09-14), whatever #2964's title says. **The one real rev1 hole is closed:** `create_workspace` had no surface between WL-39 and this lane, so a second workspace was made through the API, MCP or CLI while `create_org` made the first; the Workspaces section binds `create_workspace`, `update_workspace_settings` and `archive_workspace` from `/{org}`. **Create workspace:** the create-workspace form on this page is `create_workspace` (WL-62) with the org-only REST mount fixed (#3029), maintainer decision 13 of 2026-09-15, which replaces the 2026-09-14 rule that rev1 had no in-app way to make a second workspace; `create_org` makes the first one. **Invite** (`send_workspace_invite`) sits in the Pending invitations section header behind the same Owner or Admin gate, and asks for an email, the organization role the invitation offers, and an optional note. It asks for no workspace, because the handler records an organization invitation with no workspace column, and the copy claims none. A repeat for an address already pending reads back the existing invitation, shown as already invited rather than as a failure |
| `/{org}/roles` | **Organization › Roles** | title `pages.roles`; owner | the role and permission catalogue (`list_iam_roles`, one read): every role with the catalogue permissions its grants cover, who holds it, its scope and whether it is built in; the catalogue itself, grouped; **Create role**, **Edit** and **Delete** on a custom role (`create_role`, `set_role_grants`, `delete_role`), offered to an Owner or Admin and named for everyone else. The read carries `enforcement: { tier, enforced }` and the page prints it either way: Oxagen checks these grants on governed calls, or they are recorded, not enforced. No control is gated on a tier (ADR-069) | — |
| `/{org}/api-keys` | **Organization › API keys** | title `pages.apiKeys` | the workspace picker (`list_workspaces`) and that workspace's keys (`list_api_keys`); create, secret shown once; rotate (`rotate_api_key`, the replacement secret shown once; WL-63, maintainer decision of 2026-09-15); revoke. A key names a workspace, so the page does too: `?workspace=` is a query value on this route, not a route of its own, and the writes run in the workspace it names (ADR-073). `list_api_keys` answers the workspace's whole roster with no filter and no page of its own, so the page cuts it: it opens on the keys that have not been revoked (an expired key is not revoked, and stays), hides revoked rows behind `?show=all`, and shows 20 rows at a time on `?offset=`. Both are query values on the same route (§1.2) | — |
| `/{org}/model-funding` | **Organization › Model funding** | title `pages.modelFunding` | whose key pays for the in-app assistant's model calls (`get_model_credential`); test a key (`verify_model_credential`: is the key accepted, and for an OpenAI-compatible server can the balanced model call tools); save it (`set_model_credential`); remove it after an in-page confirm (`delete_model_credential`). Five vendors: OpenRouter and the Vercel AI Gateway take a key alone; OpenAI and Anthropic also take the balanced model; any other OpenAI-compatible server also takes its URL. Org-scoped, not workspace-scoped: the key pays for every workspace's turns. Owner or Admin, asserted in all four handlers (INV-29). The fourth Organization tab; no sidebar item. The monthly assistant spend cap is not on this page: no contract reads or writes it (decision log 2026-09-18) | — |
| `/{org}/billing` (+ `?checkout=success\|credits\|plan\|cancel`) | **Billing** | title `pages.billing` | Laid out as `pages/billing.md` in the Mockups repo draws it (amended 2026-09-18): eyebrow Organization, h1 Billing and one gold action, **Change plan** (`start_subscription_upgrade` through Stripe Checkout, WL-66; Build or Scale, Owner or Billing). Four tiles: **Plan** (`get_subscription`), **GAU this month** (`get_gau_bucket`), **Contracted rate** (`get_contract_rate`), **Due** (open rows of `list_invoices`). Left column: **This month** (the newest invoice page rolled up by kind within the bucket month), **Meters** (GAU used against the bucket, held runs and other governed actions as not recorded, the usage credit balance, then the billing mode), **Invoices** (`list_invoices`, with a kind per row). Right column: **The price list** (the published terms on the `start_subscription_upgrade` and `purchase_credits` contracts), **Auto top-up** (`set_auto_topup`), **Buy governed action units** (`purchase_gau_bucket`), **Usage credits** (`purchase_credits`), **What counts** | — (tokens and retention render nothing, §1.4) |
| `/{org}/audit` | **Audit** | title `pages.audit`; owner | **Events** (`query_audit_log`): the organization's control-plane events, newest first — When · Event · Actor · What (the capability) · Result (a dot and a word) — each row opening to the workspace, the IP address, the request id and the user agent, with the actor named from the `list_members {scope:"org"}` the Organization page already reads and printed as its public id when the roster no longer holds it. The filters (event type from `EMITTED_SECURITY_EVENT_TYPES`, result, actor, capability, from, to) and the page offset are query values, so the page adds no tab segment and no second route · **Export** (`export_audit_events`): the same filters as a CSV or NDJSON file signed HMAC-SHA256, downloaded from `/{org}/audit/export` — a `route.ts` with no `page.tsx`, so it adds no `REV1_ROUTES` entry. Ported from the deprecated `/{orgSlug}/security/audit` viewer (decision log, 2026-09-15; #3097): **+1** route. **Not drawn, because nothing records them:** the mockup's four summary tiles, the Severity and Reference columns, and actor kind. **Not built, because no store exists:** receipts, evidence bundles and the verifier, outbound events, keys and KEK rotation, retention, and the Incidents tab — `list_incidents` filters on `ctx.workspaceId`, so an org-scope caller carrying the org-only sentinel would read nothing, and an org-wide incident register is new backend | — |

Tabs are URL segments only where a page has two or more real tabs: Organization (`/{org}`, `/{org}/roles`, `/{org}/api-keys`). Exactly one `[[...tab]]` catch-all exists in rev1: `/{org}/{ws}/skills/[[...tab]]`, which ADR-090's five tabs need and which costs no extra `page.tsx`, so the route count and its `REV1_ROUTES` entry stay +1. Every other page with tabs uses URL segments as above; a second catch-all is a change to this invariant, not a precedent. After WL-33 `src/app` holds exactly 19 `page.tsx` files (6 sign-in, invite, new-organization, cli/authorize, `/`, 6 workspace pages, 3 org pages), and `e2e/routes.ts` has exactly 19 entries (INV-20). A gap lane that adds a route adds its `page.tsx`, its `REV1_ROUTES` entry, its title key and its `page-load` step in the same PR, and the count it moves the baseline by is stated in its §1.2 row before the lane starts: #2956 +2 (agent detail, agent definition source), #2957 +1 (mandate detail), #2964 +1 (`/{org}/roles`), #2967 +1 (`/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}`, ADR-065 decision 1), #3097 +1 (`/{org}/audit`), #3098 +1 (`/{org}/{ws}/skills`). No other kept lane adds a route — #2952, #2953, #2955, #2958, #2961, #2962, #2963, #2968 and #2970 all build sections, tabs or shell on routes that already exist, and a witness run renders its own tab set on `/runs/{run}` rather than a route of its own. Those six lanes add seven routes between them, so 19 → 26 `page.tsx` files and `e2e/routes.ts` entries when every lane has landed. Six of the seven have landed — #2956's two, #3098's one, #2967's register step, #3097's audit page and #2964's `/{org}/roles` — taking 19 to 25, with 10 workspace pages and 5 org pages, and #2957's mandate detail, the last and not yet built, takes it to 26. **#3091 adds one more**, outside the lane list: `/cli/complete`, the page the WL-52 cutover dropped from the rebuilt app while the CLI kept redirecting to it (25 → 26 today, 26 → 27 with every lane landed). It is the eighth route and the first anonymous one in the oracle. `e2e/routes.ts` does not exist until WL-47 (`baseline.json` carries it as a `missing-entry`), so a landed route's `REV1_ROUTES` entry and `page-load` step land with that file rather than in the lane's own PR, and WL-47 writes one entry per `page.tsx` it finds.

**Routes deleted** (at WL-07; `/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}` returns with the #2967 lane as three addressed steps, ADR-065 decision 1, and `/welcome/*` stays deleted: the gate is a rail over the real pages)**:** `/welcome/[[...step]]`, `/{org}/{ws}/register/[[...step]]`, `/{org}/{ws}/agents/[agent]/**`, `/{org}/{ws}/spend/[[...drill]]`, `/{org}/{ws}/ontology/**` and `/{org}/audit/**` outright (no rev1 page; not converted to `NotRecorded`), the `tools`/`steering` `[[...tab]]` catch-alls, `/{org}/roles`. Reasons: G15 is not backed (`backing.ts:222-228`); the onboarding panels render only from fixture data (`features/onboarding/screens.tsx:151-158,176-177,215`); none has a rev1 section.

### 1.3 Governance write in place of a kill switch

`set_kill_switch` is new in Appendix E and no contract writes `iam.emergency_denies`. The governance write in rev1 is **Halt a wrapped run** through `dispatch_tacho_command` with `command: "kill"` (`packages/oxagen/src/contracts/tacho.command.dispatch.ts:15-40`, roles Owner and Admin, `noBillingGate: true` at `:24`). Ledger runs render Halt disabled with the `run.halt.ledgerReason` catalog copy (G9). Because the contract is `noBillingGate`, the billing gate never fires for it and the Halt dialog has no `exhausted` state (§1.5).

Spec Appendix E renames this contract `dispatch_command` under ADR-025 (verb-first, the scope as an argument). This document binds the registered name as it exists today, `dispatch_tacho_command`, in four places — this section, the §1.2 Run row, the §1.5 table and INV-28's contract list. The rename is not a rev1 change: WL-50 carries it as a residue item (§8) so the contract, the handler, the API route, the MCP tool, the doc and these four bindings move in one PR, or not at all. Until WL-50 lands, `dispatch_tacho_command` is the name, and a lane that writes `dispatch_command` will not resolve in the registry.

**Ledger runs (maintainer decision 11, 2026-09-15).** The ledger ingest contract with a revocable run token (WL-61; ADR-056, 2026-09-15 amendment) gives a ledger run a connection point. `cancel` revokes the run's token in the same transaction as the command row, so Halt and Cancel reach a ledger-ingested run, and `run.halt.ledgerReason` remains only for the kinds still refused (`steer`, `pause`, `resume`).

### 1.4 Billing: what the page shows, in the customer's units

The page answers five questions with no dollar amount except where money is the answer:

| Section | Reads | Shows | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plan | `get_subscription` | tier, status, billing interval, current period; an Upgrade action to Build or Scale for Owner and Billing (`start_subscription_upgrade` → Stripe Checkout, WL-66, maintainer decision 7 of 2026-09-15); for an org with no subscription row (`subscription: null`, `billing.subscription.read.ts:28-40` — the e2e org) the card prints the catalog line "No subscription" and nothing else, and the tier the org is on is printed by the rate block from `get_contract_rate.tier` | text, dates |
| Billing mode | `get_gau_bucket.mode`, `.invoice` | read-only: "Prepaid — governed actions stop when the bucket is empty unless auto top-up refills it" (for a Free org with no saved payment method, the line continues "— add a payment method to keep governing past the allowance, or wait for the next month") or "Invoice billing — usage is never capped; overage is invoiced at period end, or once N governed action units accrue" with `invoice.gauMax`, `invoice.uninvoicedGau`, `invoice.invoicedThisPeriodGau`; a "payment past due" line while `invoice.pastDue` — an interim or period-close invoice is `open` (§3.9 item 7); a suspension line with the outstanding invoices once the org is suspended 5 days after a GAU invoice is past due (WL-60, maintainer decision 12 of 2026-09-15) | text, **GAU counts** via `formatCount` |
| Governed action bucket | `get_gau_bucket` | included, purchased, carried forward, used, remaining (printed as stored: a negative remaining renders "overdrawn by N", §3.9 item 5); period start/end; the exhausted line when `remainingGau ≤ 0` in prepaid mode and `autoTopup.paymentMethod` is null: "Add a payment method to keep governing this month, or your allowance renews on <period end>", linking to the purchase form below (the Checkout it opens saves the card, §3.9 item 6) — the Free-tier rule of 2026-09-14 (spec §4.2, ADR-055 §6) | **GAU counts** via `formatCount` (INV-09); the renewal date via the date formatter |
| Auto top-up (prepaid only) | `get_gau_bucket.autoTopup` | enabled toggle and blocks stepper (Owner/Admin write `set_auto_topup`; others see it read-only); "= N GAU per top-up"; the saved card as "charged to <brand> ····<last4>" or "no saved payment method — your next purchase saves one, and auto top-up runs from then on"; the last attempt and its outcome (`paid`; `open`, with the hosted invoice link from Invoices; `failed`) | GAU and block counts, no money |
| Your contracted rate | `get_contract_rate` | per-GAU rate, per-block price, block size, currency, **included GAUs per month**, effective dates, source: "Published <Tier> rate" or "Negotiated agreement <ref>" | `<Money precision="exact">` for the rate; `<Money>` for the block price |
| Buy governed action units (prepaid only) | `purchase_gau_bucket` | a quantity picker in GAUs stepping by the block size; the total = blocks × block price at the contracted rate, computed with `mulMicros` (§3.9); a Checkout button. Rendered for every prepaid org including a Free org with no saved card: the Checkout collects the card with `setup_future_usage: "off_session"` and is the rev1 card-saving path (§3.9 item 6) | GAU count; one `<Money>` for the total |
| Invoices | `list_invoices` | number, kind (subscription, block purchase, auto top-up, interim, period close), status, period, amount due, amount paid, Stripe-hosted link; cursor paging | `<Money>` per row |
| In-app AI usage (WL-67) | `get_subscription.creditBalanceCents`; `purchase_credits` | the usage credit balance that pays for the in-app agent's model calls, with the line "1 credit = $0.01, metered at provider cost times the published markup"; at a balance ≤ 0, the line that the in-app agent's platform-paid turns stop until a top-up lands; **Top up**: a face-value amount in dollars (minimum $5, presets at the `CREDIT_PACKS` prices) → `purchase_credits` → Stripe Checkout, an Owner/Billing write, read-only for everyone else; rendered in both billing modes (§3.9, the second meter) | credits via `formatCount`; the balance's face value and the top-up amount via `<Money>` |

Tokens and retention are not on the page. `get_subscription.periodUsage` is null for an org without an active subscription (`billing.subscription.read.ts:77-78`) and would otherwise print counts with "charged: 0 GAU", a line with no billing consequence; `get_evidence_retention` returns `storedGbBeyondIncluded: null, storedGbMeasured: false` unconditionally (`billing.evidence_retention.ts:118-125`, "No accounting job measures per-organisation evidence volume yet"), so a retention meter prints "not measured" for every organization. Both are the placeholder §3.6 forbids; neither has a port method, a view model, a mapper or a component on the billing page. `get_evidence_retention` stays a contract for its API and MCP callers; the audit adapter's `retention` port reads it today (`src/data/adapters/live/audit.ts:349-367`, mapper `toRetentionTiers` at `mappers/audit.ts:336-354`, carrying `effectiveRetentionDays` only), WL-08 deletes that adapter with the live data layer, and no rev1 port reads it after.

The rate block is labelled "Your contracted rate" with its source and effective dates. A negotiated customer sees the figure from their `contract_terms` row; a self-serve customer sees the published figures of the plan their subscription entitles them to (or the Free plan), read live. The block size is printed once, here, and carried on the rate block as `data-block-size`; the purchase form and the pay e2e read it from there.
### 1.5 Which rev1 invokes are governed actions

The kernel bills every top-level, scoped, non-`noBillingGate` invoke that carries an `orgId`: the admission gate runs at `packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:1313-1316` and the recorder at `:1543-1549` → `packages/billing/src/bootstrap.ts:39-52`. ADR-052 exclusion 2 says reading your own spend, settings or membership is never a charge. Rev1 therefore declares, and INV-28 tests:

| Rev1 contract | `noBillingGate` | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `list_runs`, `get_run`, `list_approvals`, `list_members`, `list_api_keys`, `list_orgs`, `list_workspaces` | `true` (declared in WL-17…20 and WL-19 for the two unscoped lists) | console reads: a page load or an SSE poll of `get_run` is not a governed action; without the flag the Run stream would turn a timer into GAU spend and a `hard_stop` would lock the customer out of Fleet, Run and Organization |
| `get_subscription`, `get_gau_bucket`, `get_contract_rate`, `list_invoices`, `purchase_gau_bucket` | `true` | reading your bill and buying more are never refused for lack of GAUs (INV-27) |
| `create_api_key`, `rotate_api_key`, `revoke_api_key` (`api.key.create.ts:27`, `api.key.rotate.ts:27`, `api.key.revoke.ts:24`), `dispatch_tacho_command` (`:24`; the registered name — spec Appendix E's `dispatch_command` rename lands with WL-50, §1.3), `authorize_cli` (WL-21) | `true` today or at creation | settings and credentials |
| `change_member_role`, `remove_org_member`, `accept_member_invite`, `decline_member_invite` | gain `true` in WL-22 (today none declares it: `org.member.remove.ts:20-22`, `org.member_role.change.ts:18-20`, `org.member_invite.accept.ts:12-14`, `org.member_invite.decline.ts:11-13`) | membership, per ADR-052 exclusion 2 |
| `get_spend`, `get_spend_drill`, `list_waste`, `get_spend_budget`, `export_statement`, `set_spend_budget` (the #2962 lane) | `true` | reading your own spend and capping it, per ADR-052 exclusion 2; a ceiling write is never refused for being over the ceiling |
| `query_audit_log`, `export_audit_events` (the #3097 lane) | `true` | reading your own record is never a charge, and an audit trail that goes dark when the GAU balance does is not an audit trail; `query_audit_log` gains the flag in this lane, `export_audit_events` declares it at creation |
| `list_skills`, `get_skill_config`, `list_skill_resolutions`, `list_skill_interjections`, `preview_skill_search`, `read_skill_reflection`, `set_skills_enabled`, `update_skill_config`, `approve_skill_digest`, `propose_skill`, `set_reflection_capture` (the #3098 lane, ADR-090) | `true` | reading which skills your own sessions reported and which your config admits, and **turning skills off**, per ADR-052 exclusion 2. A control an organization cannot switch off once its governed action units run out is not a control |
| `propose_agent` (New agent, roadmap creation-spec §1) | `true` | writing an agent's definition is a pull request, not a governed action; like `commit_agent_definition` and `propose_skill`, it writes no row and a run never calls it |
| `search_skills` (the #3098 lane, ADR-090) | **not set: metered** | the agent's resolution tool, consumed by a run and charged to it. The only Skills capability that meters, and the reason the exemption above is a list rather than a family |
| `create_org` | `scoped: false` (`org.create.ts:38`), so the gate skips it and the recorder sees no `orgId` | pre-tenant |
| **`resolve_approval`** (`agent.approval.resolve.ts:5-16`, no flag) | **not set: this is the governed action** | the human decision on a tool call is what ADR-052 bills. For a prepaid org whose bucket is at `remaining ≤ 0` after the auto top-up could not run (disabled, no saved payment method, or a declined charge, §3.9 item 8c) the gate refuses it with `gau_exhausted` — for a Free org with no default payment method the error carries `reason: "free_no_payment_method"` and the dialog prints "Add a payment method to keep governing this month, or your allowance renews on <date>" (§3.9 item 9); for an org in dunning suspension with `billing_suspended`; an invoice-billed org is never refused for lack of GAUs, and is refused `billing_suspended` once suspended 5 days after a GAU invoice is past due (WL-60, maintainer decision 12 of 2026-09-15). An unknown, expired or already-resolved id is `HandlerError { code: "conflict", reason: "approval_expired" }` from the handler, so it is never billed (§3.9 item 15). The approval dialog renders `exhausted` with the copy for its code and a link to `/{org}/billing` (WL-40) |

That list is the whole rev1 metering surface. It is recorded in ADR-055, and the maintainer confirmed it on 2026-09-15: `resolve_approval` is the only billable action and membership writes are free (§9). The recorder that fires for `resolve_approval` is where the GAU debit, the auto top-up and the invoice threshold live (§3.9 item 8).

Metering and roles are separate gates. The billing gate above runs for every org; the IAM role check does not: `checkIAM` returns `allow` for every capability when `canAccessACL(tier)` is false, and `canAccessACL` is `tier === "enterprise"` (`packages/iam/src/check-iam.ts:157-182`, `packages/billing/src/entitlements.ts:93-95`). Every Free, Build and Scale org, the e2e org included, bypasses the resolver, so `defaultRoles`/`defaultEffect: "deny"` on a contract is not enforced for them. Rev1 therefore checks roles in handlers (§3.2, INV-29). Maintainer decision 3 of 2026-09-15 removes the tier gate: WL-55 makes the resolver run for every tier, and the handler checks stay.

---

## 2. Layers and dependency direction

```text
                      src/proxy.ts ──────────────► src/shared
                   (edge: cookie gate + App. F redirect table)
  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ src/app/**            routes: await params → requireViewer → feature  │
  └───────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                  ▼
  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ src/features/<page>/  server components · client islands · "use server"│
  └──────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬────────────┘
         ▼                     ▼                             ▼
  ┌────────────┐   ┌───────────────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────────┐
  │ src/ui     │   │ src/data/{read,contracts,     │   │ src/server/viewer        │
  │ (present.) │──►│   ports,source,unrecorded}    │   │ src/server/kernel (write │
  └────────────┘   └───────────────┬───────────────┘   │   only from "use server")│
                                   │ source.ts only    │ src/server/sse           │
                                   ▼                   └────────────┬─────────────┘
                   ┌───────────────────────────────┐                │
                   │ src/data/live/  port impls:   │────────────────┤
                   │ kernelRead + typed mapper     │                ▼
                   └───────────────────────────────┘   ┌──────────────────────────┐
                                                       │ src/server/kernel ──► @oxagen/oxagen invoke,
                                                       │                       @oxagen/tenancy, */register
                                                       │ src/server/session ─► @oxagen/auth
                                                       │ src/server/tenancy-lookups ─► @oxagen/database
                                                       └──────────────────────────┘
   src/shared: pure, imports only src/shared (safe-path, loopback-uri, checkout-url, slug rules, navigation).
```

| Layer | One-line rule | May import (internal) |
|---|---|---|
| `src/app/**` | A route resolves its viewer, picks a feature and does nothing else. `route.ts` is a one-line delegation. | `@/features/<page>` (barrel only), `@/server/viewer`, `@/data/source`, `@/ui/*`, `@/shared/*` |
| `src/features/<page>/**` | Owns a page's sections, client islands and server actions. Never touches a store or `invoke`. | own folder, `@/ui/*`, `@/data/{read,contracts,ports,unrecorded}`, `@/server/viewer`, `@/server/session`, `@/server/kernel` (`kernelWrite`, `kernelRead` and `readToActionResult`, only from a `"use server"` module: an on-demand read — the Workspace settings dialog in the shell chrome, whose record is a live GitHub call no layout should pay for — resolves its own viewer and reads there, exactly as a write does; ADR-089. `readToActionResult` is the pure mapper that carries such a read's refusal into INV-19's `ActionResult` shape; it reaches neither the kernel nor a store, and it is named here because it is part of the same seam surface, not because the module as a whole is importable), `@/server/sse` (stream handler only), `@/shared/*`; another page only through `@/features/<x>` |
| `src/ui/**` | Presentational: props in, markup out. | `@/ui/*`, `@/data/read` (types), `@/data/contracts/*` (types), `@/data/unrecorded`, `@/shared/*` |
| `src/data/read.ts`, `src/data/contracts/**`, `src/data/unrecorded.ts` | Pure vocabulary: `Read<T>`, `PageKey`, `PAGE_FAILURES`, zod view models, `Money`, `Cost` and `moneyFromMicros` (`mulMicros` lands with its first caller in WL-44), the unrecorded table. | `zod`, each other |
| `src/data/ports.ts` | The typed list of reads a page may make. Every method has a production caller. | `@/data/{read,contracts}`, `@/server/viewer` (ctx types) |
| `src/data/source.ts` | The one composition point: returns the live `DataSource`. | `./live` |
| `src/data/live/**` | One file per port. Each method is `kernelRead` plus a mapper typed from contract output. | `@/data/{read,contracts,ports}`, `@/server/kernel`, `@oxagen/oxagen/contracts/*` (declarations only) |
| `src/server/**` | Seams to platform packages. | `@/data/read`, `@/shared/*`, each other; never `@/features/*`, `@/data/live`, `@/data/source` |
| `src/shared/**` | Pure and edge-safe. | `@/shared/*` |
| `"use client"` files | Client islands. | never `@/server/*` (types excepted), `@/data/source`, `@/data/live/*`, or a feature barrel |

**Platform-package allowlist (INV-03 and INV-05, one table, one arch test).** Outside these modules, `src/` may import `@oxagen/*` only as `@oxagen/oxagen/contracts/*` (type and declaration imports) and `captureError` from `@oxagen/telemetry`.

| Module | May import |
|---|---|
| `src/server/kernel.ts` | `@oxagen/oxagen`, `@oxagen/oxagen/kernel`, `@oxagen/oxagen/registry`, `@oxagen/oxagen/types`, `@oxagen/handlers/register`, `@oxagen/agent/register` |
| `src/server/session.ts` | `@oxagen/auth`, `@oxagen/auth/*` |
| `src/features/auth/auth-client.ts` | `@oxagen/auth/client` (the browser Better Auth client; `session.ts` is server-only and a `"use client"` module cannot import it, INV-21) |
| `src/features/shell/session-client.ts` | `@oxagen/auth/client` (the shell's own browser seam: the user menu and Account dialog sign out, list sessions, revoke one and mint fresh recovery codes, and a feature's internals are not importable across lanes) |
| `src/features/shell/export-storage.ts` | `@oxagen/storage` (the shell's blob seam: a data export's archive is a private object, so the bytes are streamed by a route rather than linked. The `app` row admits `@/server/viewer` and no other `@/server/*`, and the `features` row admits the viewer, session and kernel seams alone, so a `src/server/blob.ts` would have to widen two layer rows to be reachable. The module exports one narrow read and no adapter) |
| `src/features/audit/filters.ts` | `@oxagen/compliance` (the emitted security event types the Audit filter offers, #2528, #3097: a pure leaf package with no store and no kernel) |
| `src/server/tenancy-lookups.ts` | `@oxagen/database`, `drizzle-orm` (never `sql`) |
| `instrumentation.ts` (app root) | `@oxagen/oxagen/kernel` (gate setters), the five `bootstrap*` package entries, and by binding `initTracer` and `recordSecurityEvent` from `@oxagen/telemetry`, `makeSecurityEventInserter` from `@oxagen/database/security` and `assertRlsConnectionSafe` from `@oxagen/database` (the process bootstrap; `apps/api/src/bootstrap.ts` and `apps/mcp/src/middleware.ts` wire the same four, #3048) |
| everything else under `src/` | `@oxagen/oxagen/contracts/*`; `captureError` from `@oxagen/telemetry` |

The subpath bypass the seam config warns about (`eslint.tenancy-seams.mjs:10-13`) is closed because the allowlist names specifiers, not import names: `@oxagen/oxagen/kernel` exports `invoke` (`packages/oxagen/package.json` `"./kernel"`, `src/index.ts:2-3`) and is refused outside `kernel.ts`. `@oxagen/tenancy` (the kernel enters the tenant scope itself, §3.2 step 4), `@oxagen/billing`, non-register `@oxagen/handlers/*`, `@oxagen/auth/cli-auth`, `@oxagen/run-ledger` and the `@oxagen/telemetry` barrel (which exports `chSelect`, `packages/telemetry/src/index.ts:1`, used at `live/audit.ts:597`) are refused by omission.

A single import-graph architecture test enforces the whole matrix: INV-03, INV-05, INV-07 and INV-21. It covers static and dynamic imports and resolves relative paths, closing the relative-path bypass (`features/onboarding/actions.ts:6`, `screens.tsx:11-12`; eslint matched only `@/features/*/*` at `eslint.config.mjs:47-51`) and the `data ↔ server` cycle (`data/scope.ts:4`, `server/stream-feeds.ts:14-15`). Arch tests enumerate files with `fs.readdir`/`tinyglobby`, never by shelling out to `fd` or `rg` (the CI image carries them, `.github/docker/ci/Dockerfile:29-30`, a developer machine may not; `tools/scripts/check_ui_parity.mjs:224` already falls back to a walk for that reason).

---

## 3. Seams

Twelve seams: ten in `apps/app`, each in one module, and two in `packages/billing` — the provider seam (`createGauCheckout`, `createGauInvoice`, `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice`, `deleteOrVoidDraftInvoice`, `getCheckoutPaymentMethod`) and the recorder seam (`recordGovernedAction`, `assertGauAvailable`, the bucket and settlement writers on a caller-supplied executor), §3.9.

### 3.1 Viewer: who is asking, in what tenant — `src/server/viewer.ts`

```ts
// src/server/viewer-mint.ts — importable only from viewer.ts and viewer.testing.ts (import graph, INV-02)
export const MINT: unique symbol = Symbol("mint");

// src/server/viewer.ts
/** The stored set: packages/database/src/schema/org.ts:96 and :170 CHECK lower(role) IN (…). */
export type OrgRole = "owner" | "admin" | "member" | "billing" | "compliance" | "viewer";

/** The brand is an ES private field, installed only by the constructor, checked at runtime by `is`. Fields are `declare`d so no class-field definition runs after `super()` on a frozen instance. */
export class OrgCtx {
  #brand!: true;
  declare readonly userId: string; declare readonly orgId: string;
  declare readonly orgSlug: string; declare readonly orgName: string; declare readonly orgRole: OrgRole;
  protected constructor(token: typeof MINT, f: OrgFields) { if (token !== MINT) throw new Error("invalid_ctx"); this.#brand = true; Object.assign(this, f); }
  static mint(token: typeof MINT, f: OrgFields): OrgCtx { const c = new OrgCtx(token, f); Object.freeze(c); return c; }
  static is(x: unknown): x is OrgCtx { return typeof x === "object" && x !== null && #brand in x; }
}
export class WsCtx extends OrgCtx {
  #wsBrand!: true;
  declare readonly workspaceId: string; declare readonly wsSlug: string; declare readonly wsName: string;
  private constructor(token: typeof MINT, f: WsFields) { super(token, f); this.#wsBrand = true; }
  static override mint(token: typeof MINT, f: WsFields): WsCtx { const c = new WsCtx(token, f); Object.freeze(c); return c; }
  static override is(x: unknown): x is WsCtx { return typeof x === "object" && x !== null && #wsBrand in x; }
}
export class PretenantCtx { #brand!: true; declare readonly userId: string; private constructor(…) {…} static mint(…); static is(x: unknown): x is PretenantCtx; }
/** A signed-in person holding an invitation. Not a member: no orgRole, no read access. */
export class InviteeCtx { #brand!: true; declare readonly userId: string; declare readonly orgId: string; declare readonly invitationId: string; private constructor(…) {…} static mint(…); static is(x: unknown): x is InviteeCtx; }

export function requireViewer(org: string): Promise<OrgCtx>;            // redirect /login · notFound · MFA · 308 canonical
export function requireViewer(org: string, ws: string): Promise<WsCtx>;
export function resolveViewer(org: string, ws?: string): Promise<ViewerResolution>; // route handlers: no interrupts
export function requireUser(next?: SafePath): Promise<PretenantCtx>;    // signed in, no org yet; signed out → /login?next=<next>
export function readInvitation(token: string): Promise<InvitationRecord | null>; // the page: no session, no interrupts; a malformed or unknown token → null (#3049)
export function requireInvitee(token: string): Promise<{ ctx: InviteeCtx; invitation: InvitationRecord }>; // the accept/decline actions: redirect /login · notFound
```

**The brand is a class with an ES private field.** Revision 2's `unique symbol` brand survived spread and `Object.assign`. Revision 3's `private readonly brand` member survived `Object.assign` too: `Object.assign<T, U, V>` returns `T & U & V`, so `Object.assign({}, real, { orgId: "victim" })` has type `{} & OrgCtx & { orgId: string }`, which is assignable to `OrgCtx` because the private member originates from the same declaration; `tsc` 6.0.3 reports the `@ts-expect-error` on that line as unused (TS2578), and the revision-3 `class WsCtx extends OrgCtx` with a private base constructor is TS2675. Both were reproduced with the repo's compiler, and the mechanism below was compiled with `typescript` 6.0.3 and with `@typescript/native-preview` 7.0.0-dev.20260707 (§9.0 R3-I1). The compiler refuses the forms it can detect and the runtime refuses the rest:

- **What the compiler refuses:** an object literal and a spread (`{ ...real, orgId }`) are not assignable to `OrgCtx` (a class with a `#private` declaration is nominal for those forms: TS2741 "Property '#brand' is missing"); a `PretenantCtx` or `InviteeCtx` is not an `OrgCtx` and an `OrgCtx` is not a `WsCtx` (distinct private declarations); `new OrgCtx(…)` outside the class (protected constructor on `OrgCtx` so `WsCtx` can extend it, private on the leaves); a subclass outside `viewer.ts` cannot call `super(token, …)` without a value of type `typeof MINT`, which only `viewer-mint.ts` exports and only `viewer.ts` and `viewer.testing.ts` may import (import graph). `viewer.type-test.ts` carries exactly these six `@ts-expect-error` cases — object literal, spread, `PretenantCtx → OrgCtx`, `OrgCtx → WsCtx`, `new OrgCtx`, a subclass passing `Symbol("mint")` — plus one positive (`WsCtx` is assignable to `OrgCtx`); it is compiled by `tsc --noEmit` in the app's typecheck, so an unused directive (TS2578) fails the build the way a missing one would. `Object.assign({}, real, { orgId })` is not in the file: no directive can hold on it, and the runtime check below is what refuses it.
- **What the runtime refuses:** `#brand` is installed only by the constructor, so `OrgCtx.is(x)` is false for `Object.assign({}, real, { orgId })`, for `{ ...real }`, for `structuredClone(real)` (private fields and the prototype are not cloned), for `Object.create(OrgCtx.prototype)` and for any plain object, however shaped. The shared kernel core (§3.2 step 0) calls `OrgCtx.is` / `PretenantCtx.is` / `InviteeCtx.is` before it builds the `CapabilityContext` and returns `error("invalid_ctx", 500)` otherwise, with one telemetry report: this is a programming error. `mint` freezes the instance after construction (`Object.freeze` returns `Readonly<T>`, which drops `#brand` from the type, so `mint` freezes and returns the same reference; freezing inside the base constructor would make a subclass's own field definitions throw, which is why every field is `declare`d), so `Object.assign(real, { orgId })` throws `TypeError` in strict mode. `viewer.test.ts` asserts each form, and `kernel.test.ts` asserts the core refuses each; both were run against the probe in §9.0.
- **Lint:** `no-restricted-syntax` bans `Object.assign(` and `structuredClone(` under `src/` outside `viewer.ts` and `viewer.testing.ts`, with a probe each; the cast lint (`consistent-type-assertions: never`) keeps the same two exemptions.

Minting: `viewer.ts` calls `OrgCtx.mint(MINT, fields)` from a module-private `mint()`; `viewer.testing.ts` imports the same token for its single `unsafeMint` export, which the import graph lets only `*.test.ts(x)` and `*.builders.ts` import. `Object.create(OrgCtx.prototype)` is not used anywhere: it would not install `#brand`.

**Why `InviteeCtx` is its own class.** Today's invitee viewer carries `orgRole: "invitee"` (`features/auth/invite-actions.ts:66-78`), a value no role union holds. `accept_member_invite` is `defaultEffect: "allow"` because the invitee has no principal at accept time (`packages/oxagen/src/contracts/org.member_invite.accept.ts:20-25`). An `OrgCtx` for a non-member would satisfy `kernelRead` and every `DataSource` method. `InviteeCtx` satisfies neither: `kernelWrite` accepts it only through an overload restricted to `accept_member_invite` and `decline_member_invite` (§3.2), and a `kernel.test.ts` negative shows `kernelRead(inviteeCtx, …)` fails to compile.

**The invitation page reads through this seam without a session.** `/invite/[token]` renders for a signed-out visitor, so `requireInvitee` (which redirects one to `/login`) cannot serve it. `readInvitation` checks the token's shape, calls `systemLookups.invitationByToken` and returns the record, whatever its status and expiry; `features/auth/invitations.ts` maps it to `InvitationView` (this row may not import `@/data/contracts`) and `decideInvitation` picks the page's state: not found, closed or expired, sign in, wrong account, accept. `requireInvitee` reads through `readInvitation` too. A feature never imports `@/server/tenancy-lookups` (§2; probe `features-imports-tenancy-lookups.ts`).

**Replaces:** the unbranded `Scope` (`server/tenant-scope.ts:6`, `data/scope.ts`), the plain `Viewer` (`server/viewer-resolution.ts:20-28`, `orgRole` typed `string` at `:24`), the hand-built invitee viewer, and `getSession()` calls inside 9 adapters (`org.ts:604`, `billing.ts:164`, `tools.ts:627`, `steering.ts:235`, `spend.ts:184`, `ontology.ts:148`, `runs.ts:529`, `agents.ts:374`, `audit.ts:562`).

**Stays:** the pure core `resolveViewerWith` (`server/viewer-resolution.ts:54-112`), with `orgRole` retyped to `OrgRole`.

### 3.2 Kernel: the only path to `invoke()` — `src/server/kernel.ts`

```ts
/** Structural contract type. Both zod lines carry `_input`/`_output` and `safeParse`; the app never mixes schema objects. */
export type ToolContract<I, O> = { readonly name: string;
  readonly input:  { readonly _input: I;  safeParse(v: unknown): SafeParse<unknown> };
  readonly output: { readonly _output: O; safeParse(v: unknown): SafeParse<O> } };
export type ReadContract<I, O>     = ToolContract<I, O> & { readonly mutates: false };   // registerCapability<C>(cap: C): C keeps the literal (packages/oxagen/src/registry.ts:48)
export type UnscopedContract<I, O> = ToolContract<I, O> & { readonly scoped: false };      // create_org (org.create.ts:38), list_orgs, list_workspaces
export type UnscopedReadContract<I, O> = ReadContract<I, O> & UnscopedContract<I, O>;
export type ContractOutput<C> = C extends { output: { _output: infer O } } ? O : never;

export function kernelRead<I, O>(ctx: OrgCtx | WsCtx,
  call: { contract: ReadContract<I, O>; input: NoInfer<I>; page: PageKey }): Promise<Read<O>>;
/** Pre-tenant reads: only contracts declared `scoped: false` (list_orgs, list_workspaces); the one caller is src/data/live/pretenant.ts (§3.3). */
export function kernelRead<I, O>(ctx: PretenantCtx,
  call: { contract: UnscopedReadContract<I, O>; input: NoInfer<I>; page: PageKey }): Promise<Read<O>>;

export type ActionResult<O> =
  | { ok: true; value: O }
  | { ok: false; reason: "denied" | "invalid" | "not_found" | "conflict" | "unavailable"; code: string; field?: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: "pending_approval"; accessRequestId: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: "exhausted"; code: "gau_exhausted" | "billing_suspended" | "budget_exceeded" };

export function kernelWrite<I, O>(ctx: OrgCtx | WsCtx, contract: ToolContract<I, O>, input: NoInfer<I>): Promise<ActionResult<O>>;
/** Pre-tenant writes: only contracts declared `scoped: false` — create_org. The compile-time twin of the runtime re-check in step 4. */
export function kernelWrite<I, O>(ctx: PretenantCtx, contract: UnscopedContract<I, O>, input: NoInfer<I>): Promise<ActionResult<O>>;
export function kernelWrite<I, O>(ctx: InviteeCtx, contract: InvitationContract<I, O>, input: NoInfer<I>): Promise<ActionResult<O>>; // accept/decline only
```

Both functions share one private core:

0. **Checks the ctx was minted by `viewer.ts`** with `OrgCtx.is` / `PretenantCtx.is` / `InviteeCtx.is` (§3.1). A value that fails — a plain object with the right keys, an `Object.assign({}, ctx, …)` copy, a `structuredClone` — returns `error("invalid_ctx", 500)` (`{ reason: "unavailable", code: "invalid_ctx" }` on the write side) before anything is built, with one telemetry report. `kernel.test.ts` asserts each form is refused.
1. **Loads both handler registries** (`@oxagen/handlers/register`, `@oxagen/agent/register`). Today `server/invoke.ts:87` loads only the first, while `tools.ts:615-620` and `agents.ts:364-369` load both (ARCH-02).
2. **Checks the capability is registered** with `getCapability`.
3. **Builds the `CapabilityContext`** with `ctx.surface: "app"` and **never passes `opts.surface`**: the kernel enforces a contract's `surfaces` allowlist only when `opts.surface` is given (`packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:993`), `CapabilitySurface` has no `"app"` member (`types.ts:86`), and today's app already passes only the context field (`server/invoke.ts:131`). This is why `authorize_cli` with `surfaces: []` is reachable from `kernelWrite` and why a later "tighten surfaces" change cannot 403 the app.
4. **Lets the kernel enter the tenant scope.** `invoke()` wraps the IAM check, the gates and the handler in one `runInTenantScope` built from `ctx.orgId`/`ctx.workspaceId` whenever the capability is scoped or both ids are uuids (`packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:1064-1081`, `withScope`); today's `server/invoke.ts:133-135` wraps a second, identical scope around it, and the design drops that. The core only fills the context: `orgId` from the ctx, `workspaceId` from a `WsCtx` or the org-only sentinel `ORG_ONLY_WS` for an `OrgCtx` (a uuid-shaped constant, `server/tenant-scope.ts:13` today; `packages/tenancy/src/scope.ts:99-101` asserts uuid shape), `userId`, `requestId`, and empty ids for a `PretenantCtx`. The sentinel is `ORG_ONLY_WORKSPACE_ID`, exported from `@oxagen/oxagen` beside `CapabilityContext`, and the two surface builders that need one use that single constant: this builder for an `OrgCtx`, and `apps/api`'s `capabilityContext` for a route mounted org-only, which passed `""` and so could not reach its handler (#3029, ADR-068). It is not declared in `@oxagen/tenancy`, which `src/` may not import (INV-03). A `PretenantCtx` may only reach a contract with `scoped: false` (`org.list.ts:38`, `workspace.list.ts:37`, `org.create.ts:38`); the overloads enforce it at compile time and the core re-checks at runtime. A `mutates: true` or undeclared contract is refused by `kernelRead` at compile time (`ReadContract`) and at runtime (`capabilityMutates`, `types.ts:344-348`). `@oxagen/tenancy` is not imported anywhere under `src/` (INV-03).
5. **Pre-parses a write's input with the contract's own schema** (`contract.input.safeParse`; both zod lines carry `error.issues[].path`). A failure returns `{ reason: "invalid", code: "invalid_input", field }` with `field` the first issue's path joined by `.`, and the kernel is not called. A kernel `invalid_input` after a successful pre-parse (a handler-level refusal such as `quantityGau % blockSizeGau !== 0`) is classified `invalid` with no `field`; the kernel's `CapabilityError` carries message text only (`kernel.ts:1038-1042`) and this document forbids classifying by it.
6. **Parses the output with the contract's own schema.** A mismatch becomes `error("contract_output_mismatch", 502)` plus one telemetry report.
7. **Classifies failures in `classifyKernelFailure(err)`**, by shape (`code` property), never `instanceof` (`server/errors.ts:150-156`) and never message text:

| Code | Source | Read | ActionResult |
|---|---|---|---|
| `authz_denied`, `capability_not_installed`, `no_handler` | `CapabilityError` (`packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:545-561`) | `denied(PAGE_FAILURES[page].permission)` | `{reason:"denied"}` |
| `pending_approval` (+ `accessRequestId`, `kernel.ts:569`) | `CapabilityError` | `{ok:false, reason:"pending_approval", accessRequestId}` | same |
| `invalid_input` | `CapabilityError` | `error("invalid_input", 400)` | `{reason:"invalid"}` |
| `invalid_output` (`kernel.ts:1497`) | `CapabilityError` | `error("contract_output_mismatch", 502)` | `{reason:"unavailable"}` |
| `gau_exhausted` (new, §3.9 item 9), `billing_suspended` (`metering.ts:120`, kept by `assertGauAvailable` for an org in dunning suspension), `budget_exceeded` (`spend-budget.ts:165`; duck-typed as deny at `kernel.ts:1434-1441`; 402 in `apps/api/src/middleware/error.ts:18-22`) | billing errors | — (no app read can produce any of them: every read the app binds is `noBillingGate`, and the kernel skips both gates on that flag, `kernel.ts:1313-1316,1328-1334`; a read that did would fall to the last row) | `{reason:"exhausted", code}` |
| `forbidden` / `not_found` / `conflict` | `HandlerError` (new, WL-22, in `@oxagen/oxagen` so `packages/agent` and `packages/handlers` both throw it) | `denied` / `error(404)` / `error(409)` | `denied` / `not_found` / `conflict` |
| anything else | — | `error(PAGE_FAILURES[page].error)` | `{reason:"unavailable"}` |

`surface_denied` is not a row: the app never passes `opts.surface` (step 3), so no app call can produce it. `insufficient_credits` is not a row either: after WL-25 the gate on app calls is `assertGauAvailable`, and the credit-balance gate survives only for the ADR-053 platform-funded assistant path (`turn-credit-gate.ts`), which no rev1 app path invokes. A code with no producer is dead code and gets no test.

The UI renders `exhausted` with the catalog copy for its code (`gau_exhausted`: the bucket is empty and auto top-up could not run; `billing_suspended`: the subscription is suspended for non-payment; `budget_exceeded`: a spend ceiling) and a link to `/{org}/billing`; the one rev1 producer is `resolve_approval` (§1.5), so the component test lives on the approval dialog (WL-40) and `Read<T>` has no `exhausted` variant (§3.3). `pending_approval` renders as "waiting for approval" with the request id, not as a refusal (the kernel documents it as pollable, `kernel.ts:549-554`).
