**Roles are checked in handlers.** The kernel's IAM check allows every capability for a non-enterprise org (§1.5), so a `denied` the UI shows a Member, Admin or Viewer must be produced by the handler: `assertOrgRole(ctx, { org: [...], workspace?: [...] })` in `packages/iam/src/org-role.ts` (the `resolveActorOrgRole` query moves there from `packages/handlers/src/lib/api-key-authz.ts:31-83`, so `packages/agent`, which depends on `@oxagen/iam` and not on `@oxagen/handlers`, can call it; `api-key-authz.ts` re-exports it, which makes `@oxagen/iam` a dependency of `packages/handlers` — `packages/handlers/package.json` does not declare it today and pnpm hoists nothing across workspace packages, so WL-21 adds `"@oxagen/iam": "workspace:*"` and runs `pnpm i --no-frozen-lockfile`; no cycle results, because `@oxagen/iam` depends on `billing`, `database`, `oxagen`, `run-evidence` and `telemetry` and none of those depends on `handlers`) throws `HandlerError { code: "forbidden" }`, which the table above maps to `denied`. Every rev1 contract with a role restriction carries the check (INV-29): `authorize_cli` (Owner, Admin — replacing today's `actorCanManageApiKeys` predicate at `cli-actions.ts:55-66` rather than dropping it), `purchase_gau_bucket` (Owner, Billing), `resolve_approval` (org Owner or Admin, or workspace Owner or Member — the contract's `defaultRoles`, `agent.approval.resolve.ts:16-19`; the handler has no check today), `set_auto_topup` (Owner, Admin), `list_api_keys` (Owner, Admin), `get_subscription`, `get_gau_bucket`, `get_contract_rate`, `list_invoices` (Owner, Admin, Billing; `billing.subscription.read.ts:30-36` checks only that a principal exists today). `create_api_key`, `revoke_api_key`, `change_member_role` and `remove_org_member` already carry one (`api.key.create.ts:20-21`, `org.member.remove.ts:75-95`). Handler tests run against a tier-`free` org and assert the code. The gate reads the acting user: the signed-in user, or for an API-key call (every MCP call) the key's creator, which the handler resolves with `resolveActingUserId` and passes as `userId`; the creator's current role bounds the key, and a key with no creator is refused `no_principal` (decision log, 2026-09-15). CLAUDE.md:301 keeps its warning in corrected form (WL-51): the kernel boots IAM in `apps/app` (INV-23), and IAM enforces roles only for enterprise orgs, so role gates live in handlers.

`PageKey` and `PAGE_FAILURES` move from `src/data/page-states.ts:45` into `src/data/read.ts`, which `src/server/**` may import.

**Replaces:** `server/invoke.ts` (`invokeTool`, `invokeFixture`, `setFixtureWriteAdapter`); the 9 inline invoke copies (`org.ts:557-588`, `billing.ts:124-151`, `spend.ts:187-210`, `steering.ts:240-262`, `ontology.ts:151-173`, `tools.ts:615-650`, `agents.ts:360-398`, `audit.ts:568-592`, `runs.ts:524-540`); 8 local denial predicates with 3 code sets; `toActionFailure` (`server/errors.ts:178-203`); the collapse of every write failure into `failed` (`invite-actions.ts:82-95`); 3 copies of `reportToTelemetry`; the uncommitted `server/invoke-read.ts` and `data/settle.ts` in `wt-2-c2-contracts`.

### 3.3 Data source: what a page may read — `src/data/ports.ts` + `src/data/source.ts`

```ts
// src/data/read.ts
export type Read<T> =
  | { ok: true; value: T }
  | { ok: false; reason: "denied"; permission: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: "pending_approval"; accessRequestId: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: "error"; code: string; status: number };   // no `exhausted`: no app read can produce it (§3.2)
export type PageKey = "fleet" | "run" | "agents" | "organization" | "billing" | "audit" | "shell";
export const PAGE_FAILURES: Record<PageKey, { error: { code: string; status: number }; permission: string }>;

// src/data/ports.ts — rev1, complete
export interface DataSource {
  pretenant: { orgs(ctx: PretenantCtx): Promise<Read<OrgChoice[]>>;                                   // list_orgs (scoped:false) — callers: features/shell/landing.ts, features/auth/cli-consent.ts
               workspaces(ctx: PretenantCtx, orgSlug: string): Promise<Read<WorkspaceChoice[]>> };   // list_workspaces (scoped:false, input {orgSlug}: workspace.list.ts:52-54)
  shell:     { context(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<ShellContext>> };                                   // list_orgs + list_workspaces (WL-11)
  runs:      { list(ctx: WsCtx, q: { cursor: string | null }): Promise<Read<RunPage>>;                 // list_runs
               get(ctx: WsCtx, runId: string, frames: { after: string | null }): Promise<Read<RunDetail>> }; // get_run
  approvals: { pending(ctx: WsCtx, q: { runId: string | null }): Promise<Read<ApprovalItem[]>> };      // list_approvals
  agents:    { list(ctx: WsCtx, q: { cursor: string | null }): Promise<Read<AgentPage>>;               // list_agents (#2956)
               get(ctx: WsCtx, agent: string): Promise<Read<AgentDetail>>;                             // get_agent
               toolbelt(ctx: WsCtx, agent: string): Promise<Read<Toolbelt>>;                           // get_agent_toolbelt
               incidents(ctx: WsCtx, agent: string, q: { cursor: string | null }): Promise<Read<IncidentPage>> }; // list_incidents {agentId}
  org:       { members(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<MemberList>>;                                       // list_members {scope:"org"}
               roles(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<RoleCatalog>>;                                        // list_iam_roles (#2964): roles, the permission catalogue, enforcement
               workspaces(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<WorkspaceList>>;                                 // list_workspaces {includeArchived:true} (#2964)
               apiKeys(ctx: WsCtx): Promise<Read<ApiKey[]>> };                                        // list_api_keys, in the workspace the key names (ADR-073)
  audit:     { events(ctx: OrgCtx, q: AuditPageQuery): Promise<Read<AuditPage>>;                      // query_audit_log (#3097); the day filters arrive resolved (AuditWindow)
               exportEvents(ctx: OrgCtx, q: AuditExportQuery): Promise<Read<AuditExport>> };           // export_audit_events, over the same window
  billing:   { plan(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<PlanCard>>;                                            // get_subscription
               bucket(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<GauBucket>>;                                         // get_gau_bucket: mode, meter, invoice thresholds, auto top-up state
               contractRate(ctx: OrgCtx): Promise<Read<ContractRate>>;                                // get_contract_rate
               invoices(ctx: OrgCtx, q: { cursor: string | null }): Promise<Read<InvoicePage>> };     // list_invoices
}
export function dataSource(): DataSource;   // src/data/source.ts: returns liveSource; the only importer of ./live
```

`src/data/live/pretenant.ts` is the only caller of the `PretenantCtx` overload of `kernelRead`, and its two methods have exactly two production callers: the `/` landing (`features/shell/landing.ts`, WL-32) and the CLI consent page (`features/auth/cli-consent.ts`, WL-12), so INV-17 holds. `shell.context` stays on `OrgCtx` for the switchers.

`Read` never carries `not_backed`: `data/not-backed.ts` and the `not_backed` variant go in WL-08, and `shell.context` is deleted in the same PR (today it is a stub, `data/adapters/live/shell.ts:11-12` returns `notBackedFor("shell","context")`; nothing in `src/` calls `list_orgs` or `list_workspaces` except a workspaces read at `live/org.ts:42,283` that WL-08 deletes). WL-11 adds `shell.context` back on `kernelRead`, as the kernel seam's first production caller. Between WL-08 and WL-11 the switchers render the current org and workspace from the layout's viewer (`server/viewer-resolution.ts:20-28` carries slug and name) with no list, so the `login` harness (WL-46) has a defined shell.

This replaces 13 ports and 83 methods (`data/ports.ts:97-270`), `data/backing.ts` (its `lane`/`store` bookkeeping only its tests read, `backing.test.ts:16,23,31`), and the fixture selection in `data/source.ts:12-17`. Components take `source: DataSource` as a prop; tests pass a fake built from typed builders.

### 3.4 Mapper: contract output to view model — `src/data/live/mappers/<port>.ts`

```ts
import type { ContractOutput } from "@/server/kernel";
export function toRunRow(o: ContractOutput<typeof runList>["runs"][number]): z.input<typeof RunRow>;
```

**Mappers are total and typed from the contract's `_output`, never from `z.output<>`.** The contracts are zod 3.25 (`packages/oxagen/package.json:35`) and the app is zod 4 (`apps/app/package.json:46`); zod 4's `output<T>` resolves to `unknown` for a schema without `_zod` (`node_modules/zod/v4/core/core.d.ts:34-38`), which is why `server/invoke.ts:38-54` already reads `_output`. `ContractOutput<C>` (§3.2) reads the same property, so a nullable contract field mapped into a required view field is a `tsc` error. `mappers/mappers.type-test.ts` carries two `// @ts-expect-error` cases: nullable contract field → required view field, and (the reverse, via a helper type `NullableOnlyWhenSourceIs<View, Out>`) a required contract field mapped into a nullable view field, so "nullable exactly when the source may not record it" holds in both directions.

A view-model field is `.nullable()` exactly when the contract can omit it; "not recorded" is a null cell, never a whole-page state. The adapter runs `View.parse` at the boundary; a failure returns `error("record_unmappable", 502)`, reported once. That is the fix for `runs.ts ~700` (returns early if one row fails) and `approvals.ts:158` (`notBacked` for any open item).

**No fabricated zeros (plan:1058).** `Cost` is `Cost | null` on every view model and contract where the source may not carry it; `tacho.sessions.total_cost_micros` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` with a nullable `cost_basis` (`packages/database/src/schema/tacho.ts:348-351`), so the `list_runs` handler maps a null `cost_basis` to `cost: null`, and a handler test proves it. The arch test `no-fabricated-zeros.test.ts` bans `?? 0`, `|| 0`, `?? "0"` and a literal `0`/`"0"` assigned to a `Count`- or `Money`-typed property under `src/data/live/mappers/**` and in the handlers of the contracts phase D adds.

### 3.5 Live stream: one SSE transport — `src/server/sse.ts` + `src/features/run/stream.ts`

```ts
export function sseResponse<F extends { cursor: string }>(opts: {
  signal: AbortSignal; start: string | null;
  next: (after: string | null) => Promise<Read<{ frames: F[]; cursor: string | null }>>;
  idleMs: number;
}): Response;                                                    // `id: <cursor>` per frame; `event: state` + close on a failed Read

export function handleRunStream(req: Request, params: { org: string; ws: string },
  deps: { resolveViewer: typeof resolveViewer; source: DataSource }): Promise<Response>;
```

The cursor is opaque and owned by `get_run` (fixes the fixture/ledger origin drift: `ports.ts:100-105`, `contracts/runs.ts:114`, `runs.ts:780-781`). Every poll is a `kernelRead` of a `noBillingGate` contract (§1.5), so a revoked membership stops the stream at the next poll and a poll never meters.

**Poll budget.** Every poll is a full kernel invoke: an IAM check (a `resolveOrgTierDetailed` query for a non-enterprise org, `fetchAuthz` for an enterprise one), one audit event to ClickHouse and one security event (`packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:1118-1145`, `packages/iam/src/check-iam.ts:165-180`). The stream keeps that write amplification bounded with a handler-side long poll instead of a client timer: `get_run` takes `waitMs: z.number().int().min(0).max(20_000)` (default 0) and its handler waits inside the tenant scope for up to `waitMs` for a frame after the cursor, checking the ledger every 500 ms, returning as soon as one lands or the wait expires. `handleRunStream` calls with `waitMs: 20_000`, so an idle open Run page costs at most three invokes (three audit rows) per minute and a busy one costs one invoke per frame batch; `idleMs` is 300 000 (five minutes without a frame closes the stream with `event: state`, and the client reconnects on the next user action). The numbers are constants in `features/run/stream.ts` and the handler test covers wait-then-frame, wait-then-timeout and `waitMs: 0`. The IAM query and the two emissions happen once per invoke whatever `waitMs` is, because the wait is inside the handler, after the gates and before the output validates (`kernel.ts:1313-1316,1543-1549`). A 20 s handler inside a stream that stays open for `idleMs` is new to `apps/app`: the stream `route.ts` exports `maxDuration` sized to `idleMs` plus one poll, and the hosting platform's function limit is the bound the constants must stay under (WL-35). Deleted: `server/stream-feeds.ts` (fleet feed never backed, `:36-37`), `server/stream-seq.ts`, `ui/hooks/use-fleet-live.ts`, `stream-merge.ts`.

### 3.6 Not recorded: one state — `src/data/unrecorded.ts` + `src/ui/not-recorded.tsx`

```ts
export const UNRECORDED = {
  agents: { gap: null }, tools: { gap: null }, steering: { gap: null },
  spend: { gap: null },                     // one row per kept page; its gap lane deletes the row
  "run.frames_wrapped": { gap: "G6" },
} as const satisfies Record<string, { gap: `G${number}` | null }>;
export function NotRecorded(props: { section: keyof typeof UNRECORDED }): React.JSX.Element; // prose from catalog `unrecorded.<key>`
```

Five rows at WL-33, and only slices genuinely unbuilt in this release (Tools is the exception the decision log of 2026-09-16 records: its row stays beneath the mandates ledger #2957 landed, naming the four tabs #2958 owes, and #2958 deletes it): the four whole-page rows exist because Appendix F redirects target those pages and the page-load smoke asserts their titles, and each gap lane deletes its page's row in the PR that lands the page (§8, Gap lanes), so the table shrinks and never grows. Audit has no row: it has a page with real UI (decision log, 2026-09-15; #3097). `run.frames_wrapped` is the one in-page row: a customer opening a wrapped run expects frames, and a silent absence reads as a defect. Every other unbacked in-page slice of revision 2 (`run.proof`, `run.context`, `run.cost_strip`, `organization.settings`, `billing.calculator`) renders nothing — no row, no prose, no data-gap attribute — because a roadmap placeholder is product surface a customer sees and "bare minimum" says no. Halt's disabled reason on ledger runs is dialog copy (`run.halt.ledgerReason`), not a NotRecorded row. Replaces `NotBacked { milestone, gap }` in the runtime `Read` (`data/not-backed.ts:9-33`), `ui/not-recorded-yet.tsx:20-30`, and every `notBacked("M1","G15")` on placeholder routes.

### 3.7 Pre-scope system reads and session — `src/server/tenancy-lookups.ts` + `src/server/session.ts`

```ts
export interface SystemLookups {                    // the only withSystemDb module in apps/app; read-only
  orgBySlug(slug): Promise<OrgRecord | null>;       orgBySlugHistory(slug): Promise<OrgRecord | null>;
  orgRole(orgId, userId): Promise<OrgRole | null>;
  workspaceBySlug(orgId, slug): Promise<WsRecord | null>; workspaceBySlugHistory(orgId, slug): Promise<WsRecord | null>;
  isWorkspaceMember(workspaceId, userId): Promise<boolean>;
  mfaPolicy(orgId): Promise<MfaPolicy>;             twoFactorEnabled(userId): Promise<boolean>;
  invitationByToken(token): Promise<InvitationRecord | null>;   // carries orgId and invitationId
}
export const systemLookups: SystemLookups;

// src/server/session.ts — the only @oxagen/auth server import
export function getSession(): Promise<AppSession | null>;
export function getAuthUser(): Promise<SessionUser | null>;
```

**The table allowlist (INV-05), as the module reads today.** The drizzle exports `tenancy-lookups.ts` may touch: `schema.organizations`, `orgSlugHistory`, `workspaces`, `workspaceSlugHistory`, `orgUsers`, `workspaceUsers`, `orgSecurityPolicy` (the MFA policy: `tenancy-lookups.ts:204-209`, `packages/database/src/schema/security.ts:145`), `users` with a column allowlist of `id` and `twoFactorEnabled` (`tenancy-lookups.ts:218-220`; `schema/auth.ts:63,78`), and `invitations`. Revision 2 named `org_mfa_policies` and `two_factor`, neither of which the module reads (`rg org_mfa_policies packages/database/src/schema` is empty; `auth.two_factor` at `schema/auth.ts:263` is written by the Better Auth plugin only). The arch test walks `schema.<table>` member accesses and also bans `.query.` member access (`tx.query.invitations.findFirst` is the form `invite-actions.ts:56-60` uses today) and any `sql` import from `drizzle-orm` in that file, so no access form escapes the walk.

**What is not in the seam, and why.** `cliScopes` reads `org_users`, `workspace_users`, `organizations` and `workspaces` (`features/auth/cli-authorize.ts:123-151`) while `list_orgs` and `list_workspaces` exist for exactly this picker and are `scoped: false, mutates: false` (`org.list.ts:38-45`, `workspace.list.ts:37-40`); the consent page (`features/auth/cli-consent.ts`) reads through the `pretenant` port (§3.3), which is `kernelRead(PretenantCtx, …)` behind `src/data/live/pretenant.ts`. `githubLanding` reads tenant `source_connections` rows with no IAM check (`github-setup-queries.ts:33-58`) to land on routes rev1 deletes; `/github/setup` keeps only its auth gate and lands on `/`.

**CLI code mint goes through the kernel.** `features/auth/cli-actions.ts:55-80` dynamically imports the `@oxagen/handlers` barrel for `actorCanManageApiKeys`, `runInTenantScope` from `@oxagen/tenancy` and `createCliAuthCode` from `@oxagen/auth/cli-auth`. Rev1 adds `authorize_cli` (WL-21): `mutates: true`, `scoped: true`, `noBillingGate: true`, `surfaces: []` (the mint has no API caller: the CLI opens the browser at `/cli/authorize`, `apps/cli/src/auth/loopback-login.ts:175`, and exchanges the code at `POST /v1/auth/cli/token`, `apps/api/src/routes/v1/auth.cli.token.ts:2`; consent needs a Better Auth browser session, so no API route or MCP tool is built; `surfaces` is optional, `types.ts:184`), `layers: ["schema","unit","docs"]`, `defaultRoles.org = {Owner, Admin}` (the set `API_KEY_AUTHORIZED_ROLES` encodes, `packages/handlers/src/lib/api-key-authz.ts:10`), input `{ codeChallenge, codeChallengeMethod, redirectUri, label, state }`, output `{ code }`; its handler owns the `@oxagen/auth/cli-auth` import and calls `assertOrgRole(ctx, { org: ["Owner", "Admin"] })` itself (§3.2): the IAM check does not enforce `defaultRoles` for a non-enterprise org, and today's predicate at `cli-actions.ts:55-66` is replaced, not dropped. The app action becomes `kernelWrite(await requireViewer(org, ws), authorizeCli, …)` then `redirectToLoopback(uri, { code, state })`.

**Replaces:** the `withSystemDb` reads in features (`create-organization.ts:59-140`, `invite-actions.ts:55-62`, `cli-authorize.ts:123-150`, `github-setup-queries.ts:33-100`), `data/adapters/live/onboarding.ts:18-60`, the duplicate `INVITATION_NOT_FOUND` (`invitations.ts:12`, `live/onboarding.ts:15`), and the fixture/live lookup switch (`server/scope.ts:34-36`).

### 3.8 Navigation: every redirect has a typed target — `src/shared/{safe-path,loopback-uri,checkout-url,navigation}.ts` + `src/ui/navigation.ts`

```ts
// src/shared/safe-path.ts      — same-origin paths from user input
export type SafePath = Branded<string, "safe-path">;
export function sanitizeNext(raw: string | null, fallback: SafePath): SafePath;   // refuses //, /\, schemes, control chars, sign-in targets
export const routes = { login(next?: SafePath), fleet(org, ws), run(org, ws, run), people(org), apiKeys(org), billing(org, q?: { checkout: "success" | "cancel" }), newOrganization(), … }; // every builder returns SafePath; people(org) is `/{org}` (no separate org(slug))

// src/shared/loopback-uri.ts   — RFC 8252 §7.3 loopback redirect for the CLI
export type LoopbackUri = Branded<string, "loopback-uri">;
export function parseLoopbackUri(raw: string): LoopbackUri | null;                 // http://127.0.0.1:<port>/… or http://[::1]:<port>/… only

// src/shared/checkout-url.ts   — the one external target
export type ExternalCheckoutUrl = Branded<string, "checkout-url">;
export function parseCheckoutUrl(raw: string): ExternalCheckoutUrl | null;         // https + host === "checkout.stripe.com" only

// src/shared/navigation.ts     — the only module that calls next/navigation redirect()
export function redirectTo(path: SafePath): never;
export function permanentRedirectTo(path: SafePath): never;                    // 308 to a renamed org's or workspace's canonical slug (viewer.ts)
export function redirectToLoopback(uri: LoopbackUri, q: { code: string; state: string } | { error: "access_denied"; state: string }): never;
export function redirectToCheckout(url: ExternalCheckoutUrl): never;
export function responseRedirect(req: Request, path: SafePath, status?: 307 | 308): NextResponse; // route handlers and proxy.ts; 308 for the App. F table

// src/ui/navigation.tsx        — the only useRouter importer, and the only file with a computed href/action
export function useNavigate(): { replace(path: SafePath): void; push(path: SafePath): void };
export function SafeLink(props: { to: SafePath; children: React.ReactNode; … }): React.JSX.Element;   // renders next/link with href={props.to}
export function SafeForm(props: { action: SafePath | ((fd: FormData) => Promise<…>); … }): React.JSX.Element;
```

`redirect`, `permanentRedirect`, `NextResponse.redirect` and `Response.redirect` are banned outside `shared/navigation.ts` (today: `app/github/setup/route.ts:16,27`, `proxy.ts:54`, `cli-actions.ts:89-91,112-114`); `useRouter` outside `ui/navigation.ts`; and, cheap now because none is used (`rg 'window.location|location.assign' apps/app/src` is empty), `window.location.{href,assign,replace}` (`MemberExpression[object.name='location']`) everywhere. Computed link targets are a syntactic rule, since no core, `@next` or typescript-eslint rule types a JSX attribute: `no-restricted-syntax` with selector `JSXOpeningElement[name.name=/^(a|Link|form)$/] > JSXAttribute[name.name=/^(href|action)$/] > JSXExpressionContainer` bans a non-literal `href` on `<a>`/`<Link>` and `action` on `<form>` everywhere but `src/ui/navigation.tsx`; components render `<SafeLink to={routes.run(org, ws, id)}>` and `<SafeForm action={…}>`, whose props are `SafePath`-typed, so the type check happens on the wrapper's props. The Better Auth `callbackURL` (`features/auth/ui/oauth-buttons.tsx:45`, `features/auth/actions.ts:166`) and `redirectTo` (`actions.ts:103`) arguments go through typed wrappers in `features/auth/auth-client.ts` that accept `SafePath` only. `successUrl` and `cancelUrl` for Checkout are `${NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL}${routes.billing(org, {checkout})}` built server-side, never from request headers; the package defaults `/billing/return` and `/billing/credits` (`packages/billing/src/checkout.ts:96,150-151`) are never reached.

### 3.9 Billing: GAU buckets at a contracted rate — the model, the two billing modes, the recorder, the contracts, the seams

**Today, in one paragraph.** The only allowance is annual, on the shared plan row (`plans.included_actions_annual`, `packages/database/src/schema/billing.ts:36-45`), counted over the calendar year (`action-metering.ts:283-285`; `governed_action_counters` keyed on org + `period_start`, `schema/billing.ts:657-700`), ignoring `subscriptions.current_period_start/end` (`:77-84`). Past the allowance the recorder prices at a global USD band (`ACTION_RATE_BANDS`, `action-metering.ts:56-81`) and debits a cents-denominated credit balance (`action-metering.ts:492-518` → `consumeCredits`); exhaustion is a zero credit balance (`metering.ts:157-164`), and the credits path already has an off-session auto-reload with a per-episode Stripe idempotency key (`autoreload.ts`, `org_billing_settings.auto_reload_episode_key`, `schema/billing.ts:400-414`, #1420). The only self-serve purchase sells dollars (`billing.credits.purchase.ts:25-52`; `stripe-provider.ts:631-671` creates one `price_data` line at `priceCents`, `quantity: 1`; `webhooks.ts:204-209` → `grantCreditPackForCheckout`). `billing.plans` carries prices, seats and an annual action count but no per-GAU rate, block size or currency (`schema/billing.ts:18-56`); `syncSubscriptionFromStripe` rewrites `subscriptions.plan_id` on every subscription webhook (`subscriptions.ts:37-91`); `resolveOrgActionEntitlement` already joins the org's entitled subscription to its plan (`plan-allowance.ts:50-99`). There is no invoice contract; `billing.invoices` is written only by `syncInvoiceFromStripe` (`invoices.ts:32-80`) from `invoice.*` events (`webhooks.ts:154-158,191-196`), and Stripe issues no Invoice for a payment-mode Checkout unless `invoice_creation` is enabled (`rg invoice_creation packages/billing/src` is empty). `ensureStripeCustomer` reuses a customer id off a `subscriptions` row, then falls back to a metadata search before creating one (`customers.ts:18-45`). The normalised Checkout session carries no invoice or payment-method field (`provider.ts:341-349`); `upsertPaymentMethod` resolves the org from a `subscriptions` row and returns for an org with none (`webhooks.ts:257-261`); `onInvoicePaymentFailed` and `onInvoiceRecovered` act on any invoice they can bind to an org, subscription or not (`dunning.ts:143-330`); `chargeOffSession` (`stripe-provider.ts:547-577`) is the one off-session charge and creates a bare PaymentIntent with no invoice. `create_org` grants $5 of credits after the org transaction (`org.create.ts:11,169-195` → `grants.ts:150-162`). `chargeEvidenceRetention` debits credits and has no production caller (`action-metering.ts:595-627`).

**The model (ADR-055, WL-23).** ADR-052 keeps its unit — the governed action — and its four exclusions. ADR-055 refines "tier allowance plus per-action overage" into:

1. **Published terms live on the plan.** `billing.plans` gains `currency`, `rate_per_gau_micros`, `block_size_gau`, `included_gau_per_month` (WL-24). `seed.ts` writes them for the Free plan (`PLAN_SEEDS`, `seed.ts:25-45`, the only static tier data today); paid plans get them from `pricing.ts` through `pnpm billing:stripe-sync`, the existing single source of truth for paid-plan rows (`seed.ts:29-33`). `plans.included_actions_annual` is dropped with the counters (WL-27).
2. **Negotiated terms live in one row per organization** (`billing.contract_terms`): `org_id`, `agreement_ref`, `currency`, `rate_per_gau_micros`, `block_size_gau`, `included_gau_per_month`, `effective_from`, `effective_to` (nullable). Partial unique index on `(org_id) WHERE effective_to IS NULL`. Written by an operator migration or a later `set_contract_terms` capability, never by the app, never by `create_org`. There is no `source`, `tier`, `stripe_price_id` or `exhaustion_policy` column: the source is implied by the table, the tier is the entitlement's, the checkout uses `price_data`, and what happens past the allowance is the org's billing mode (item 6), which lives on the org's billing row.
3. **Effective terms are resolved on every read.** `resolveContractTerms(orgId)` (in `packages/billing/src/contract-terms.ts`) returns the effective `contract_terms` row as `{ source: "negotiated", … }` when one exists, otherwise `{ source: "published_tier", tier, … }` from the plan row of the org's entitled subscription through `resolveOrgActionEntitlement`'s join, or the Free plan for an org with no entitled subscription. It does not read `organizations.plan_type`: `create_org` only ever writes `free` (`org.create.ts` contract `planSlug: z.literal("free")`), so that leg (`plan-allowance.ts:94-97`) only ever answers for pre-billing-table rows, and ADR-055 records that the GAU model has one resolver with one fallback; `resolveOrgTier` (`tier.ts:122-127`) keeps its leg because it answers a different question (the IAM tier gate, which WL-55 removes under maintainer decision 3 of 2026-09-15). A plan change through Stripe is reflected on the next read and the next bucket. CHECK on both tables: `(rate_per_gau_micros * block_size_gau) % 10000 = 0`, so a block prices to whole cents and the Checkout line needs no rounding.
4. **The allowance has a unit, per month, and so does the bucket.** Every `gau_buckets` row covers one month. `periodFor(subscription | null, now)` in `gau-bucket.ts` returns it: for an org with an entitled subscription, the month-long slice of the current cycle that contains `now`, starting on the cycle's anniversary day — `[addMonths(current_period_start, k), addMonths(current_period_start, k + 1))` for the `k` in `0…11` that contains `now`, where `addMonths` clamps to the last day of a shorter month the way Stripe's billing-cycle anchor does; for a `month` subscription that slice is `current_period_start/end` itself (`schema/billing.ts:77-84`); for an org with no subscription, the UTC calendar month. `included_gau = included_gau_per_month`, with no multiplier. The subscription is billed in advance by Stripe on its own cycle; the allowance is what each month of that advance payment includes. The published figures are per month in the spec's §4.2 table, at the v1 rates the maintainer set 2026-09-14 (`governed-action-metering.md` §4.2): 5,000 / 50,000 / 300,000 included GAUs a month on Free / Build / Scale at $0 / $199 / $999 a month, every published tier at 5,000 micros per GAU ($5 per 1,000) in 5,000-GAU blocks ($25.00), USD; Enterprise is negotiated on its `contract_terms` row and has no published plan (`enterprise-v2` leaves `SUBSCRIPTION_PLANS` and Stripe in WL-56, maintainer decision 3 of 2026-09-15). Free stops at exhaustion until the org saves a card or the next month opens (item 6, the 2026-09-14 rule). The page prints "included GAUs per month". Revision 4's `months_in_period` (12 for a `year` subscription) gave an annual subscriber a twelve-month allowance to spend in month one and, in invoice billing, one overage invoice a year; the amendment's model is monthly for every org (§9, R4-A3).
5. **A GAU bucket per organization per month** (`billing.gau_buckets`): `org_id`, `period_start`, `period_end`, `included_gau`, `purchased_gau`, `carried_gau`, `used_gau`, `overage_invoiced_gau` (invoice mode: overage already covered by an interim or period-close settlement), `interim_seq` (invoice mode: how many interim settlements this period has claimed), `topup_seq` and `open_topup_settlement_id` (prepaid: the auto top-up episode counter and the one episode allowed open at a time, nullable), `closed_at` (nullable; set by the close job). Unique index `(org_id, period_start)` — the arbiter for the recorder's upsert, carried over from `governed_action_counters_org_period_idx` (`schema/billing.ts:688-691`). CHECK: every count `>= 0`, `overage_invoiced_gau <= used_gau`. `remaining = included + purchased + carried − used` and **may be negative**: the gate checks `remaining > 0` before the handler and the recorder debits after it, so concurrent governed actions can drive `used_gau` past the total; the stored figure is not clamped and the page prints "overdrawn by N" (§1.4). `overage = max(0, used − included − purchased − carried)`. On rollover `carried_gau = min(prev.purchased + prev.carried, max(0, prev.remaining))`: bought units survive a month boundary; included ones do not. `readBucket` applies the same formula to the virtual bucket it computes when no row exists yet (item 9), so a prepaid org whose balance is all purchased units is admitted at the first action of a new month and the page prints its carry before the first action. There is no `terms_source`/`terms_ref` column: nothing reads one — terms are resolved live (item 3) and every settlement row records the rate it charged (item 7).
6. **Two billing modes, decided by the org's billing row.** `org_billing_settings` (one row per org, `schema/billing.ts:371-498`; it already holds the credits auto-reload preferences and the dunning state) gains, beside `stripe_customer_id` (WL-24):

   | Column | Type | Set by | Meaning |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `approved_for_invoice_billing` | `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` | platform operator (`set_org_billing_terms`, item 12); customer read-only | `true` → invoice billing; `false` → prepaid |
   | `invoice_gau_max` | `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 100000 CHECK > 0` | platform operator; customer read-only | read only when `approved_for_invoice_billing` is true; when false the value is stored and has no effect anywhere (no validation against it, no meaning in the UI beyond the mode label) |
   | `auto_topup_enabled` | `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true` | customer, Owner or Admin (`set_auto_topup`) | prepaid only |
   | `auto_topup_blocks` | `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK > 0` | customer, Owner or Admin | blocks charged per auto top-up |

   The GAU path reads the row through `readOrgBillingSettings(orgId)` (new in `billing-settings.ts`, WL-25): a `SELECT` that returns the column defaults for an org with no row and never inserts. `getOrgBillingSettings` (`billing-settings.ts:97-115`, an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on every call) keeps its callers on the credits and dunning paths; the gate, the recorder and `get_gau_bucket` never call it, so "a read never writes" (item 9, WL-27) is true of the settings read as well as the bucket read. The row is written by `set_auto_topup`, `set_org_billing_terms` and `ensureStripeCustomer`, each an upsert keyed on `org_id`.

   The behaviour beyond the allowance:

   | | Prepaid (default) | Invoice billing (`approved_for_invoice_billing = true`) |
   |---|---|---|
   | Source of extra GAUs | purchased blocks: unit quantities at the contracted rate, through Checkout (item 11) or auto top-up (item 8c) | accrued overage; consumption is never capped |
   | At exhaustion (`remaining ≤ 0` after a debit) | the recorder charges the saved payment method for `auto_topup_blocks` blocks immediately and the bucket continues; if `auto_topup_enabled` is false, no payment method is saved, or the charge does not succeed, the next governed action is refused by the gate (`gau_exhausted` / 402; the approval dialog links to `/{org}/billing`; for a Free org with no default payment method the error carries `reason: "free_no_payment_method"`) | never stops |
   | Invoicing | every block purchase and every auto top-up is a Stripe Invoice | one invoice per month at period end for the accrued overage at the contracted rate, unless accrued uninvoiced overage exceeds `invoice_gau_max` first — at overage unit `invoice_gau_max`+1 an interim invoice for exactly `invoice_gau_max` GAUs is finalized that day, a charge on the saved payment method is attempted that day, and that unit opens the next accrual period (maintainer decision 6 of 2026-09-15: the cap bounds overage beyond the allowance; WL-59 moves the comparison from `≥` to `>`) |
   | No saved payment method | auto top-up cannot run; the gate refuses at exhaustion. **Free saves a card or waits (maintainer, 2026-09-14: "they should be required to save a card after they burn through free tier once or they must wait for the next month").** A Free org that exhausts its 5,000-GAU monthly allowance is refused further governed actions (`gau_exhausted` / 402, `reason: "free_no_payment_method"`) until either (a) the next monthly period opens a new allowance, or (b) the org saves a payment method. Saving a card is the gate, not a purchase: once a card is on file the org behaves like any prepaid org — auto top-up (enabled by default, `auto_topup_blocks = 1`) charges the saved card for a 5,000-GAU block at the list rate and consumption continues; manual block purchase through Checkout is also available. The exhausted state on the billing page (§1.4) and in the approval dialog (WL-40) says "Add a payment method to keep governing this month, or your allowance renews on <date>". The card is saved as the default payment method by the first block purchase — the Checkout of item 11 collects it with `setup_future_usage: "off_session"`, so `purchase_gau_bucket` is offered to a Free org with no card and is the rev1 card-saving path — or by a SetupIntent (`createPaymentMethodSetupIntent`, `packages/billing/src/payment-methods.ts:121`, the helper a card form on the billing page would use; that form is a later lane, openQuestions). The refusal for want of a card applies only to the recorder's auto top-up (item 8c), never to the purchase | the interim and period-close invoices are created with `collection_method: "send_invoice"`, `days_until_due: 30`: Stripe emails the invoice, the hosted invoice page collects it, and `invoice.paid` settles it (openQuestions: the 30-day term, and that rev1 has no card form for an invoice-billed org) |

   Two rules adopted from the amendment's assumptions (openQuestions, for the maintainer to override): an interim invoice does not move the monthly clock — the period-end invoice covers the remainder of the month; and an interim or period-close invoice that is not paid on the day leaves the org running, flags it past due on the billing page, and stays collectable by Stripe (its retry schedule on a `charge_automatically` invoice; the hosted invoice page in either collection method) — no automatic suspension in rev1. On 2026-09-15 the maintainer confirmed the first rule and replaced the second (decision 12): an invoice-billed org is suspended 5 days after an interim or period-close invoice is past due, metering continues while it is suspended, and paying the full outstanding balance reactivates it (WL-60).
7. **One settlement ledger** (`billing.gau_settlements`; it replaces revision 3's `gau_purchases`): `id` (uuid, also the Stripe idempotency key), `org_id`, `bucket_id` (FK `gau_buckets`), `kind` (`checkout` | `auto_topup` | `interim_invoice` | `period_close`), `seq` (per bucket and kind; `NULL` for `checkout`), `quantity_gau`, `rate_per_gau_micros`, `currency`, `status`, `stripe_checkout_session_id` (nullable), `stripe_invoice_id` (nullable), `created_at`, `settled_at`. Four statuses, each with a named producer and reader:

   | `status` | Meaning | Set by | Read by |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `pending` | the row is claimed; Stripe holds no finalized invoice for it yet (`stripe_invoice_id` is `NULL` until `invoices.create` returns, then set; the draft it names is created with `auto_advance: false`, so Stripe never finalizes or collects it on its own) | `claimAutoTopup`, `claimInterimInvoice`, the close job's claim | the job's resume step (item 10) |
   | `open` | Stripe holds a finalized, unpaid invoice; Stripe owns collection from here | `settleGauOpen` — the synchronous result, or `invoice.payment_failed` | `get_gau_bucket.invoice.pastDue`, `.autoTopup.lastAttempt`; `list_invoices` through the mirror |
   | `paid` | the invoice is paid and the GAUs (for `checkout` and `auto_topup`) are granted — the only terminal state | `settleGauPaid` — the synchronous result, `invoice.paid`, or the Checkout grant | `lastAttempt`; `list_invoices` |
   | `failed` | Oxagen holds no invoice for this row, or deleted the draft it held: the idempotency window closed before Stripe ever answered (`idempotency_key_expired`; `stripe_invoice_id` is `NULL`), or a paid Checkout superseded a pending auto top-up (`superseded`; when `stripe_invoice_id` is set the job deleted the draft, or voided it when Stripe reported it `open`, before marking the row). A `failed` row never has a collectable invoice behind it: the draft was created with `auto_advance: false`, so a draft Oxagen did not finalize is never collected, and the superseded branch removes it anyway. The reason is in the job's log line, not a column. A `failed` auto top-up ends its episode: `settleGauFailed` clears `open_topup_settlement_id` wherever it names the row, so the month's next exhaustion claims a new one (decision log, 2026-09-15) | the job only | `lastAttempt` |

   Unique indexes: `(bucket_id, kind, seq) WHERE seq IS NOT NULL` — the database-level backstop for "one interim settlement per threshold crossing, one top-up per episode" — and `(stripe_checkout_session_id) WHERE NOT NULL` — the webhook grant's idempotency key. A `checkout` row is inserted by the webhook already `paid` (the session metadata is self-sufficient, so no handler-side pending insert and no `expired` status exist). `stripe_invoice_id` is the key `list_invoices` joins on to label an invoice's kind; the amount, the hosted URL and Stripe's own status are read from the webhook mirror `billing.invoices`, so the ledger carries no `amount_cents`, `failure_reason` or `hosted_invoice_url` column and no `(org_id, created_at)` index: every reader of a settlement reaches it through its bucket.
8. **The recorder path.** `recordGovernedAction({ orgId, actions, … })` stays the usage recorder `bootstrap.ts:39-52` installs; the kernel calls it once per completed top-level governed invocation, inside a re-entered tenant scope, catches anything it throws and never retries it (`packages/oxagen/src/kernel.ts:1544-1590`). So the debit is idempotent because it runs exactly once per invocation; every money operation the recorder starts carries its own key; and concurrent actions serialise on the bucket row. **Every bucket and settlement writer takes its executor as the first argument** — `ensureCurrentBucket(tx, …)`, `claimAutoTopup(tx, …)`, `claimInterimInvoice(tx, …)`, `recordGauInvoice(tx, …)`, `settleGauPaid(tx, …)`, `settleGauOpen(tx, …)`, `settleGauFailed(tx, …)` — the shape `tryInsertGrantLedger(tx, …)` has (`grants.ts:150-162`), never `withTenantDb` inside the function the way `incrementActionCounter` does (`action-metering.ts:305-317`, "the caller must be inside a tenant scope"). The recorder passes a `withTenantDb` transaction (the kernel re-entered the scope); the webhook branches and the job pass a `withSystemDb` transaction with the package's `// tenancy: system bypass` comment (`webhooks.ts:1-4`, `grants.ts:154-162`), because neither runs inside a tenant scope and `withTenantDb` there throws `TenantScopeError` under enforced RLS. In order:
   - **a. Resolve.** `terms = resolveContractTerms(orgId)`; `settings = readOrgBillingSettings(orgId)`; `mode = settings.approved_for_invoice_billing ? "invoice" : "prepaid"`; `period = periodFor(subscription, now)`.
   - **b. Debit, one statement.** `ensureCurrentBucket(tx, orgId, { period, terms, usedDelta: n, purchasedDelta: 0 })` runs `INSERT INTO billing.gau_buckets (org_id, period_start, period_end, included_gau, carried_gau, used_gau, purchased_gau) VALUES (…) ON CONFLICT (org_id, period_start) DO UPDATE SET used_gau = gau_buckets.used_gau + EXCLUDED.used_gau, purchased_gau = gau_buckets.purchased_gau + EXCLUDED.purchased_gau RETURNING *` — the statement shape of `incrementActionCounter` (`action-metering.ts:317-338`) on the caller's executor. The insert branch is the lazy create: `included_gau` from the terms (item 4), `carried_gau` from the previous bucket (item 5), read before the statement; if two recorders race the create, one inserts and the other lands in the conflict branch. The upsert takes the row lock, so a concurrent action for the same organization waits and reads the post-write total — the boundary is exact under concurrency. The same function, with `purchasedDelta` and `usedDelta: 0`, is how every grant lands (item 8c, the webhook paragraph): one writer for the bucket. The returned row is the input to c and d.
   - **c. Prepaid: auto top-up, at most one episode at a time.** If `remaining ≤ 0` and `auto_topup_enabled` and the org has a saved payment method (`billing.payment_methods` row with `is_default`, item 11): claim the episode — `UPDATE billing.gau_buckets SET open_topup_settlement_id = :id, topup_seq = topup_seq + 1 WHERE id = :bucket AND open_topup_settlement_id IS NULL AND included_gau + purchased_gau + carried_gau − used_gau <= 0 RETURNING topup_seq` — then, in the same transaction, insert the `auto_topup` settlement (`id = :id`, `seq = topup_seq`, `quantity_gau = auto_topup_blocks × block_size_gau`, the terms' rate and currency, `pending`). Postgres re-evaluates the `WHERE` after the row lock is granted (READ COMMITTED), so of N concurrent recorders exactly one gets a row back and the rest skip; the unique index on `(bucket_id, kind, seq)` backs that up. **The claim transaction commits before the first provider call**, so a crash anywhere after it leaves a `pending` row the job can resume; a claim rolled back after `pay` would have left a paid Stripe invoice under keys nothing remembers. The winner then runs the settlement sequence: `customerId = settings.stripe_customer_id` (non-null here: a default card exists only after a Checkout or a confirmed SetupIntent, each of which resolved the customer through `ensureStripeCustomer`, which wrote the column) → `provider.createGauInvoice(…)` (item 11) → `recordGauInvoice(tx, id, invoiceId)` (`UPDATE gau_settlements SET stripe_invoice_id = :inv WHERE id = :id AND stripe_invoice_id IS NULL`, committed) → `provider.finalizeAndPayGauInvoice(…)` → `paid` → `settleGauPaid(tx, id)`; `open` → `settleGauOpen(tx, id)`. `settleGauPaid` is `UPDATE gau_settlements SET status = 'paid', settled_at = now() WHERE id = :id AND status <> 'paid' RETURNING *` plus, for `checkout` and `auto_topup` kinds and only when the `UPDATE` returned a row, `ensureCurrentBucket(tx, orgId, { purchasedDelta: quantity })` and `UPDATE gau_buckets SET open_topup_settlement_id = NULL WHERE open_topup_settlement_id = :id`, in one transaction: the guard makes the synchronous result, the `invoice.paid` webhook of the first attempt and the `invoice.paid` of Stripe's later retry converge on one grant whichever arrives first, a row already `paid` changes nothing, and a row that was `open` or `failed` still grants when its invoice is eventually paid. The grant goes to the org's **current** bucket through the lazy create — the settlement's own bucket while the month is running, the next month's bucket when Stripe collects after rollover — so a late payment is never credited to a closed bucket whose carry was already computed. `settleGauOpen` is `UPDATE … SET status = 'open' WHERE id = :id AND status = 'pending'`; it leaves `open_topup_settlement_id` pointing at the row, so the month gets no second automatic attempt: the episode ends when that settlement is paid (Stripe's retry on the card, or the customer paying the hosted invoice) or a paid Checkout clears it, or the next month opens a new bucket. A `pending` auto top-up the job marks `failed` (item 10) ends its episode the same way: `settleGauFailed` runs `UPDATE gau_buckets SET open_topup_settlement_id = NULL WHERE open_topup_settlement_id = :id` in its transaction, so an org whose top-up never reached Stripe inside the idempotency window gets its next automatic attempt at the next exhaustion. No saved payment method or `auto_topup_enabled = false` → no claim, no row; the gate refuses the next action and the page says why — for a Free org with no card, that it must add a payment method or wait for the next month (item 6).
   - **d. Invoice billing: the threshold.** `uninvoiced = max(0, used − included − purchased − carried) − overage_invoiced_gau`. If `uninvoiced > invoice_gau_max` (maintainer decision 6 of 2026-09-15; the tree compares `≥` until WL-59): claim — `claimInterimInvoice(tx, bucketId, invoice_gau_max)`: `UPDATE billing.gau_buckets SET overage_invoiced_gau = overage_invoiced_gau + :quantity, interim_seq = interim_seq + 1 WHERE id = :bucket AND used_gau − included_gau − purchased_gau − carried_gau − overage_invoiced_gau >= :quantity RETURNING interim_seq` — then insert the `interim_invoice` settlement (`seq = interim_seq`, `quantity_gau = :quantity`, `pending`) in the same transaction, commit, and run the same settlement sequence with `customerId = ensureStripeCustomer(orgId)` (tenant scope; it returns `org_billing_settings.stripe_customer_id` when set and creates the customer once otherwise — an invoice-billed org's first Stripe object may be this invoice) and the collection method item 11 derives from the org's default payment method. The re-checked `WHERE` gives exactly one claim per threshold crossing; a second crossing in the same month gets `seq + 1`; accrual restarts at GAU #`invoice_gau_max`+1 by construction, because the claim subtracts exactly `invoice_gau_max`. The same function with `quantity = uninvoiced` is what `set_org_billing_terms` calls when it turns invoice billing off with overage outstanding (item 12); a threshold crossing is the only caller that passes the max. Invoice-kind settlements never touch `purchased_gau`, with one exception: when `set_org_billing_terms` turns invoice billing off, the close moves `overage_invoiced_gau` into `purchased_gau` (item 12; maintainer decision 5 of 2026-09-15, WL-58). The org keeps running in every branch.
   - **e. Never throws.** Every provider call is in a `try`; a failure leaves the settlement `pending` — with `stripe_invoice_id NULL` when `createGauInvoice` failed, with the id when `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice` did — and the hourly job (item 10) resumes it from Stripe's own state. The kernel's own catch (`kernel.ts:1577-1590`) is a backstop.
9. **The gate.** `assertGauAvailable(orgId)` replaces `assertCanStartTurn` in `setBillingAdmissionGate` (`bootstrap.ts:33`); the credit-balance gate survives only inside the ADR-053 platform-funded assistant turn (`turn-credit-gate.ts`), which no rev1 app path invokes. It reads the settings (`readOrgBillingSettings`) and the current bucket through `readBucket` (a virtual bucket when no row exists yet — `included` from the terms, `carried` from the previous row by the item 5 formula, `used = 0`; the gate never writes) and: throws `BillingSuspendedError { code: "billing_suspended" }` when `dunning_state = 'suspended'` (`assertOrgCanConsume`, kept: subscription dunning is unchanged, and it applies in either mode); returns for invoice billing; returns for prepaid with `remaining > 0`; otherwise throws `GauExhaustedError { code: "gau_exhausted" }` — with `reason: "free_no_payment_method"` when the resolved terms are the published Free tier and the org has no default `billing.payment_methods` row, so the page and the dialog can print the add-a-card line (item 6), and no reason otherwise (a Free org with a card is on the prepaid path: auto top-up ran or could not, exactly as for Build). The gate never charges: a gate that charges fails open when Stripe is down (`bootstrap.ts:39-43`). The API maps `gau_exhausted` to 402 beside the codes at `apps/api/src/middleware/error.ts:18-22`; `insufficient_credits` keeps its row for the assistant path.
10. **One scheduled job: `billing.gau-close`** (`packages/inngest-functions/src/functions/billing.gau-close.ts`, `createFunction` with `cron: "10 * * * *"`, the shape of `billing.dunning-sweep.ts:20-23` at an hourly cadence, keyset-paged the same way). It runs hourly because the amendment wants an interim invoice cut and charged on the day it is claimed, and a resume step that runs at 00:10 turns a Stripe outage on the 17th into an invoice on the 18th. Every write is on a `withSystemDb` transaction; Stripe customers come from `ensureStripeCustomer(orgId, { system: true })`, the `opts.system` switch `getOrgBillingSettings` already has (`billing-settings.ts:80-96`), since the job runs with no tenant scope. Step 1, `closeEndedGauPeriods`: for every bucket with `period_end <= now()` and `closed_at IS NULL` — invoice billing with `uninvoiced > 0` → claim (`UPDATE … SET closed_at = now(), overage_invoiced_gau = overage_invoiced_gau + :uninvoiced WHERE id = :bucket AND closed_at IS NULL RETURNING`), insert the `period_close` settlement (`seq = 0`, `quantity_gau = uninvoiced`), commit, then the item 8c settlement sequence; prepaid, or nothing to invoice → `closed_at = now()`. Months end on each org's own anniversary day (or the calendar month end for the fallback), so each org closes on its own day. Step 2, `resumePendingGauSettlements`, over `status = 'pending' AND created_at < now() − interval '1 hour'`: an `auto_topup` row whose bucket now has `remaining > 0` (a Checkout landed in between) → when `stripe_invoice_id` is set, `provider.deleteOrVoidDraftInvoice({ settlementId, invoiceId })` first (`invoices.retrieve`; `draft` → `invoices.del`; `open` → `invoices.voidInvoice`; already `void` or `deleted` → nothing; `paid` → the call returns `{ outcome: "paid" }` and the job runs `settleGauPaid(tx, id)` instead, because the customer was charged and the grant is owed), then `settleGauFailed(tx, id)`, logged `superseded`, no charge — a draft created with `auto_advance: false` would never collect on its own, and deleting it keeps a superseded row from leaving a draft in the customer's Stripe account that a later manual finalization could turn into a charge; a row with `stripe_invoice_id IS NULL` younger than 24 hours → `createGauInvoice` with the same keys — Stripe returns the stored result for a replayed key, so this recovers only a request that never reached Stripe or whose response was lost, which is the only way a row can be in this state; older than 24 hours → `settleGauFailed`, logged `idempotency_key_expired` with alert `billing_gau_settlement_stale`, the rule the credits auto-reload already applies (`schema/billing.ts:408-414`), and for an `auto_topup` row the same transaction clears the bucket's `open_topup_settlement_id` so the month can claim another episode (`settleGauFailed`, #3073); a row with `stripe_invoice_id` set → `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice`, which reads the invoice from Stripe and does only what is left. Both steps are idempotent and safe under Inngest retries: a second run finds nothing to claim and nothing pending.
11. **The Stripe objects, one per path.**

   | Path | Stripe object | Created by | Idempotency | Settled by |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | Subscription (the advance bill and the included allowance) | Subscription via `createSubscriptionCheckout` (existing) | `start_subscription_upgrade`, bound on the Billing page's Plan card in rev1 (WL-66, maintainer decision 7 of 2026-09-15) | existing | `customer.subscription.*` → `syncSubscriptionFromStripe` (existing) |
   | Block purchase | Checkout Session `mode: "payment"`, one `price_data` line (`unit_amount: blockPriceCents`, `quantity: blocks`, `product_data.metadata.oxagen_kind: "gau_block"`; after WL-57 `price_data.product` names the catalogued GAU block product in place of `product_data`, maintainer decision 2 of 2026-09-15), session metadata `{ oxagen_kind: "gau_purchase", org_id, gau_quantity, block_size_gau, rate_per_gau_micros, currency }`, `invoice_creation: { enabled: true, invoice_data: { metadata: same } }`, `payment_intent_data: { setup_future_usage: "off_session" }` (the card Checkout collects is saved to the customer for auto top-up; Checkout shows the customer that it will be) | `purchase_gau_bucket` handler → `BillingProvider.createGauCheckout` | the session id (the webhook grant is an upsert on it) | `checkout.session.completed` → `grantGauPurchaseForCheckout`; `invoice.paid` → mirror + receipt |
   | Auto top-up | Invoice, in two provider calls. `createGauInvoice({ customerId, orgId, settlementId, kind, quantityGau, ratePerGauMicros, currency, description, collection })`: `invoices.create({ customer, auto_advance: false, pending_invoice_items_behavior: "exclude", metadata: { org_id, oxagen_kind: "gau_auto_topup", gau_settlement_id, gau_quantity, rate_per_gau_micros, currency }, …collection })` where `collection` is `{ collection_method: "charge_automatically", default_payment_method }` when the org has a default `billing.payment_methods` row and `{ collection_method: "send_invoice", days_until_due: 30 }` otherwise, then `invoiceItems.create({ customer, invoice, quantity: quantityGau, unit_amount_decimal: <rate in cents, micros ÷ 10 000, at most four decimals>, currency, description })`; returns `{ invoiceId }`. `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice({ settlementId, invoiceId })`: `invoices.retrieve`; `draft` → `invoices.finalizeInvoice(invoiceId, { auto_advance: true })`; `open` and `charge_automatically` → `invoices.pay({ off_session: true })` (the invoice carries its `default_payment_method`); returns `{ status: "paid" | "open", amountCents: invoice.amount_due, hostedInvoiceUrl }`. `auto_advance` is `false` on create and `true` on finalize: Stripe's automatic collection and its retry schedule (the Smart Retries on a `charge_automatically` invoice, the reminder emails on a `send_invoice` one) apply only to an invoice Oxagen finalized, and a draft Oxagen abandons — a crash between `invoices.create` and `finalizeInvoice`, or a superseded top-up — is never finalized or collected by Stripe (Stripe, *Automatic advancement and collection*, https://docs.stripe.com/invoicing/integration/automatic-advancement-collection: an invoice with `auto_advance: true` is finalized automatically one hour after creation and then collected on its schedule; with `auto_advance: false` it stays a draft until the integration finalizes it). Stripe creates and confirms the PaymentIntent on the saved payment method under the invoice, so the charge and the invoice are one record | recorder (item 8c) → the two provider calls with `recordGauInvoice` between them | the settlement id, suffixed per request (`<id>:invoice`, `<id>:item`, `<id>:finalize`, `<id>:pay`); a replay within 24 hours returns the same objects, and `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice` is idempotent by Stripe's state whatever the key | the synchronous result, then `invoice.paid` / `invoice.payment_failed` (`gauSettlementId`) |
   | Interim invoice (`invoice_gau_max` reached) | the same Invoice shape, `oxagen_kind: "gau_interim"` | recorder (item 8d) | same | same |
   | Period-close invoice | the same Invoice shape, `oxagen_kind: "gau_period_close"` | `billing.gau-close` (item 10) | same | same |

   `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice` returns `open` and never throws for every outcome in which Stripe holds a finalized invoice that is not paid: a card error on `pay`, `invoice_payment_intent_requires_action` (SCA), a customer with no payment source (Stripe's `invalid_request_error` for `pay` on a customer without one — unreachable once `collection` is chosen from the default payment method, and covered anyway), and a `send_invoice` invoice, for which `pay` is never called. From finalization on, Stripe owns collection: its retry schedule works a `charge_automatically` invoice (what "Stripe dunning handles retries" means in item 6), and the hosted invoice page collects either kind. Any other error throws, and the row stays `pending` with its invoice id for the job to resume. `unit_amount_decimal` carries the sub-cent per-GAU rate exactly (a rate in micros is at most four decimal places of a cent) and Stripe rounds the line once; the app never computes an invoice amount. `chargeOffSession` is not used on this path: a bare PaymentIntent leaves no invoice, and the amendment wants one per top-up. There is no `{ price }` branch on a GAU path: the GAU block product WL-57 catalogues under a lookup key is named by `price_data.product` while the amount stays the contracted rate (maintainer decision 2 of 2026-09-15), and `createPaymentCheckout` (`provider.ts:452-455`, `stripe-provider.ts:612-627`) already covers that case for a later lane.
12. **Who may change the mode.** `set_org_billing_terms` is a platform-operator capability: `scoped: false`, `surfaces: []`, `layers: ["schema", "unit", "docs"]`, `sensitivity: "high"`, `noBillingGate: true`, `defaultEffect: "deny"`, `defaultRoles: {}`, and a new contract field `platformOnly: true`. The kernel refuses a `platformOnly` capability before the IAM check unless the `CapabilityContext` carries a kernel-issued `platformOperator` binding (`CapabilityError("authz_denied")`). The binding is a kernel-issued object: `createPlatformOperatorContext({ requestId })` in `packages/oxagen/src/platform-operator.ts` mints it and records it in a module-private `WeakSet`, the pattern `deployedAgentInvocation` already uses (`types.ts:442-453`, `kernel.ts:908-948`); `_invokeCore` treats any `platformOperator` value the set does not hold — a literal `true`, a spread copy, a JSON-shaped object — as a forged platform binding and refuses it with a security event, the branch that already refuses a caller-supplied `authorizationDecision`. No surface context builder mints one — not `apps/api/src/middleware/auth.ts`, not the MCP context, not `src/server/kernel.ts` step 3 — and each builder's unit test asserts the key is absent (INV-31). The one caller is the operator script `tools/scripts/billing-terms.ts` (`pnpm billing:terms --org <slug> --invoice-billing on|off --invoice-gau-max <n>`), beside `stripe-sync.ts`, run with the production `DATABASE_URL` from Parameter Store the way `pnpm billing:stripe-sync` and `db-migrate.yml` are; it loads the handler registry and invokes the contract with `{ platformOperator: createPlatformOperatorContext(…), surface: "runner", requestId }` — `surface` being the `CapabilityContext.surface` field, never `opts.surface`, which `surfaces: []` would refuse as `surface_denied` (`kernel.ts:993`) — so every credit decision is a kernel audit row. The handler runs on `withSystemDb` (the context is unscoped and carries no tenant; its upsert on `org_billing_settings` is keyed on the input's `orgId`), and when it turns `approved_for_invoice_billing` off on an org whose current bucket has `uninvoiced > 0` it closes the accrual in the same call — `claimInterimInvoice(tx, bucketId, uninvoiced)` and the item 8c settlement sequence with `ensureStripeCustomer(orgId, { system: true })` — so no overage is stranded between the modes (the close job invoices only buckets that are in invoice mode when it closes them). Before that claim it closes every bucket of the org whose month has ended and whose `closed_at` is still NULL, the item 10 close with the org still invoice-billed, so a month that ended before the switch and before the next `billing.gau-close` run gets its `period_close` settlement. After that claim the same transaction adds the bucket's `overage_invoiced_gau` to `purchased_gau` and sets it to 0, so the prepaid bucket opens at `remaining = 0` with no overdraft and a later switch back counts nothing twice (maintainer decision 5 of 2026-09-15, WL-58). Turning it on needs no settlement: purchased units stay usable as carry. The customer sees the mode and the cap read-only on the billing page. `set_auto_topup` is the customer half: `mutates: true`, `scoped: true`, `noBillingGate: true`, Owner or Admin checked in the handler (§3.2), input `{ enabled, blocks: int 1…100 }`, an upsert on `org_billing_settings`.
13. **Tokens and retention are not on the page.** `get_subscription.periodUsage` stays a report (§1.4). No table or app component carries a retention rate; ADR-055 states that retention settles later as an invoice line at a contracted per-GB-month rate added to `plans` and `contract_terms` by the lane that meters it. `chargeEvidenceRetention` and `retentionCreditsForGbMonths` are deleted in WL-25 (no caller; `RETENTION_INCLUDED_MONTHS` and `RETENTION_USD_PER_GB_MONTH` stay for the two handlers that print them, `billing.evidence_retention.ts:27-28`, `billing.action_rate_card.ts:20-21`). `OXAGEN_ACTION_METER_MODE` and `resolveActionMeterMode` (`action-metering.ts:260-264`, `packages/config/src/env.ts:334`, `registry.ts:763`, `.env.example:194`) go with the credit debit in WL-25: a shadow mode that charged a card or cut an invoice would not be a shadow, and a shadow that did neither would leave a prepaid org running past exhaustion with no record; no config stays without a reader. The maintainer waived the metering shadow period on 2026-09-15 (decision 14; metering spec §6).
14. **`create_org` writes the $5 signup grant and no other billing row.** WL-16 deleted the `grantFreeCredits` call with its retry; the maintainer's decision of 2026-09-15 (§9) restores the grant as `grantSignupCredits(tx, orgId)` on the org's bootstrap transaction, so an org and the grant that funds its first in-app agent turns commit together. No `contract_terms`, `gau_buckets`, `gau_settlements` or `org_billing_settings` row is written at creation; the settings row appears on the first write that needs it (item 6).
15. **Which invokes are governed actions** is §1.5, restated in the ADR. `resolve_approval` bills a decision that matched a row: when its `UPDATE` matches none (unknown id, already resolved, expired, wrong workspace — today's `updated.length === 0 ? "expired" : input.decision`, `agent.approval.resolve.ts:39-47`, returned as a success) the handler throws `HandlerError { code: "conflict", reason: "approval_expired" }` (WL-14, on WL-22's `HandlerError`), so the invocation leaves through the kernel's catch and the recorder never runs; `expired` leaves the contract's output enum (`agent.approval.resolve.ts:27`). A no-op that returned a success would be one GAU and, for a prepaid org at `remaining ≤ 0`, an auto top-up charge (§9, R4-I3).

**The contracts.**

| Contract | State | Shape (relevant part) |
|---|---|---|
| `get_subscription` | exists; gains `noBillingGate: true` | unchanged output; the app mapper reads `subscription` only (§1.4) |
| `get_gau_bucket` | **new** (WL-27); `mutates: false`, `noBillingGate: true`, Owner/Admin/Billing | `{ mode: "prepaid" \| "invoice", period: { start, end }, includedGau, purchasedGau, carriedGau, usedGau, remainingGau, invoice: { gauMax, uninvoicedGau, invoicedThisPeriodGau, pastDue } \| null, autoTopup: { enabled, blocks, paymentMethod: { brand, last4 } \| null, lastAttempt: { at, status: "paid" \| "open" \| "failed" } \| null } \| null }` — integers, dates, booleans and short strings; `invoice` is non-null exactly in invoice mode and `autoTopup` exactly in prepaid; no money, no `blockSizeGau` (the rate block owns it; two readers of one figure on one page can disagree), no `meterMode`. The handler reads through `readBucket` and `readOrgBillingSettings` — a virtual bucket with its carry when no row exists — and never inserts (a read that writes is the defect §7.1 removes; the recorder's lazy create is the only writer); `pastDue` is true while any `interim_invoice` or `period_close` settlement of the period is `open`; `lastAttempt` is the latest `auto_topup` row of the period; `paymentMethod` is the org's default `billing.payment_methods` row |
| `get_action_usage` | exists; **retired** in WL-27 with its deprecated panel (it reads `readActionCounter`, `handlers/billing.action_usage.ts:31,161`, and prints bands and true-ups in USD, `app_deprecated/.../action-usage-panel.tsx:90-91,133-144`) | — |
| `get_contract_rate` | new (WL-26); `mutates: false`, `noBillingGate: true`, Owner/Admin/Billing | `{ source: "published_tier" \| "negotiated", agreementRef: string \| null, tier, currency, ratePerGauMicros: string, blockSizeGau, includedGauPerMonth, effectiveFrom, effectiveTo: string \| null }` — from `resolveContractTerms`, never `ACTION_RATE_BANDS` |
| `purchase_gau_bucket` | new (WL-28); `mutates: true`, `scoped: true`, `noBillingGate: true`, `sensitivity: "high"`, Owner/Billing, `agent.requiresApproval: true` | input `{ quantityGau: z.number().int().positive().max(1_000_000), successPath, cancelPath }` (paths validated app-relative; the handler prefixes `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`); output `{ checkoutUrl, quantityGau, blockSizeGau, blocks }`; refuses `quantityGau % blockSizeGau !== 0` as `invalid_input` and refuses an invoice-billed org with `HandlerError { code: "conflict", reason: "invoice_billed" }` (the page does not render the form in that mode; the refusal is for API and MCP callers); a Free org with no saved card is not refused — its Checkout saves the card (item 6); the handler recomputes the amount from `resolveContractTerms` at submit time, so a terms change between render and submit cannot mis-price (Stripe shows the authoritative figure); no money field |
| `set_auto_topup` | new (WL-30); `mutates: true`, `scoped: true`, `noBillingGate: true`, Owner/Admin | input `{ enabled: boolean, blocks: z.number().int().min(1).max(100) }`; output the same two fields as stored; prepaid orgs only in the UI, accepted in either mode (the columns exist in both; they are read in prepaid) |
| `set_org_billing_terms` | new (WL-30); `mutates: true`, `scoped: false`, `platformOnly: true`, `surfaces: []`, `sensitivity: "high"`, `noBillingGate: true` | input `{ orgId, approvedForInvoiceBilling: boolean, invoiceGauMax: z.number().int().min(1).max(100_000_000) }`; output the stored row; upsert on `org_billing_settings` on `withSystemDb`; closes the accrual when switching invoice billing off (item 12); the only caller is `tools/scripts/billing-terms.ts` |
| `list_invoices` | new (WL-29); `mutates: false`, `noBillingGate: true`, Owner/Admin/Billing; cursor paging | items `{ publicId, number, status, kind: "subscription" \| "gau_purchase" \| "gau_auto_topup" \| "gau_interim" \| "gau_period_close", amountDueMicros: string, amountPaidMicros: string, currency, periodStart, periodEnd, hostedInvoiceUrl }`; reads the webhook-mirrored `billing.invoices` LEFT JOIN `gau_settlements` on `stripe_invoice_id` for `kind` (`subscription` when no settlement matches), superseding App. A:1947; an `open` interim invoice of a card-less org is listed with its hosted URL, which is how that org pays it |
| `get_evidence_retention` | exists (`noBillingGate` already); retired at cutover by WL-50 (maintainer decision 7 of 2026-09-15) | read today by the audit adapter's `retention` port (`live/audit.ts:349-367`), which WL-08 deletes; no rev1 port reads it (§1.4); untouched for its API and MCP callers |
| `purchase_credits`, `start_subscription_upgrade` | exist; neither is retired (maintainer decisions 2 and 7 of 2026-09-15) | `purchase_credits` is the in-app AI usage credit top-up of the ADR-053 balance, bound on the Billing page with its contract and handler changes by WL-67 (§9, 2026-09-15, two meters); `start_subscription_upgrade` is the Build and Scale upgrade on the synced Stripe prices, bound on the Plan card with its contract and handler changes by WL-66; Enterprise is negotiated on its `contract_terms` row and has no Checkout. The Billing page sells GAU blocks and credit packs, each on its own meter, and no dollars of governed-action usage |
| `get_rate_card`, `preview_action_cost` | exist; retired at cutover by WL-50 with `get_evidence_retention` (contract, handler, API route, MCP tool and docs deleted, and the WL-50 ADR names their retired `app` layer; maintainer decision 7 of 2026-09-15); until then their `includedActionsAnnual` figure is `included_gau_per_month × 12` from the plan row once WL-27 drops the column (`plan-allowance.ts:87-90` is the one line that changes; `billing.action_rate_card.ts:59,66,85`, `billing.action_estimate.ts:65-110` and `bootstrap.ts:48` are untouched) | — |

Every new `packages/handlers/src/billing.*.ts` file meets `pnpm check:audit-coverage` (`packages/compliance/src/audit-coverage.test.ts:96-137`, on every PR): the three mutations (`purchase_gau_bucket`, `set_auto_topup`, `set_org_billing_terms`) call `emitSecurityEvent(` and the three reads (`get_gau_bucket`, `get_contract_rate`, `list_invoices`) carry `// audit-exempt: <reason>`.

**The provider and the webhook.** `BillingProvider` gains five methods and two fields. `createGauCheckout({ customerId, orgId, quantityGau, blocks, blockPriceCents, ratePerGauMicros, currency, successUrl, cancelUrl })` builds the Checkout Session of item 11 — one method, one path, no `{ price }` branch. `createGauInvoice(…)` and `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice(…)` are the Invoice path of item 11, with `auto_advance: false` on `invoices.create` and `auto_advance: true` on `invoices.finalizeInvoice`. `deleteOrVoidDraftInvoice({ settlementId, invoiceId })` is the close job's cleanup for a superseded row that already holds an invoice id (item 10): `invoices.retrieve`, then `invoices.del` for a `draft`, `invoices.voidInvoice` for an `open` invoice (keyed `<id>:void`; `del` needs no key — a second delete of a deleted invoice is a `resource_missing` the method treats as done), nothing for one already `void` or `deleted`, and `{ outcome: "paid" }` for a paid one, which the job settles as paid; it returns `{ outcome: "deleted" | "voided" | "paid" | "absent" }` and never throws for a state it recognises. `getCheckoutPaymentMethod(sessionId)` retrieves the session with `expand: ["payment_intent.payment_method"]` — one level deeper than `payment_intent`, which would leave `payment_method` a string id — and returns `{ id, brand, last4, expMonth, expYear } | null`. `BillingCheckoutSession` gains `invoiceId: string | null` (`sess.invoice`, a string or an expanded object, present only when `invoice_creation` was enabled), since the grant records `gau_settlements.stripe_invoice_id` and the normalised session carries no invoice today (`provider.ts:341-349`). `BillingInvoice` gains `gauSettlementId: string | null`, read from `invoice.metadata.gau_settlement_id` in `stripeInvoiceToNeutral` beside `resolveInvoiceOrgId` (`stripe-provider.ts:227-237`), because the normalised invoice keeps only `orgId` and `billingReason` from the metadata today (`provider.ts:210-233`) and the webhook dispatch below routes on it. `ensureStripeCustomer(orgId, opts?: { system?: boolean })` reads `org_billing_settings.stripe_customer_id` first and writes it after creation through an upsert keyed on `org_id` (nothing creates the row for a new org — `org.create.ts` has no reference, WL-16 writes nothing billing-shaped — so the write is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (org_id) DO UPDATE SET stripe_customer_id`, and a test runs it against an org with no row); `opts.system` routes it through `withSystemDb` for the job and the operator handler, the switch `getOrgBillingSettings` already carries (`billing-settings.ts:80-96`), because today's function reads through `withTenantDb` only (`customers.ts:19`). The column is the authoritative id because the existing fallback, `provider.findCustomerByOrgId` (`customers.ts:38-45`), is a metadata search that is eventually consistent, so two quick purchases by a subscription-less org can each create a customer. `upsertPaymentMethod` (`webhooks.ts:244-303`) resolves the org from `org_billing_settings.stripe_customer_id` before the `subscriptions` lookup, so a Free org's card events mirror too.

The webhook dispatch (`webhooks.ts:133-242`) changes in three places, every GAU write on a `withSystemDb` transaction passed into the settlement functions (item 8). `checkout.session.completed` branches on `metadata.oxagen_kind === "gau_purchase"` → `grantGauPurchaseForCheckout(session)`: an **upsert keyed on the session id** that reads quantity, block size, rate and currency from the session metadata (self-sufficient: the handler inserts nothing pending), `paid` only, inserts the `checkout` settlement as `paid` with `stripe_invoice_id = session.invoiceId`, adds the quantity to the current bucket's `purchased_gau` through `ensureCurrentBucket(tx, …)` and clears `open_topup_settlement_id` on the bucket that holds one, then saves the card: `getCheckoutPaymentMethod(session.id)`, `setDefaultPaymentMethod(customerId, pm.id)` when the customer has none (`getDefaultPaymentMethodId`), and an upsert of the `billing.payment_methods` row with `is_default = true`; a second delivery grants nothing (test); every other session keeps `grantCreditPackForCheckout`. `invoice.paid` with `gauSettlementId` set → `settleGauPaid(tx, id)`; `invoice.payment_failed` with it set → `settleGauOpen(tx, id)` (a no-op when the synchronous path already recorded `open`); both after `syncInvoiceFromStripe`, both idempotent (item 8c), and neither reaches the dunning functions: `onInvoiceRecovered` and `onInvoicePaymentFailed` run **only when `invoice.subscriptionId !== null`** — today they act on any invoice, so without the guard a suspended org that bought one GAU block would be reset to `active` by a payment that did not settle the overdue subscription invoice (`dunning.ts:280-330` clears `delinquent_since`, `grace_ends_at`, `suspended_at` for any paid invoice it can bind to an org), and a declined GAU top-up would start dunning (`dunning.ts:143-270`). A webhook test covers each: an org in `suspended` receives `invoice.paid` for a `gau_purchase` invoice and its dunning row is unchanged; a subscription invoice still recovers it; `invoice.payment_failed` on a GAU invoice leaves the settlement `open` and does not enter grace; `invoice.paid` for an `open` settlement after rollover grants to the current bucket and clears the episode; both GAU branches run with no active tenant scope under `TENANT_RLS_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED`. `syncInvoiceFromStripe` (org id from `invoice.metadata.org_id`, `stripe-provider.ts:227`) and `sendPaymentReceipt` (`webhooks.ts:159-176`) run for every paid invoice; `grantPlanCreditsForInvoicePaid` ignores GAU invoices because their `billing_reason` is `manual`, not `subscription_create`/`subscription_cycle` (`grants.ts:205-217`).

**The seams in the app.** The four reads are `kernelRead` through the `billing` port (§3.3); the two customer writes are `kernelWrite(purchaseGauBucket)` and `kernelWrite(setAutoTopup)` from `features/billing/actions.ts` (`"use server"`); the purchase action parses the returned URL with `parseCheckoutUrl` and calls `redirectToCheckout`. **Money renders only in the components INV-25 names** (`summary.tsx`, `this-month.tsx`, `invoices.tsx`, `price-list.tsx`, `change-plan.tsx`, `usage-credits.tsx`), plus the total on `purchase-form.tsx`, computed by `mulMicros(ratePerGauMicros, quantityGau)` — the one BigInt helper, in `src/data/contracts/money.ts` beside `Money`, so INV-09 stays a single arithmetic-and-formatting seam. The auto top-up control prints blocks and GAU counts only (the block price is on the rate block, two lines above). Every other billing figure is a GAU count. `billing-units.test.ts` enforces it (INV-25).

**Money precision.** `Money = { micros: string; currency: string }`; `Cost = Money & { basis: CostBasis | null }`. `<Money value precision?>`: `"cents"` (default) rounds half-even once at display (spec §12.3); `"exact"` prints up to six fractional digits trimmed to the last non-zero, at least two — the contracted rate is sub-cent (5,000 micros per GAU on every published tier; 2,000–5,000 micros across the v1 §4.1 bands, `ACTION_RATE_BANDS` in `action-metering.ts`). Counts (GAUs, blocks) go through `formatCount` in `ui/money-format.ts`, the same module (INV-09).

**The second meter: in-app AI usage credits (maintainer, 2026-09-15; §9).** The in-app agent's model calls on the platform key are metered in usage credits, 1 credit = $0.01 (`CREDIT_VALUE_USD`, `packages/billing/src/pricing.ts`). The credit gate (`evaluateTurnCreditGate`, `packages/billing/src/turn-credit-gate.ts`) admits the turn, and each call debits `ceil(provider cost × meter markup ÷ $0.01)` credits (ADR-053 §3); the markup is `resolveMeterMarkup()`, so tokens are never passed through at cost. A new org's balance starts at the $5 signup grant `create_org` writes (#3055) and is topped up with credit packs through `purchase_credits` (Stripe Checkout; the webhook grants through `grantCreditPackForCheckout`). The credit ledger, its gate and its grant are separate from the GAU bucket and the settlements: credits never buy GAUs, a GAU block never adds credits, and neither gate reads the other meter's balance. On the Billing page (§1.4, WL-67) the section reads `get_subscription.creditBalanceCents` through the `billing` port and writes `kernelWrite(purchaseCredits)` from `features/billing/actions.ts`; `features/billing/usage-credits.tsx` is the fourth component that renders money (the balance's face value and the top-up amount), and INV-25 names it.
