For `purchase_credits` this replaces the contracts table's "not bound to the app; unchanged": WL-67 declares `noBillingGate: true` on the contract (today a credit top-up is a governed action and a prepaid org at `remaining ≤ 0` is refused it) and gates Owner or Billing with `assertOrgRole` in the handler (today it checks only that a principal exists). Proven spend (#2955) is a report figure on neither meter.

---

## 4. Invariants

Each invariant is one sentence with a named mechanism. Architecture tests live in `src/test/arch/` and run in `test:unit`; they use the `typescript` package (6.0.x) compiler API — TS 7 (`@typescript/native`) exposes none — and enumerate files with `fs.readdir`/`tinyglobby`, never `fd`. When one lands while violations still exist, it carries `src/test/arch/baseline.json`; the test fails on a new violation and on a baseline entry that no longer occurs. Every lint rule has a probe file in `src/test/arch/probes/`, linted through the ESLint Node API with `ignore: false`; the probes directory is in eslint `globalIgnores`, excluded from `tsconfig.json`, and listed with `src/test/**`, `src/**/*.builders.ts` and `src/server/viewer.testing.ts` under knip's `ignore`, so `pnpm lint` (`eslint . --max-warnings 0`, `package.json:14`), `tsc` and `knip --production --strict` stay green while every probe still produces its error.

| # | Invariant | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|
| INV-01 | Every `page.tsx`, `layout.tsx` and `route.ts` whose path contains an `[org]` segment (under `src/app/[org]/**` and `src/app/api/**`) resolves its viewer with `requireViewer` or `resolveViewer`, passing every dynamic segment it has; a delegating `route.ts` satisfies it when its delegate's `deps` type requires `resolveViewer` and the delegate's unit test includes the non-member 404 negative. | Arch test `route-guard.test.ts` (TypeScript AST; follows a one-line `route.ts` to its delegate). Fixes F3 (`features/shell/load.ts:54`) and the 16 pages that never call `requireViewer`. |
| INV-02 | Only `src/server/viewer.ts` (and `viewer.testing.ts`, from tests) can construct `OrgCtx`, `WsCtx`, `PretenantCtx` or `InviteeCtx`; the compiler refuses an object literal, a spread, a cross-class assignment, `new` and a forged subclass, and the kernel core refuses at runtime every value the constructor did not brand — an `Object.assign({}, ctx, …)` copy, a `structuredClone`, a plain object. | Type: classes with an ES `#brand` private field, `declare`d fields, a protected base constructor and private leaf constructors, minted only with the `MINT` token from `viewer-mint.ts` (importable by `viewer.ts` and `viewer.testing.ts` alone); `viewer.type-test.ts` with six `@ts-expect-error` cases and one positive (§3.1), compiled by the app's `tsc --noEmit` (`@typescript/native`, `npm:typescript@7.0.2`, the `typecheck` script, `apps/app/package.json:15,65`) and by the `typescript` alias (`npm:@typescript/typescript6@6.0.2`, `:75`, the JS API the arch tests and typescript-eslint use; the workspace pins `typescript@<7` to 6.0.3 elsewhere, `pnpm-workspace.yaml:16-19`) — the pair the §9.0 probe exercised. Runtime: `OrgCtx.is` / `PretenantCtx.is` / `InviteeCtx.is` in the kernel core (§3.2 step 0) → `error("invalid_ctx", 500)`; `kernel.test.ts` negatives for the `Object.assign` copy, the spread copy, `structuredClone`, `Object.create(OrgCtx.prototype)` and a plain object; `viewer.test.ts`: instances are frozen, `Object.assign(ctx, { orgId })` throws, a forged token throws. Lint: `@typescript-eslint/consistent-type-assertions` with `assertionStyle: "never"`, and `no-restricted-syntax` on `Object.assign(` and `structuredClone(`, both under `src/` outside `viewer.ts` and `viewer.testing.ts`, a probe each. Import graph: `viewer-mint.ts` and `viewer.testing.ts` importable only as stated. |
| INV-03 | Outside `src/server/kernel.ts`, `src/` imports `@oxagen/oxagen` only as `@oxagen/oxagen/contracts/*`; `runInTenantScope`, `invoke`, `getCapability`, `CapabilityError` and every `*/register` module are reachable from `kernel.ts` alone. | The §2 allowlist in `import-graph.test.ts` (specifier-level, covers `.`, `/kernel`, `/registry`, `/types` and dynamic imports). Probe per subpath. |
| INV-04 | Every kernel result reaches callers parsed by the contract's output schema, or as `error("contract_output_mismatch", 502)`; `invalid_input` and `invalid_output` are classified explicitly. | INV-03 single path + unit tests in `server/kernel.test.ts`. |

Arch test `tenancy-lookups-tables.test.ts` walks `schema.<table>` member accesses, bans `.query.` member access and any `sql` import in that file. |

Unit tests on the three builders (`apps/api/src/middleware/auth.ts` `capabilityContext`, the MCP context builder, `apps/app/src/server/kernel.ts` step 3) assert `"platformOperator" in ctx` is false. ## 5. Fixtures and demo data

| Artifact | Purpose | Location | Shipped? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **E2E seed** | Exactly what the three tests read: one user (`owner@e2e.oxagen.test`) that can sign in, one organization (`e2e-org`) with its workspace (`core`), and one ledger run with two events (so `/{org}/{ws}/runs/{run}` has a title to load). Nothing else: no invitation (the invite route is smoked in its not-found state), no billing rows (the Free plan's published terms come from `seed.ts`). | `e2e/support/seed/index.ts` + `package.json` script `seed:e2e` | No: outside `src/`, never imported by `src/` (INV-07) |
| **Test builders** | Typed view-model and contract-output values for unit and component tests. | `src/**/*.builders.ts`, `src/server/viewer.testing.ts` | No: import-graph rule plus knip production mode |
| **Local dev data** | A separate, explicitly scoped `scripts/dev-seed.ts` that is not part of the e2e harness and has no test reader. Out of this document's scope. | — | No |

**The per-page design of record.** A lane reads `pages/<page>.md` in the Mockups repo at `origin/main`, not a line number in `mc.html`. Each file carries one page's route, every section on it, its data sources with the target store and what backs them today, its functionality, every state's copy, its mobile treatment, its permissions and its known gaps; `pages/<page>-<state>.html` and `-<state>-mobile.html` render each state; `pages/<page>.audit-prompt.md` is the per-page audit; `check-pages.mjs` verifies the set at 2,223 assertions. The generator landed with `48f5e8a`, the same commit that removed Ontology and shifted `mc.html` by 502 insertions and 900 deletions, so every `mc.html:NNNN` written before it is off by roughly −557 lines mid-file and −613 late-file. Where this document cites `mc.html:NNNN` it is at Mockups `origin/main` and is evidence for a claim, never the handoff; the handoff is the page file.

**What no longer exists:** the runtime fixture adapter (`src/data/adapters/fixture/**`, 12,942 lines), `mc_state`, `MC_DATA` (including its residue at `turbo.json:222` and `tools/scripts/env-check.ts:72`; `.gitignore:41` is the fixture suite's `**/e2e/screenshots/`, deleted with it), the fixture session (`server/fixture-session.ts:14-36`), the fixture tenancy, and the 20+ `isFixtureMode()` branches. The mockup (`mc-baseline-w1:mc.html`) is the design reference, not a runtime dependency.

**The seed writes only through package APIs.** Better Auth's sign-up API creates the user, kernel `invoke` for `create_org` creates the organization, and `@oxagen/run-ledger` `RunStore` creates the run. No raw SQL. It is idempotent (keyed on the email and the org slug) and runs in CI before Playwright; a seed failure fails the job. **The seed proves the user is sign-in-able**: after sign-up it performs a credential sign-in through Better Auth's API and fails loudly if that returns anything but a session. Sign-up leaves `emailVerified` false and Better Auth requires verification whenever the auth env is not local (`packages/auth/src/auth.ts:309` `requireEmailVerification: !isLocalEnv`; `local-env.ts:39-64`: off-Vercel, local means `NODE_ENV` development/test or `E2E_TEST === "true"`); `next start` hardcodes `NODE_ENV=production` (`pipeline.yml:334`), and the e2e job sets `VERCEL_ENV: preview` (`:335`), not `development`. The harness therefore sets **`E2E_TEST=true`** (exact string; `nightly.yml:309`'s `"1"` does not satisfy `auth.ts:63`) in the `webServer.env` of `playwright.config.ts`, as the deprecated config does today (`apps/app_deprecated/playwright.config.ts:144,159`), and `turbo.json` already passes `E2E_TEST` to `test:e2e` (`:189`). That relaxation lives in `packages/auth`, outside `apps/app`, which is what INV-22 states.

**How production is proven unreachable:** the INV-22 zero-hit arch test; the import graph forbids `src/` from importing `e2e/**`, `*.builders.ts` or `viewer.testing.ts`; knip production mode; the post-build sentinel scan, mutation-checked once by planting the sentinel in a server component; and the e2e suite runs on the production build.

---

## 6. Testing policy

### 6.1 The pyramid

| Level | What it proves | Where | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Architecture** | The invariants in §4 | `src/test/arch/*.test.ts` | Every rule has a probe that must fail it |
| **Unit** | Logic, mappers, guards, seams, actions | co-located `*.test.ts` | Every guard has a negative test (below). Assert semantic `data-*` attributes, never Tailwind classes (T13). |
| **Component** | Every rev1 section in every state: `ok`, empty, `denied`, `error`, `exhausted` and `pending_approval` where a producer exists; `NotRecorded`; axe clean per state (INV-26) | co-located `*.test.tsx`, RTL in jsdom, fake `DataSource` from builders | Query by role, text or test id (T7). |
| **Kernel** | New or changed contracts and handlers (phases D and D2) | `packages/*/src/**/*.test.ts` in the package's CI lane | A DB test no CI job runs is deleted (T5). A Postgres-backed test in `test:unit` (`gau-settlements.pg.test.ts`) is `describe.skipIf(!process.env.DATABASE_URL)`, the gate `packages/telemetry`'s `*.integration.test.ts` files use for ClickHouse, so a local narrow run without Postgres is skipped, not red, while CI's `test` job — which migrates Postgres with Atlas before `turbo run build test:unit` (`pipeline.yml:300-319`) and carries `DATABASE_URL` in turbo's `globalEnv` (`turbo.json:27`) — runs it. |
| **E2E** | Sign-in works, a customer can reach payment, every page loads with its title | `e2e/{login,pay,page-load}.spec.ts` | Exactly three files, exactly three `test()`s (INV-20, §6.3). `retries: 0`, `failOnFlakyTests`, one shard, production build. |

**Guards and their required negative tests:**

| Guard | Negative tests |
|---|---|
| `resolveViewerWith` | unauthenticated, malformed slug, unknown org, non-member, unknown or non-member workspace, MFA enroll, historical slug, each of the six stored roles resolves |
| `proxy` | no cookie → `/login?next=`; App. F table (every row, §7.3) |
| `sanitizeNext` | `//evil`, `/\evil`, `https://evil`, control characters, sign-in targets |
| `parseLoopbackUri` | non-loopback host, https, missing port, path traversal |
| `parseCheckoutUrl` | http, another host, `checkout.stripe.com.evil`, userinfo |
| `kernelRead` | each code with a Read producer in the §3.2 table, output mismatch, mutating contract refused (type and runtime), unregistered tool, `PretenantCtx` on a scoped contract refused, `InviteeCtx` refused (type test), an unbranded ctx (`Object.assign` copy, spread copy, `structuredClone`, plain object) → `invalid_ctx`, a `noBillingGate` read succeeds at `remaining = 0` (INV-28) |
| `kernelWrite` | denial → `denied`; input invalid → `invalid` with field; `gau_exhausted`, `billing_suspended`, `budget_exceeded` → `exhausted` with the code; `pending_approval` carries the id; an unbranded ctx → `unavailable` / `invalid_ctx` |
| `handleRunStream` | signed out 401, non-member 404, bad run id 400, resume without duplicates, closes on `denied` |
| GitHub setup handler | signed out → `/login?next=` keeps the installation query (T8) |
| CLI authorize action | non-loopback redirect refused; member → `denied`; owner → redirect carries code and state |
| `/` landing | no org → `/new-organization`; one org → its first workspace; a `denied` shell read → error page (unit test on the pure resolver) |
| Billing purchase action | quantity not a block multiple → `invalid` (no kernel call); Admin → `denied`; Member on the page → `denied` read; invoice-billed org → `conflict` (the form is not rendered in that mode; component test); kernel returns a non-Stripe URL → `unavailable`, no redirect; ok → `redirectToCheckout` |
| Auto top-up action and control | blocks outside 1…100 → `invalid` with field (no kernel call); Member → `denied`; Owner and Admin → ok and the control re-renders from the returned values; the control renders the saved-card line and the no-card line; hidden in invoice mode |
| Provider (`packages/billing/src/stripe-provider.ts`) | `createGauInvoice` sends `auto_advance: false` on `invoices.create` and `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice` sends `auto_advance: true` to `invoices.finalizeInvoice` (both asserted on the fake Stripe client's call arguments); `deleteOrVoidDraftInvoice`: `draft` → `invoices.del` and `deleted`; `open` → `invoices.voidInvoice` and `voided`; `paid` → `paid` with no write; `void` or `deleted` → `absent` with no write; a `resource_missing` on `del` → `absent` |
| Recorder (`packages/billing`) | one debit per call; `periodFor`: a `month` subscription gets `current_period_start/end`, a `year` subscription anchored on the 31st gets the anniversary-day slices (Jan 31–Feb 28, Feb 28–Mar 31, Mar 31–Apr 30), no subscription gets the UTC calendar month, `included_gau = included_gau_per_month` in each; the rollover carry, and the virtual bucket's carry before any row exists; prepaid at `remaining ≤ 0`: claims once under 20 concurrent recorders, the claim is committed before the fake provider is called (asserted on the query log order), `createGauInvoice` → `stripe_invoice_id` recorded → `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice`; `paid` grants `auto_topup_blocks × block_size` and clears the episode; `open` leaves the row `open`, the episode set, and a second exhaustion in the month claims nothing; `settleGauPaid` on that `open` row (the webhook) grants once and clears the episode; `settleGauPaid` on a `paid` row changes nothing; disabled or no card claims nothing; invoice mode: crossing `invoice_gau_max` claims exactly one `interim_invoice` under concurrency, quantity is exactly the max, `uninvoiced` restarts, a second crossing gets `seq + 1`, the bucket never refuses; an org with no default payment method gets a `send_invoice` invoice, ends `open` with an invoice id and no `failed` row, `list_invoices` labels it `gau_interim`, a later `invoice.paid` marks it `paid` and `pastDue` false; a provider that throws on create leaves `pending` with no invoice id, one that throws on finalize leaves `pending` with the id, and the recorder returns in both; `terms` changing mid-month prices the next settlement at the new rate; every writer receives the executor it runs on (a fake `tx` records the calls) |
| Gate (`assertGauAvailable`) | prepaid `remaining > 0` → ok; prepaid `remaining ≤ 0` → `gau_exhausted`; invoice-billed at any `remaining` → ok; suspended in either mode → `billing_suspended`; no bucket row → virtual bucket with the previous month's carry, no insert on `gau_buckets` or `org_billing_settings` (asserted on the query log: `readOrgBillingSettings`, not `getOrgBillingSettings`) |
| Close job (`billing.gau-close`, hourly) | an ended invoice-mode month with uninvoiced overage → one `period_close` settlement and `closed_at`, the customer from `ensureStripeCustomer(orgId, { system: true })`; an ended prepaid month → `closed_at` only; a month that has not ended → untouched; a `pending` `auto_topup` row whose bucket has `remaining > 0` → `failed`: with an invoice id, `deleteOrVoidDraftInvoice` is called with that id before `settleGauFailed` and is the only provider call (asserted on the fake provider's call log), and when it returns `paid` the row is settled `paid` instead; without an invoice id, no provider call; a `pending` row with no invoice older than an hour → `createGauInvoice` with the same keys; older than 24 h → `failed`, alert logged; a `pending` row with an invoice id → `finalizeAndPayGauInvoice` only; every write with no active tenant scope under `TENANT_RLS_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED`; running the job twice changes nothing |
| Webhook GAU branches | `checkout.session.completed` (`gau_purchase`): grant is an upsert on the session id, second delivery grants nothing, the card becomes the default when the customer had none, `open_topup_settlement_id` cleared; `invoice.paid` with `gauSettlementId`: `settleGauPaid` once, dunning untouched for a suspended org, an `open` row granted and the episode cleared, a row paid after rollover granted to the current bucket; `invoice.payment_failed` with `gauSettlementId`: `open`, no grace; a subscription invoice still recovers and still enters grace; both branches run with no active tenant scope under `TENANT_RLS_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` |
| `set_org_billing_terms` and `platformOnly` | invoked from a surface-shaped context → `authz_denied` before IAM; with `platformOperator: true` or a copied binding → `authz_denied` as a forged binding; from a minted binding → upsert on `withSystemDb`, `invoice_gau_max` accepted with `approved_for_invoice_billing = false` and ignored by the gate and recorder (test: an unapproved org past the max is never invoiced); switching invoice billing off with `uninvoiced > 0` → one `interim_invoice` for exactly that quantity and the bucket's `uninvoiced` at 0; switching it on → no settlement |
| Contract-rate component | prints the negotiated row's rate for a negotiated org and the plan's rate for a published-tier org (the mapper test proves neither comes from `ACTION_RATE_BANDS`) |
| Approval action and dialog | deny without a reason → `invalid`; member → `denied`; `gau_exhausted`, `billing_suspended`, `budget_exceeded` → `exhausted` rendered with the copy for the code and the billing link; `conflict` (`approval_expired`, from the handler's `HandlerError`) → `conflict`; handler test: an unknown or already-resolved id throws and a fake recorder records no debit |
| Each other action | invalid, denied, ok (and `conflict` where the handler can refuse: last owner, expired approval) |

### 6.2 What moves down from today's e2e

The 102 `test()` calls (129 after loop expansion) in 9 specs are fixture-mode (`playwright.config.ts:41`) and are deleted with their support directory, `@axe-core/playwright` and the `MC_DATA` webServer env (WL-03). The 53 specs and the config under `apps/app_deprecated/e2e` run nowhere today (`apps/app_deprecated/package.json:10-18` has no `test:e2e` script) and exist only as the files `check_manifest.mjs:206` resolves for the `e2e` layer of 32 contracts; they are deleted in WL-50 with that layer. State checks already have component tests (`features/shell/shell-client.test.tsx:279-565`, `features/auth/forms.test.tsx:66-450`, `views.test.tsx:92-158`, `server/stream-route.test.ts:78-320`); they are kept where their subject survives.

| Revision 1 journey | Now proved by |
|---|---|
| J01 sign-in + safe next | `login.spec.ts` (sign-in and the gate) + `sanitizeNext` unit tests |
| J02 sign up + create org | action unit test for `create_org`; handler DB test (WL-16) |
| J03 two-factor | `resolveViewerWith` MFA negative; forms component tests |
| J04 invitation | `requireInvitee` unit test; invite action tests; invite page component tests (found, not found, expired, wrong email) |
| J05 CLI authorize | CLI action tests; `parseLoopbackUri` tests; `authorize_cli` handler test |
| J06 tenancy 404s | `viewer-resolution.test.ts`, `stream.test.ts` (INV-15) |
| J07 Fleet → Run + SSE | `list_runs`/`get_run` handler tests; `stream.test.ts`; Fleet and Run component tests |
| J08–J10 approve, deny, halt | action tests per path; `resolve_approval` handler test (WL-14); approval dialog component test incl. `exhausted` |
| J11 write denial | action tests: member → `denied`; dialog component tests in the `denied` state |
| J12–J13 members, API keys | action tests; `list_members`/`list_api_keys` handler tests; secret-shown-once component test |
| J14 axe + 400 px + 308s | INV-26 axe in component tests; `MobileNav` component test at a 400 px container; `proxy.test.ts` over the App. F table |

### 6.3 The e2e suite: three files, three tests

The Playwright config has three projects: `login` (`testMatch: login.spec.ts`), then `page-load` and `pay` with `dependencies: ["login"]` and `storageState: e2e/.auth/owner.json`, which `login.spec.ts` writes after a successful sign-in. The web server is `next start` on the build the job's `Build` step already produced (`pnpm turbo run build … --filter=!@oxagen/app-deprecated`, `pipeline.yml` ~:487-506): the `webServer.command` is `pnpm --filter @oxagen/app start`, so `apps/app` is built once, and the INV-22 sentinel scan reads that same `.next`. `webServer.env` lists, in addition to the turbo `test:e2e` env list (`turbo.json:185-224`, which Playwright inherits from the runner's `process.env` and which carries the `CLICKHOUSE_*`, `NEO4J_*`, `INNGEST_*` names `instrumentation.ts`'s bootstraps read), exactly what the production build needs to boot and sign in, so the harness is reproducible locally with the same seed: `VERCEL_ENV=preview` (the boot signal, `pipeline.yml:334-340`), `E2E_TEST=true` (§5), `DATABASE_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `STRIPE_E2E`; no model-provider secret (login, pay and page-load call no model; `pipeline.yml:381-383` drops them once WL-46 shows `next start` boots without them). `apps/app/package.json:19`'s `--pass-with-no-tests` is removed in WL-47 once the three specs exist, so an empty match fails.

| File | The one test | Asserts |
|---|---|---|
| `login.spec.ts` | an anonymous visit to `/{org}/{ws}` redirects to `/login?next=/{org}/{ws}`; signing in as the seeded owner lands on `/{org}/{ws}` (URL only); the context's storage state is saved | the proxy gate, a Better Auth session, `next` preserved |
| `page-load.spec.ts` | `test("every rev1 page loads with its title", …)` iterating `REV1_ROUTES` with `test.step(r.path, …)` per entry — the maintainer's "basic page load test" is singular, and one test with 19 steps at WL-33 (one per `REV1_ROUTES` entry; a gap lane that adds a route adds a step, by the stated amount in its §1.2 row: #2956 +2, #2957 +1, #2964 +1, #2967 +1, #3097 +1, #3098 +1, every other lane +0, so the suite ends at 26 steps) reports each route's failure by name; anonymous entries use a fresh context, owner entries the saved state; each step visits `r.path` with `r.query` and asserts either `document.title === "<title> · Oxagen"` and the `h1` equals `<title>` (read from `messages/en.json` at `r.expect.title`) or, for `/`, that the response landed on `r.expect.redirectTo` | every rev1 page renders with the right name for a real session; INV-20 keeps `REV1_ROUTES` equal to the route files |
| `pay.spec.ts` | on `/{org}/billing` the owner reads the block size from the rate block's `data-block-size` attribute (§1.4), picks 2 blocks in the GAU quantity picker and submits; the browser is sent to a `https://checkout.stripe.com/…` URL (stubbed with `page.route`; the `cs_test_…` id read from the path); the spec then calls the Stripe test API with the job's key — `GET /v1/checkout/sessions/{id}/line_items` shows one line with `quantity === 2`; `GET /v1/checkout/sessions/{id}` shows `payment_status === "unpaid"`, `metadata.gau_quantity === String(2 × blockSize)` and `invoice_creation.enabled === true` (the cheapest proof the purchase will surface in Invoices) — and finally `POST /v1/checkout/sessions/{id}/expire`; no payment is completed and the page's rate figure is not asserted here (that is the contract-rate component test) | buying a GAU bucket reaches Stripe test-mode Checkout at the right quantity with no spend, and will be invoiced |

**How `pay` runs in CI without real spend, fail-closed.** The pipeline's `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY: sk_test_ci` (`pipeline.yml:83`) cannot create a session, and `stripeClient()` has no host override for stripe-mock (`stripe-provider.ts:130-139`). The e2e job reads a repository secret `STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY` (a test-mode key from the Oxagen Stripe account; test mode never charges) into `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` and sets `STRIPE_E2E=1` when it resolved non-empty; **`STRIPE_E2E` is added to `turbo.json`'s `test:e2e` `env` list** (`turbo.json:181-224` lists `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` but not `STRIPE_E2E`, and turbo strips unlisted workflow env before the task starts — CLAUDE.md:310: "Always update `pipeline.yml` AND `turbo.json` together"). The rule is fail-closed: on a push to `main` and on a same-repository pull request, a missing key **fails the job** with a named step (`Stripe test key present`); only a fork pull request (`github.event.pull_request.head.repo.fork`) may set `STRIPE_E2E=0`, in which case `pay.spec.ts` calls `test.skip(true, "fork PR: no Stripe test key")` and the job summary annotates it. A mutation check is recorded once: unset the secret on a same-repo branch → the job fails, never a green skip. The e2e job is excluded from `workflow_dispatch` (`pipeline.yml:326-327`), and this design does not add it. `nightly.yml:344` also runs `pnpm test:e2e`, under `E2E_TEST: "1"` (`:309`) and no Stripe key; WL-48 gives it the same `Stripe test key present` step and WL-46 sets `E2E_TEST: "true"` there, so the nightly `pay` run is fail-closed too rather than running against the placeholder key. The GAU checkout uses `price_data`, so no Stripe Price is provisioned and the seeded plan's placeholder price ids (`seed.ts:35-37`) are never sent; the customer is created once and persisted on `org_billing_settings.stripe_customer_id`, so repeated CI runs do not mint duplicate customers. stripe-mock is not used: it answers from OpenAPI fixtures, so the quantity assertion would test the fixture.

No e2e coverage report exists; coverage is the vitest 90% ratchet (INV-24).

### 7.3 Legacy redirects (Appendix F)

`src/shared/legacy-routes.ts` holds the table as data; `proxy.ts` applies it with 308; `proxy.test.ts` asserts every row's target is a §1.2 route. Two rows the plan (`:918`) and spec (`:2276`) omit are added for the shipped deprecated routes: `/{org}/billing/governed-actions` → `/{org}/billing` and `/{org}/billing/usage` → `/{org}/billing`. The rows the spec's Appendix F sends to Ontology retarget: legacy `/{org}/{ws}/knowledge/**` (`knowledge`, `knowledge/graph`, `knowledge/graph/[nodeId]`, `knowledge/ontology`, `knowledge/sources`, `knowledge/sources/connect`) and `/{org}/{ws}/settings/github` redirect to `/{org}/{ws}` (Fleet); `knowledge/memory` → `/{org}/{ws}/steering` is unchanged. The rows Appendix F sends to Audit split (decision log, 2026-09-15): `/{org}/security/audit` lands on the Audit page, `/{org}/audit`, and `/{org}/security`, `security/compliance`, `security/mfa`, `security/trust`, `governance` and `access` land on `/{org}` (Organization › People), none of them having a rev1 page of its own. No row sits under `/{org}/audit/**`: the page is a §1.2 route and its signed export is a `route.ts` beneath it, so a catch-all there would swallow the download. No row targets `/{org}/{ws}/ontology`. This table is the one source `proxy.test.ts` asserts against: the spec's own Appendix F prose still sends the Ontology and Audit rows to pages that do not ship and omits the two billing rows, and where it and this section disagree this document wins (§9, and the Status block's rule).

## 8. Worklist summary

Each item is one PR into `app-rebuild`. Phases run A → B → C → E → F → G → H in the app, with D and D2 (packages only, no app dependency) running alongside from the start; a C item waits on the D items it names (WL-11 on WL-19 and WL-22, WL-12 on WL-21); within D and D2, WL-21 waits on WL-22 (the `HandlerError` class it throws), and WL-14 and WL-25–WL-30 wait on WL-21 and WL-22 (the `assertOrgRole` helper, the `@oxagen/iam` dependency `packages/handlers` gains for it, and the class) and E/F items on the D/D2 contracts they bind; items in a phase may run in parallel where `dependsOn` allows. Every item's acceptance includes `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` on `apps/app` passing, the affected routes rendering (CLAUDE.md:44), and `pnpm check:ui-parity --strict` passing with `APP_DIR` unchanged: no PR before WL-50 adds `"app"` to a contract's `layers`, because the strict forward gate reads `apps/app_deprecated/capability-ui-map.json` until `APP_DIR` flips (`tools/scripts/lib/app-dir.mjs:11`, `check_ui_parity.mjs:51-53,175-181,201,361`) and forbids growing the baseline. Between phase B (adapters deleted) and phase E (pages built), Fleet, Run, People, API keys and Billing render `<PageHeader>` with their title and no body, so "every remaining route renders" is checkable and `UNRECORDED` keeps its five rows until the gap lanes start deleting them. Acceptance criteria are in the structured worklist.

**Gap lanes.** The kept gap issues are built as lanes, one issue per lane, in the order the review sets: witness first (#2955 — proof/witness tab on Run: the flip, verdicts, proven spend), then #2952 (recorder and replay), #2953 (run controls), #2956 with #2957 (Agents: identity, credential broker, roles, toolbelt, mandates), #2958 (Tools: registry, connections, kill switches, auto-approval rules), #2961 (Steering: records → proposals → Context PR), #2962 (the cost rollup: `get_spend`, `cost.price_entries`, `cost.run_totals`, `get_run_cost` — the contracts Spend's tabs and drill, Fleet's spend and cache tiles and Run's Cost tab all read), #2963 (Spend: attribution and findings), #2964 (Organization: people, roles, invitations, workspaces, API keys), #2967 (onboarding gate), #2968 (shell: the assistant flyout on `stella-serve` per ADR-053, notifications, ⌘K), #2970 (auto-approval rules: `set_approval_rules`, `get_auto_eligibility`, the eligibility line on every approval card and the Tools › Auto-approvals card list), #3098 (Skills: resolution over `.oxagen/skills.toml` — catalog, `search_skills`, the interjection seat, quarantined reflection and the config's version history — plus the observed-inventory read `list_skills`; ADR-090, decision log 2026-09-18; depends on #3241 for the repository binding the config is read through). **Audit is a lane again** (#3097, decision log 2026-09-15): the rev1 subset of the design #2966 closed — the deprecated events viewer and its signed export, ported onto the new skin. It is one PR rather than two, because its backend half wires a contract that exists (`query_audit_log`) and moves the deprecated export behind one (`export_audit_events`), building no store, no table and no job.Each lane is two PRs into `app-rebuild`: the backend half (contract → handler → API route → MCP tool, in the package lanes, with no app dependency, so it may start now) and the app half (port method, view model, mapper, section, component tests, `UNRECORDED` row deleted, any new route with its `REV1_ROUTES` entry and title key), which lands after WL-33 and before WL-50, so the cutover PR adds the lane's `"app"` layers and `capability-ui-map.json` bindings with the rest. A lane obeys §1.1(b): its app half binds only contracts its backend half registered. The thinned issues carry a "Scope note" section (2026-09-14) that is the lane's scope; the closed issues (#2954, #2959, #2960, #2966, #2969) have no lane.

**#2962 and #2970 are lanes, not #2963's and #2958's scope.** Both were open `kind:gap` issues that appeared in no lane list while §1.2 attributed their contracts to other issues, and the cost of leaving it there is visible: the approval card ships with no eligibility line and Run ships with no Cost tab, with nothing in the tree recording that as a decision. #2962 owns the cost rollup because #2963's kept half is attribution and costed findings, which read the rollup rather than build it, and because #2963's reconciliation half is cut. #2970 owns the auto-approval rules because #2958's kept scope is the registry, connections, credential grants and kill switches. #2962 is ordered after #2955 and #2952 (the run record its per-run cost reads) and before #2963; #2970 is ordered after #2958.

**The `pBilling` exception, closed 2026-09-18.** Billing was the one rev1 page whose design was this document and not the mockup, because `pBilling` drew the superseded per-run model. `pages/billing.md` has since been rewritten for GAUs and usage credits, and the maintainer asked for the page to match it. The page now follows its layout: header and Change plan, four tiles, then This month, Meters and Invoices beside the price list, Auto top-up, the purchase form, Usage credits and What counts. §1.4 and §3.9 still own the billing model; the page spec owns the arrangement. Two differences remain, and each is the record's limit: Invoices shows what each invoice charged for, since no row records a GAU quantity, and This month prints tax as not recorded, since invoice rows carry no tax line.

## 9. Decision log

**2026-09-19: A mandate with no approval trigger says so, instead of naming its approvers.** The gate fills `ruleIds` from a consequence tag matching `alwaysHumanFor` or a `humanAbove` threshold that is exceeded, and proceeds when that list is empty (`packages/rules/src/mandates.ts`). The contract defaults both collections to empty. The detail panel rendered the approvers line unconditionally, so on a default mandate the Approval row said who answers when nothing would ever be routed to anyone. **This is the same claim the product page was corrected for two rounds earlier, and it is worse here.** A marketing page overstating human review misleads a prospect; the mandate record overstating it misleads the operator who reads that page to learn what the mandate does, and it does so in the reassuring direction: an authority with no brake looks like one with a brake. The row now renders a no-trigger state naming what is absent and what would add it, and the approvers line only where a trigger exists. The two are exclusive rather than the approvers line merely being hidden, because an approver list beside "nothing waits for a person" is the same claim in two minds. Applied: `features/mandate/grant.tsx`, `features/mandate/mandate.test.tsx`, `messages/mandate.json`, `src/i18n/messages.d.ts`.

**2026-09-19: A day-shaped value is not a day, and the check that knew that lived in the wrong place.** `changeMandateLimits` validated `validTo` with `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` and then converted it. `2027-02-31` passes that pattern, and `Date.UTC(2027, 1, 31)` rolls forward to 3 March without complaint, so a caller sending a date that does not exist got a window three days longer than any real date could have named. A server action is reachable by anyone holding a session, so the shape check was the only thing between a manipulated request and extra authority. **`features/audit/filters.ts` already had the fix**, as a module-private `isDay` whose comment names `2026-02-31` and the 3 March roll-forward by name. Two surfaces took a day from a caller; one of them knew the trap and the other did not. The check is now `isCalendarDay` in `shared/calendar-day.ts`, beside the conversions that depend on it, and `filters.ts` delegates to it: one implementation, two callers, and the third copy of the regex is gone. Seventeen cases pin it, including that 2027 is not a leap year and `2027-02-29` is refused while `2028-02-29` is taken. **Two claims in the same review, both wider than their authority.** The product page promised "The agent never sees the key" in five places; `positioning.md` limits that to mediated connections and says in terms never to promise the agent has nothing to leak, because it still holds its own identity. All five now name the connection credential. And the denial state told a member that a mandate is readable only through `org.billing`, when `readerFilter` admits Owner, Admin, Billing or Compliance and, failing those, lets anyone read the mandates of agents they created. That copy sent a denied reader to ask for the most privileged finance role when a less privileged one, or nothing at all, would have done. Applied: `shared/calendar-day.ts` and its test, `features/mandate/actions.ts` and its test, `features/audit/filters.ts`, `messages/mandate.json`, `apps/web/products/oxagen/index.html`.

**2026-09-19: A target rule with no allow pattern reads as unrestricted, because that is what enforcement does with it.** `targetAllowed` (`packages/rules/src/mandates/measures.ts`) tries the allow patterns, then the deny patterns, and its last line is `return rule.allow.length === 0`. So an empty allow list permits every target the deny list does not name. The panel rendered both empty lists through one string and showed "allow no pattern, deny no pattern", which reads as a mandate that permits nothing where enforcement permits everything. **The direction is what makes this worth fixing rather than carrying.** Every other finding of this family on this PR errs toward showing more authority than was granted; this one errs toward showing less, which is the reassuring misreading, and an auditor reading a mandate as tightly scoped when it is unrestricted is the failure an authority record exists to prevent. The allow line now says "any target" when neither list has a pattern, "anything not denied" when only a deny pattern is recorded, and the patterns themselves otherwise. The deny line keeps "no pattern", which is true: nothing is denied. **One string changed that the finding did not name.** `anyTarget`, used when a mandate records no target rule at all, said "no target rule" — a fact that leaves the reader to infer what it means, and the same gap one level up. It now says "no target rule, so any target". That is a deliberate change rather than a side effect: the rule-level case took a new key. Three cases in `mandate.test.tsx` pin all of it. Applied: `features/mandate/grant.tsx`, `features/mandate/mandate.test.tsx`, `messages/mandate.json`, `src/i18n/messages.d.ts`.

**2026-09-19: The mandate lane's three components take the app's formatter, because next-intl's own reads the request default on the server.** `@/ui/formatter` exists for one reason: a server component is formatted with the request config, which is read while the static shell prerenders and carries only the default zone, so `<ViewerClock>` writes the viewer's zone into a per-request slot and that wrapper formats in it. `mandate.tsx`, `grant.tsx` and `ledger.tsx` imported `useFormatter` straight from `next-intl`, so the validity window, the read-failure instant and every ledger timestamp rendered in Pacific for every viewer whose zone is not Pacific. Ten other features in this app already take the wrapper; these three were written without noticing it. **This correction matters beyond its P2 badge, because it falsifies what the entries above it assert.** Two of them, and two review replies, say the detail panel renders the stored instant in the reader's zone, and use that as half the argument for resolving a picked day in the viewer's zone. It did not. Worse, the two halves were wrong in opposite directions and compounded: with the write fixed and the display not, an operator in Tokyo who picked 31 December had `2026-12-31T14:59:59.999Z` written correctly and then displayed in Pacific as 30 December, so the page showed a day *earlier* than the one they chose. The write was right and the record said so; the surface disagreed with both. A swap of three imports, and the reason the wrapper is a drop-in with the same return type is so that this is all it ever takes. Applied: `features/mandate/{mandate,grant,ledger}.tsx`.

**2026-09-19: A zone that cannot be established refuses the write; it does not fall back.** The ninth finding of one shape on this PR, *a surface that can store a wider bound than the operator entered*, and this one was mine: the entry below this one introduced it and argued for it in the commit message. Both mandate writes resolved a picked day in the viewer's zone and fell back to Pacific when `get_user_preferences` did not answer, or when the stored name was one this runtime cannot format in. The argument was that the pages fall back the same way, so the window would at least agree with the dates beside it. **That reasoning holds for drawing a date and fails for writing a boundary.** A date rendered in the wrong zone is a cosmetic error a reader can see. For an operator in Tokyo, Pacific moves the end of their 31 January from `14:59:59.999Z` to the next day's `07:59:59.999Z`: seventeen hours of authority nobody granted, with nothing on screen afterwards saying a zone was guessed. Every zone east of the default widens, and the asymmetry rule this lane has now applied nine times decides it: when being wrong is not symmetric, take the side that cannot grant more than was asked for. A refusal is visible and retryable; the widened window was neither. **Decision: `server/viewer-zone.ts` answers the zone or the refusal, and both writes take it.** A failed or denied read gives `unavailable` / `time_zone_unavailable`, which is retryable and says so. A stored zone this runtime cannot format in gives `conflict` / `time_zone_unsupported`, because retrying cannot help until the person stores a zone this runtime knows, and its sentence sends them to their account settings. Both have real sentences in `messages/{mandate,agents}.json` rather than rendering a bare code through the generic fallback, since a person reading a refusal on a page of financial authority is owed the reason. **One module, not one per lane.** The grant path and the change-limits path each had their own copy of the read and the fallback, and the same decision expressed twice is how they come to disagree about what a picked day means. `viewerTimeZone` is the only place either asks. **The zone is still read only when a day was submitted,** so a limit-only change makes one kernel call and is not held up by a preference read it never makes. **Two sessions fixed this at once, and the merge kept parts of both.** A parallel session landed `054888f` against the same finding while this was in flight. It refused the failed read and left the unsupported stored zone falling back to Pacific, and it kept the read in each lane rather than sharing it. This resolution keeps the shared module and the second refusal, and takes two things from that commit: the `time_zone_*` spelling of the codes, which `data/live/shell.ts` was already using, and a correction worth more than either implementation. **That commit found that the mandate test fake answered `get_user_preferences` with a `timezone` and nothing else.** The kernel validates a contract's output, so every one of those reads had been failing, and each case that asserted a Pacific instant was passing through the fallback rather than through the zone it named. The fake now returns the whole output, which is what makes these cases evidence at all. Applied: `server/viewer-zone.ts` and its test (new), `features/mandate/actions.ts`, `features/agents/actions.ts`, both lanes' `action-failure.ts`, `messages/mandate.json`, `messages/agents.json`, `src/i18n/messages.d.ts`, and both actions' tests, where the two fallback cases are now the two refusals.

**2026-09-19: The change-limits dialog prefills from either bound, because a limit may name only its per-call cap.** `mandateLimitSchema` requires a limit to name `perCall`, `perPeriod` or both, so a bound that caps a single call and leaves the period open is a valid record. `measureDefaults` selected a measure by `perPeriod?.kind === "count"` alone, so on such a mandate the dialog opened with a blank measure, unit and per-call field while the mandate plainly held a bound, and an operator wanting to lower that cap had to retype the measure and the unit from the page behind the dialog before they could. It now selects on either bound being a count, and takes the unit from whichever bound carries it, so the per-period field stays empty and every other field is the record's. Applied: `features/mandate/mandate-actions.tsx`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.test.tsx`.

**2026-09-19: A draft mandate can be declined in the app, and four public claims now carry the scope the record can back.** Five findings from one review, all of the same family: a surface saying something the system does not do. **(a) The decline path did not exist.** `revoke_mandate` accepts `draft` as well as `active` — declining a request is the revocation of a mandate that never took effect, and the handler says so — but `MandateActions` returned `null` for anything but `active`, and its comment claimed the decline lived on the agent's page. It does not: `revokeMandate` has exactly one call site in the app, this component. So a requested mandate could be granted and never declined from any screen, which is a capability the API has and the app does not, the gap this repo weighs as heavily as a missing route. Revoke is now offered for `draft` and `active`, Change limits for `active` alone. **The dialog's copy could not simply be reused:** it says the mandate ends now, that reservations held by undispatched calls are released, and that the ledger keeps every movement recorded. Of a draft, all three are false, because nothing was ever reserved and no movement exists. A draft gets its own words under `mandate.actions.revoke.draft`, and the verb is Decline, which is what the handler's own comment calls it. **(b) The revocation reason did not reach Audit.** The hint said it was recorded on the mandate and in the audit record. The handler stores it on `mandates.revoked_reason` and emits `mandate.revoked` carrying event type, actor, tenant, capability, outcome and request id, with no field for a reason. An operator was told their rationale would appear somewhere it cannot. The hint now says the audit record shows that it was revoked, not why. Carrying the reason into an auditable shape is the better answer and is the audit lane's object, not this one's. **(c) to (e) Three public claims wider than the record.** The product page's metadata said Oxagen prices *every* model call and answers *every* agent request with a rule you wrote. ADR-095 requires a control claim to carry "for actions routed through Oxagen", harness traffic is client-attested, and `gateway` for a wrapped harness has not shipped; the rules layer also answers from identity when no rule matches. The metering claims now name routed traffic in all three places they appear, and the rule sentence no longer quantifies over every request. And the retired line "Never re-explain yourself to AI ever again" had reappeared as a headline on both public pages, four occurrences, with "Taught once, known by every agent you run" beside it making the same promise in other words; the registry retires that line for promising universal recall when retrieval is scoped, and names the replacement. All five are the equipment section, which now leads with the approved line. Applied: `features/mandate/mandate-actions.tsx`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.test.tsx`, `features/mandate/mandate.test.tsx` (a comment that repeated the same wrong belief about drafts), `messages/mandate.json`, `src/i18n/messages.d.ts`, and in `apps/web` the two `index.html` pages.

**2026-09-19: The end of a change-limits validity window is the viewer's day, through the one seam that already knew how (#3337).** The eighth finding of one shape on this PR, *a surface that can store a wider bound than the operator entered*, and the first where the widening is silent in one direction and a silent narrowing in the other. `changeMandateLimits` appended `T23:59:59.999Z` to the day a `<input type="date">` gave it. That is the right instant only for an operator already on UTC: at UTC+9, picking 31 December authorised the agent until 08:59 on 1 January in their own calendar, nine hours nobody granted, and at UTC-8 it cut eight hours off the day they chose. Neither is visible on the form, and the detail panel renders the stored instant in the viewer's zone, so the page then displayed a boundary contradicting the day the operator had picked. **Decision: resolve the day through `shared/calendar-day.ts` with the viewer's stored zone, exactly as the grant path does.** `endOfPickedDay` reads `get_user_preferences` through the kernel seam and calls `endOfZonedDay(day, timeZone)` — the same three lines as `features/agents/actions.ts`, which grants the mandate this dialog later changes. **Consistency with grant is the deciding argument, not tidiness:** if these two paths resolved a day differently, granting a window and then changing it would write different instants for the same picked date, and no reading of the record would reveal which path produced it. **The first attempt got this wrong in an instructive way** and is recorded because the branch carries it: it added a `validToOffsetMinutes` field, took `getTimezoneOffset()` in the browser for the day after the picked one, and composed the instant from that. It was correct about the defect and about DST, and it was a second implementation of a module that already existed and was better — `calendar-day.ts` resolves IANA names through `Intl` rather than a single offset, and handles a DST gap that skips local midnight, which an offset cannot express. Its own doc comment names mandates as a caller. **The rule that catches this: before writing a date helper in a feature, look in `shared/` for the one that already exists** — `#3337` added that module on `main` and the duplicate was written against a tree that had not yet merged it. **Two properties, each pinned by a test.** The preference is read only when a day was actually submitted, so a limit-only change still makes exactly one kernel call. And a failed preference read, or a stored zone this runtime cannot format in, falls back to Pacific — the same fallback the pages draw dates in, because a window disagreeing with the dates beside it is worse than one in a zone the operator did not pick. **What the zone read is not.** The assertion this lane cares about is that the action never reads *the mandate*: that read was the stale snapshot ADR-102 removed. A zone preference is not a snapshot of any bound this write could restore, so "exactly one kernel call" was narrowed to "exactly one write, and no `get_mandate`" rather than dropped. **Still not settled:** `endOfZonedDay` returns the day's last millisecond while validity is half-open, `[validFrom, validTo)`, so that final millisecond is excluded and a window is one millisecond short of the day it names. It is wrong identically in the grant path, which is why it was not changed on one side only; fixing it means `startOfNextZonedDay` in both, in a lane that owns both. Applied: `features/mandate/actions.ts`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.tsx`, `features/mandate/actions.test.ts`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.test.tsx`, `messages/mandate.json`.

**2026-09-19: The change-limits patch is sparse, because an atomic merge cannot tell an edit from an echo (ADR-102, amended).** The seventh finding of one shape on this PR, *a surface that can store a wider bound than the operator entered*, and the same class as the entry below this one found one layer up. That entry moved the merge into the handler, under the row lock, so two operators editing different bounds could not overwrite each other. It was right about where the merge belongs and incomplete about what the client sends. `measureDefaults` prefills the measure, the unit, the window and both figures from the mandate the page read, and `changeMandateLimits` turned everything the form submitted into the change. So an operator who moved only the validity date, or only the calls cap, still asserted the count bound the dialog had read minutes earlier, and the handler applied it: every field a change carries is an explicit edit, which is the only reading a change has. On a mandate a second operator had lowered in between, the old wider bound came back through the locked merge rather than around it. **Decision: the change carries a field only when the operator changed it.** The dialog emits each prefilled value as a hidden field beside the field it fills (`baselineMeasure`, `baselineUnit`, `baselinePerCall`, `baselinePerPeriod`, `baselinePeriod`, `baselineCallsPerDay`), both seeded from the same `measureDefaults` result in the same render, and the action carries a field into `limitChanges` only where the two differ. Both come from one call and both are set through `defaultValue`, so the pair is seeded together or not at all: a baseline cannot describe a default the operator never saw, and a later render with a fresher mandate moves neither. Reopening the dialog unmounts the form and reads both again. **Why the baseline is read from the form, not from the closure.** `defaults` is recomputed on every render while `defaultValue` seeds the DOM once, so comparing against the closure would compare the operator's value to a prefill they were never shown. The hidden field is the same string the visible field started with, by construction. **Not a version token.** ADR-102 weighed one and rejected it: a sparse change holds no snapshot, so there is nothing stale to detect and no caller needs a retry loop. This is that decision applied to the client. **Three consequences, each pinned by a test.** A submission that changed only the window sends no `limitChanges` and is still one legal call. A cleared field and an untouched field produce the same patch, because neither says to change the stored bound, and clearing is not deletion. A submission that changed nothing at all is refused here naming `perPeriod`, rather than reaching the kernel as a schema failure that names no field. Validation still runs on every value the form carries, prefills included, because a server action is reachable by anyone holding a session. Applied: `features/mandate/actions.ts`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.tsx`, `features/mandate/actions.test.ts`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.test.tsx` (new), `docs/adr/ADR-102-a-limit-change-merges-under-the-mandates-row-lock.md` (amendment).

**2026-09-19 — The limit merge moved into the handler, under the row lock, because a read-merge-replace round trip can restore a bound somebody lowered (ADR-102).** The sixth finding of one shape on this PR — *a surface that can store a wider bound than the operator entered* — and the first one about two operators rather than one form. The entry below this one fixed a deleted bound by having `changeMandateLimits` read the mandate and lay the edit over the stored `limits`. That read was correct about where the untouched bounds come from and wrong about when: `update_mandate_limits` locks the row for its own write (`lockMandate`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) and nothing held a lock across the app's read, so two operators editing one mandate both read the same record and both submit complete replacements. One lowers the amount cap, the other changes the calls cap, and the second write puts the old amount cap back from a snapshot taken before the first write landed. Nothing errors, nobody typed it, and remaining authority keeps reading correctly against the restored, wider limit. **Decision: `update_mandate_limits` takes `limitChanges` beside `limits`, and the merge happens in the handler inside the locking transaction.** `limitChanges` is keyed by measure and carries partial bounds; the two-depth rule the app used to run is now `applyLimitChanges` over `locked.limits` — every measure the change does not name keeps its bound, and within a named measure every field it does not carry keeps its stored value, `period` included. The rule moved; it was not duplicated. The two inputs are mutually exclusive and the contract refuses both, because both states two intentions for one field. **Replacement did not change.** An API, MCP, CLI or agent caller sending `limits` gets the semantics it always had, which is the only way to delete a bound and the right primitive for a caller holding the whole record — so the reason the entry below gives for not fixing this in the handler still stands, and this change does not contradict it: the handler gained a second, additive input rather than new behaviour for the old one. **What the app lost:** the `get_mandate` read, the refusal mapping that read needed (`fromRead`), the `StoredLimits`/`StoredMeasure` plumbing and the type assertion in the merge. The action now makes exactly one kernel call and holds no snapshot. The dialog's copy is unchanged and its promise is now kept under the lock: a blank field leaves that bound as it is, and removing a bound altogether is a whole-record `limits` write over the API. **What is still last-write-wins:** two operators changing the *same* figure, in lock order, which is what one figure with two authors means. Neither of them restores a third bound. Applied: `packages/oxagen/src/mandates/schemas.ts`, `packages/oxagen/src/contracts/mandate.limits.update.ts`, `packages/handlers/src/mandate.limits.update.ts` (+ its new unit test and the Postgres suite's lost-update case), `features/mandate/actions.ts`, `features/mandate/actions.test.ts`, `docs/capabilities/update_mandate_limits.md`.

**2026-09-19 — The change-limits form merges over the stored record, because `update_mandate_limits` replaces it (#2957 app half).** The third finding on this lane of the one shape §9 already records twice — *a form that can store a wider bound than the operator entered* — and the first one where the widening comes from a field the form never showed. `update_mandate_limits` **sets** `limits` to what its input carries (`packages/handlers/src/mandate.limits.update.ts`: `const limits = input.limits ?? locked.limits`, then `.set({ limits })`), so a submission naming one measure deletes every other measure's bound, and a deleted bound is *unbounded* authority for that measure at the next reservation. The dialog exposes one count measure and the built-in `calls` cap, so on a mandate holding an amount limit as well — which is the demo record and the common case, since the form cannot express a money limit at all (the 2026-09-16 entry) — changing the calls cap would have removed the money bound. Nobody types that, nothing on screen says it, and the ledger's own figures keep reading correctly afterwards because remaining authority is computed against a limit that is now absent. **Decision: `changeMandateLimits` reads the mandate and lays the edit over the stored `limits`.** Read through the kernel seam (`kernelRead` with `mandateGet`, `ledgerLimit: 1`), not carried up from the page: the dialog holds the mandate's `authority` and could reconstruct the record from it digit for digit, but then the bounds resubmitted would be the ones a client sent, and the entire point of the call is that the untouched ones are the ones the record holds. A read that does not answer stops the write rather than falling back to the edit alone, and its refusal is reported with the handler's own reason — `kernelRead` collapses a denial to the page's permission, and `get_mandate` has exactly one `forbidden` reason and `loadMandateRow` exactly one `not_found` reason, so both can be named from this side without guessing. **The consequence a person has to be told, and is:** a blank field now leaves that measure's bound as it is rather than removing it. Removing a limit means sending a `limits` record without it, which is `update_mandate_limits` over the API or MCP; a form whose blank fields deleted bounds would delete them by accident far more often than on purpose, and the deletion is the direction that grants authority. **Why not fixed in the handler.** Merging per measure there would change the semantics on the API and MCP too and would remove the only way to delete a measure's limit at all — replacement is a correct primitive for a caller holding the whole record, which is what those surfaces have and what a form does not. The durable improvement is the same one the 2026-09-16 entry names: `measures` on `list_tool_declarations`' output, which would let the dialog edit the complete record because it would know each measure's type, unit and scale. Applied: `features/mandate/actions.ts`, `features/mandate/mandate-actions.tsx`, `messages/mandate.json`, `features/mandate/actions.test.ts`.

**2026-09-19 — The mandate detail route is flat, and its ledger line claims only what the read establishes (#2957 app half).** Two corrections landing with the page. **(a) The route.** The mockup's route nests the mandate under its agent (`…/agents/invoice-bot/mandates/<id>`) and §1.2 has always stated the flat one, `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}`; the page shipped nested first and is now flat. §1.2 is right on the merits, not only by authority: a mandate's public id identifies it inside the workspace on its own and `get_mandate` takes that id alone, so an agent segment above it is a second name for the same record that nothing checks — a link carrying the wrong agent opened the right mandate, and the page never read the segment. The agent comes from the record instead: the header links to the agent the mandate was granted to, which is the one place that knows it. **(b) The ledger's read line.** `get_mandate` takes a `ledgerLimit` and answers no total, no has-more flag and no cursor, so a ledger of exactly the bound is indistinguishable from one of the bound plus a thousand. The view model's field was called `truncatedAt` and the copy said older movements are not listed here, which on a ledger of exactly 500 is a false statement about an audit record — the one kind of claim this page exists to be trusted on. The field is now `readBound` and the line says what it read and that it cannot tell whether there is more. The name changed as well as the string, because the name is where the false claim started. Telling full from truncated needs the contract to answer it; `MandateList.truncatedAt` carries the same limitation for `list_mandates` and its two surfaces, and is left as it is here rather than renamed across two other lanes in an app-half PR. Applied: `app/[org]/[ws]/mandates/[mandate]/page.tsx` (moved), `shared/safe-path.ts`, `features/mandate/{view,mandate,ledger}.ts(x)`, `features/agents/mandates.tsx`, `data/contracts/mandates.ts`, `data/live/mappers/mandates.ts`, `messages/mandate.json`, `capability-ui-map.json`, `docs/capabilities/{get,update_mandate_limits,revoke}_mandate.md`.

**2026-09-18 — Steering is the hub; Skills and Ontology lose their own nav entries in Phase 2; a tier word never claims more than what was routed (ADR-091 and ADR-093 to ADR-097).** Source: the maintainer, 2026-09-18, approving the architecture review of that date in full. **What is true in this app today:** the sidebar carries Tools, Skills and Steering as three entries (`features/shell/nav.ts` `WORKSPACE_NAV`), Ontology has none, the Steering page reads the published registry and proposals (#2961), and nothing on any page is delivered to a wrapped agent until Phase 0 merges (PR #3289, ADR-091: active `must` and `should` records compile into the bundle's `context.system`). **The target, Phase 2 (after Phase 1's assembler exists):** one Steering hub with seven tabs, in this order: Records, Skills, Memory, Ontology, Policy, Proposals, Preview. Preview picks an agent and a prompt and shows exactly what would be injected, what was cut, and why; it is the assembler (`assembleSteering`, ADR-093) run without delivery, read through one new port method, not a second implementation in the app. `/{org}/{ws}/skills[/<tab>]` redirects to the Skills tab and `skills` leaves `WORKSPACE_NAV`; ADR-090's five views become sections of that tab (see ADR-090's amendment of the same date). §1.2's Skills and Steering rows describe what is built and stand until the Phase 2 PR changes them in the same commit as the routes. **Build order:** Phase 0 in review, Phase 4 (the gateway, ADR-094) in build, then Phases 1, 2, 3, 5. **Words (ADR-095), binding on every string this app renders:** a run on the `harness` tier is "delivered", "recorded", "client-attested", "fail-open", never "enforced"; "observed" metering and "enforced" budgets belong to the `gateway` tier and only for routed traffic; "enforced" without that qualifier belongs to `contained`. Until Phases 4 and 5 land, `gateway` (for a wrapped harness) and `contained` render as tiers not yet available, and no page claims a model proxy, observed metering, an enforced session budget, a real interrupt or a sandbox as present. New governance ceremony on the Steering page is frozen until ADR-091 §6's proof is recorded on #2592.

**2026-09-18: Model funding is back in rev1, as its own Organization tab.** Source: the maintainer, 2026-09-17, asking that an organization be able to bring its own key for any OpenAI-compatible vendor and use the in-app assistant on it. This reverses the model-funding half of the 2026-09-14 cut. Data-plane UI stays cut. The backend already existed (ADR-053, #2807) and PR #3290 widened it to five vendors, so the page is an app half only: port method `org.modelCredential`, view model `ModelCredential`, mapper, section, form, three actions and their tests. **Why a tab and not a section of People:** the credential is org-scoped and pays for every workspace, which is the same reason API keys and Roles are tabs; a section on People would hide a billing decision under a membership page. **Three defects fixed with it**, because the page could not have worked without them: none of the four handlers asserted a role, and the non-enterprise IAM fast path never reads `defaultRoles`, so any member could set the key, and with an OpenAI-compatible endpoint send every assistant conversation to a server they own; `get_model_credential` did not declare `mutates: false`, so `kernelRead` refused it; and verifying a stored key that did not exist threw a plain `Error`. **Not on this page:** the monthly assistant spend cap. The deprecated page read and wrote it through `@oxagen/billing` directly, which `apps/app` may not import, and no contract carries it. A cap control needs get/set capabilities first. Applied: `app/[org]/model-funding/page.tsx` (new, +1 route), `features/organization/model-funding*.ts(x)` (new), `data/ports.ts`, `data/contracts/org.ts`, `data/live/org.ts`, `data/live/mappers/org.ts`, every `DataSource` fake, `features/organization/tabs.tsx`, `features/shell/{nav,commands,nav-icons}.ts(x)`, `shared/safe-path.ts`, `shared/legacy-routes.ts` (`/{org}/settings/model-funding` now lands on the page), `e2e/routes.ts`, `RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS`, `capability-ui-map.json` (four bindings), `messages/{organization,en,shell}.json`, §1.1 and §1.2.

**2026-09-17 — The API keys roster opens on the keys that have not been revoked, and is cut into pages.** Source: the maintainer, 2026-09-17. `list_api_keys` answers the workspace's whole roster with no filter and no page of its own — the handler's comment says so, and the contract returns revoked rows deliberately so a page can show them — so the roster grew without bound and led with rows that authenticate nothing. Decided: the page hides revoked keys by default and shows 20 rows at a time, both as query values on the one route (§1.2, no new route), parsed in `features/organization/api-keys-view.ts` and falling back to the default for a value the page cannot read. **The filter is judged on `revokedAt` alone, never on the clock.** An expired key stays on the default roster: expiry is a state the row's own running clock crosses while the page is open (`key-row.tsx`, `ui/expiry-clock.ts`), so filtering on it would move a row out from under a reader as a second ticked over, and the server's roster and the browser's would disagree. A revocation is recorded and cannot un-happen. Three consequences fall out and are pinned by tests. (1) **The empty line distinguishes an empty workspace from a filtered one** — "This workspace has no API keys" over a workspace holding three revoked ones is a lie the filter would be telling on the page's behalf — and the All link carries the count it is holding back, so asking for the revoked keys is never a guess about whether there are any. (2) **`listedIds` stays the whole read, not the visible page.** It is what clears a shown secret (`create-key-dialog.tsx`): the first render on which the roster names the minted key, the secret leaves state. A key minted while the roster was filtered or paged past the first is in the read and not on screen, so narrowing this to the rendered rows would leave a secret up with nothing left to clear it. (3) **A mint returns to the first page of the filter in view, a revocation to the page it was read on.** The roster is newest first, so a created or rotated key is on the first page and nowhere else; returning a rotation to page four answers it with a screen that does not hold its result. Revoke changes no order, and under the default filter the row it ended leaves the page, which is the point. The offset is clamped to a page that exists, so revoking the last key on the last page — or narrowing the filter from a deep page — lands on the last page rather than on an empty table with no way back but the address bar. The workspace picker carries the filter across and drops the page: a person who asked to see revoked keys asked about keys, while page four of this roster says nothing about the next one. **Two names the review corrected, both the same mistake at different altitudes.** The filter's label was "Active", over a roster that keeps a row on `revokedAt` alone, so an expired key sat under it with its own status chip reading "expired": the page contradicted itself in two places at once. The label now names the predicate it has, "Not revoked". And an offset is aligned down to a multiple of `API_KEYS_PAGE` as well as clamped, because the query string is shareable and hand-editable: `?offset=1` showed rows 2 to 21 while Previous, at offset 0, showed rows 1 to 20, the two pages repeating 19 rows with no sequence of clicks ever reaching a boundary again. Pages partition the roster; they do not slide along it. Not done here: `list_api_keys` gains no filter and no cursor, so every key in the workspace crosses the wire on every load. That is the right trade while a workspace holds tens of keys and the wrong one at thousands; moving the cut into the contract is a change on the API and MCP as well, and is left to its own lane. Applied: `features/organization/api-keys-view.ts` (new), `features/organization/api-keys.tsx`, `features/organization/key-row.tsx`, `features/organization/create-key-dialog.tsx`, `features/organization/index.ts`, `app/[org]/api-keys/page.tsx`, `shared/safe-path.ts` (`routes.apiKeys` takes `show` and `offset`), `messages/organization.json`, §1.2's API keys row.

**2026-09-16 — Tools keeps its `UNRECORDED` row while the page is part-built (#2957 app half).** The mandates ledger is the first section on `/{org}/{ws}/tools` to have a store behind it, and §3.6's rule was written for a page with no section at all: delete the row in the PR that lands the page. Deleting it here would have left the registry, connections, kill switches and auto-approvals with no statement at all, and `knip --production --strict` reported the mechanical consequence — `NotRecorded` and `UNRECORDED` become unused files, because nothing else renders a row until WL-35 renders `run.frames_wrapped`. The row therefore stays, rendered beneath the ledger rather than in place of a page, with prose that names exactly the four tabs #2958 owes; #2958 deletes it. The rule this refines: a whole-page row is deleted by the lane that lands the **last** of its page's sections, not the first. Applied here: §1.2 Tools row, §3.6, `src/data/unrecorded.ts`, `src/ui/not-recorded.tsx`.

**2026-09-16 — The mandate request form never scales a limit (#2957 app half).** A mandate limit is stored in the units the ledger records, and which those are is a property of the tool version's declaration: micros for an `amount`, whole units for a `count` (INV-09). `readMeasure` (`packages/rules/src/mandates/measures.ts`) reads the call by that declaration and compares it against the stored limit as an integer. The declaration is not reachable from the app — `measureDeclarationsSchema` is an input of `publish_tool_declaration` and appears in no output, `list_tool_declarations` answers the version and checksum and no `measures`, `get_agent_toolbelt` carries none, and reading `agent.tool_versions` is banned (INV-05) — and nothing downstream checks the scaling either: `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` (`packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`) refuses a measure that is undeclared or `text` and never compares its `type` to anything the request states. Two rounds of review found the same defect by two routes: first an unconditional `microsFromDecimal`, then an operator-stated `kind` whose default was `amount`. Both put a count of 50 into the ledger as 50,000,000. **Decision: the form multiplies nothing.** Being wrong is not symmetric — scaling a declared count grants a millionfold more authority than was typed, silently, while storing a declared amount verbatim grants a millionfold less and denies calls where a person sees it. A mandate is bounded authority, so a form that can store a wider bound than the operator entered is a worse failure than one that cannot express every bound. Every limit the dialog writes is whole units, stored digit for digit; the unit field refuses an ISO 4217 code in either casing, so what this form writes always reads back as the count it is. A money limit is requested over the API or MCP, by a caller that holds the declaration; the app reads one back as money either way. **The durable answer is `measures` on `list_tool_declarations`' output** — with it the form resolves the declaration for the named measure across the tools its patterns match, defaults from it, refuses a mismatch, and can scale an amount because it knows it is one. That is `list_tool_declarations`' object and not this lane's, which is why it is recorded here rather than built. Applied: `features/agents/actions.ts` (`MandateDraft` loses `kind` and renames `currency` to `unit`), `features/agents/mandate-request.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A mandate surface states authority, not row count, and never overstates what it can see (#2957 app half).** Three findings of one shape, each a page asserting more than it had established. (a) `list_mandates` narrows a reader without an accountable org role to the agents they created and reports the narrowing as a *successful* list, so an empty answer is not evidence of an empty ledger; `readsEveryMandate` (`data/contracts/mandates.ts`, mirroring `ACCOUNTABLE_ORG_ROLES`) decides whether a surface may say no authority exists, and a reader outside that set is told what the view cannot show instead. A role added to the handler's set and not to the app's hedges for a reader who needs no hedge, which is the direction that cannot mislead. (b) A row is not authority: a `draft`, a `revoked` and an `expired` row all authorize nothing, and `request_mandate` writes a draft — so a page counting rows stopped warning at the moment an operator asked for a mandate, the moment the agent still had none. `isEffective` requires `active` **and** the instant inside the validity window, and the rows stay listed because the request and the history are what the office reads. (c) `MandateList` carries `asOf`, the instant the ledger answered, because whether a mandate is in effect is a question about an instant and a component may not ask a clock during render (`@eslint-react/purity`); the mapper stamps it. The precedent is the truncation line: say what the view cannot show rather than imply a fact. Applied: `data/contracts/mandates.ts`, `data/live/mappers/mandates.ts`, `features/agents/mandates.tsx`, `features/tools/mandates-ledger.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`, `messages/tools.json`.

**2026-09-16 — Every request-dialog field, and which of them the tool already declares (#2957 app half).** Five rounds of review found five defects on this dialog and they were one defect: *the form asks the operator for facts only the tool's declaration holds, and the app cannot read the declaration.* Rather than wait for the sixth, every field was classified.

| Field | Decided by | What catches a wrong value |
|---|---|---|
| `consequenceTags` | **the tool** (`agent.tool_versions.consequence_tags`) | nothing — `findCoveringMandate` silently covers no tool. The rule is stated in the dialog and the set is open |
| `measure` | **the tool** (`measures` key) | `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` → `measure_not_declared`, refused at request time and shown in the dialog |
| `unit` | **the tool** (`measures[name].unit`) | partial: a currency code is refused, so the *kind* cannot be misread; a wrong *name* mislabels the ledger and changes no authority |
| `perCall`, `perPeriod` | the operator | the *scale* is the tool's (`measures[name].scale`), which is why nothing is scaled at all |
| `period`, `callsPerDay` | the operator | `calls` is built in and declares nothing |
| `tools` | the operator | `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` → `no_tool_matches` |
| `purpose`, `validFrom`, `validTo` | the operator | — |

Three fields are in the tool's half, and each is handled at the strongest level available: `measure` has a real backstop, `unit` cannot produce a wrong kind, and `consequenceTags` — which has no backstop at all — states its rule in the dialog and accepts any `consequenceTagSchema` tag, because the starter six are a set the workspace extends (`publish_tool_declaration` says so) and a tool declaring `ships_code` must be nameable or it can never hold a mandate. The other eight are genuinely the operator's and need nothing. **This enumeration is the convergence claim**: no remaining field asks the operator to restate a declared fact without either a backstop or a stated rule.

**`get_workspace_settings` is not the missing source.** Its output carries `consequenceRoles`, and the keys are consequence tags, so it looks like the answer — it is not. That map is *tags with an accountable org role* (the starter set plus the workspace's overrides; an unmapped custom tag falls to `DEFAULT_CONSEQUENCE_ROLES.other`), which is a different set from *tags a tool declares*. Reading it would produce a better-populated checkbox list and would not remove the need for free entry, so it was not wired.

**What `list_tool_declarations`' output must carry** to end this class, in full, so the next lane to open that contract finds the whole list and not two thirds of it: `measures` as the declaration record — each measure's `type`, `unit` and `scale`, not just its name — and `consequence_tags`. With `type` and `scale` the form scales an amount because it knows it is one; with `unit` it defaults and validates the denomination; with `consequence_tags` it collects the set from the matched patterns and refuses a request that cannot cover them. That is one contract change closing all five findings at the source.

**2026-09-16 — `list_mandates`' grants contradict its own creator filter (#2957; not fixed here, reported for a decision).** `request_mandate` grants org Owner/Admin/**Member** and workspace Owner/Member. `list_mandates` grants **only** the four accountable org roles — no workspace role, no org Member. So a workspace Owner or Member may create a draft and is then denied the read of it.
