The question is which side is the defect, and the evidence settles it: `readerFilter` (`packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`) exists precisely to return the acting user id for a reader *without* an accountable org role, and `mandate.list.ts` uses it to narrow the answer to the agents that reader created — **that branch is unreachable under the current grants**, because IAM refuses before the handler runs. A filter written to be reachable, and grants that make it dead code: the grants are wrong, not the filter, and not the request button. Fixing it means widening `defaultRoles` on a read capability, which is an IAM change in `packages/oxagen` and #3024's object; this PR touches no contract, and quietly widening who may read a mandate ledger is not a change to smuggle into an app half. Reported rather than taken. Two notes for whoever does it: the app is already correct for the aligned world — `blindSpotOf`'s `reader_scope` branch is the surface half of the same filter, and is equally unreachable today — and until then a non-accountable requester sees `denied` with the permission named, which is accurate rather than silent.

**2026-09-19: Fixed (#3440, ADR-107).** `list_mandates` and `get_mandate` now grant org Owner/Admin/Billing/Compliance (unnarrowed) plus workspace Owner/Member (narrowed by `readerFilter`), matching `request_mandate`. Both `readerFilter` and `blindSpotOf`'s `reader_scope` branch are reachable now, not dead code, and were widened further mid-fix to admit a mandate the caller requested for an agent they did not create, not only ones they created (the requester grant), then narrowed again to exclude a soft-deleted agent's mandates and to fail closed rather than open when a narrowed reader has no live agent to narrow by. `apps/app/messages/{tools,agents,mandate}.json`'s narrowed-reader "partial" copy updated to state both grants.

**2026-09-16 — The ledger shows the tool scope and the accounting window (#2957 app half).** Two facts the page held and discarded, each making two genuinely different authorities render identically: a mandate scoped `*` and one scoped `payments.read@*` were the same row — the difference between an agent that may call everything and one that may call a single tool, on the page an accountable reader uses to review what they granted — and a daily limit of 100 looked like a monthly one, with settled, reserved and remaining given no window to be counted over. **Assessed against #3130 before building, because the third review in a row to find a dropped fact raises whether the mapper should be projecting at all — and here it is not**: `MandateRow.tools` and `MandateAuthority.period`/`periodKey` are already on the view model and already mapped by `toMandateList`. The projection that dropped them is the ledger's own local `Authority` component, which mapped each measure down to a name and a value. So this is additive inside existing shapes and reaches no contract, unlike the measure kind. Tool scope is a column of its own, and a pattern of exactly `*` is called out as **every tool** rather than printed, because a reader should not have to notice one character to see that a mandate is unrestricted. The window sits with the limit it qualifies — per measure, not per row, since a mandate may cap calls daily and money monthly — and names the period and the `periodKey`, which is what says *which* window a balance belongs to. Applied: `features/tools/mandates-ledger.tsx`, `messages/tools.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A count is enforced on its digits, never through a double (#2957, `packages/rules`).** `readMeasure`'s count branch routed the tool's reported figure through `Number` and back: `Number("9007199254740995")` is `9007199254740996`, so **a call one unit over its mandate was admitted** and nothing logged a conflict — the fourth appearance on this lane of two halves each self-consistent and disagreeing only in the direction that releases the call. A large value became scientific notation, and `exceeds` then threw when it handed `"1e+30"` to `BigInt`, so the form could also create a limit that made the gate crash rather than deny. INV-09 has said money is micros and BigInt and never a float since the beginning; this is that rule one column over, and the count path had not been held to it. **Fixed in `readMeasure`, not at the app form**, for the reason the unit check was: every writer goes through the gate, and refusing at the form would leave `grant_mandate` and `update_mandate_limits` able to create limits the gate cannot enforce. A string count is carried digit for digit (admitted by the same `MEASURE_VALUE` as before, so nothing new is accepted); a JSON number past the safe range is refused rather than rounded, because it was already inexact when the parser read it and no reading of it is the figure the tool meant — a tool needing a count that large reports it as a string. **The return shape is unchanged**, which is what kept this inside `packages/rules`: `read.measure.value` is still a decimal integer string and its only consumers are `values[measure]` and `exceeds`, which was already BigInt. A consequence worth stating: the request form's thirty-digit allowance, which mirrored `measureValueSchema`, is now *correct* rather than optimistic — the gate can enforce those figures exactly. Applied: `packages/rules/src/mandates/measures.ts`, `packages/rules/src/mandates/measures.test.ts`.

**2026-09-16: Nothing carries a measure's kind, and that is the root of the unit family (#2957; not fixed here, scope recorded for a decision). Fixed 2026-09-19 by #3130 / ADR-108.** Four findings in four rounds were one fact: the limit's unit disagreeing with the declaration; `calls` exempt from that comparison; `toMandateList` reading `250000000` as `$250.00`; and a declared **count** whose unit is legitimately `"USD"` rendering as currency. Each was correctly fixed and each closed one site, and the supply of sites is not bounded by any of them, because **`isCurrencyCode(unit)` is a heuristic standing in for a fact the system already holds.**

Where the fact is, and where it is guessed — the whole reader list, since the enumeration is the useful part:

| Site | Asks money-or-count? | How it answers |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/rules` `readMeasure` — the gate | yes | `switch (declaration.type)`. **The fact.** Never guesses |
| `packages/handlers` `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` | no, but **holds the declaration** while validating the write | ~~the kind is in hand here and is discarded~~ **fixed:** stamps `kind` onto the limit it returns for storage (ADR-108) |
| `packages/rules` `readAuthority`, `_mandate.ts` `mapMandates` | ~~no~~ **fixed** | ~~pass `currencyOrUnit` through from the stored limit, which carries no kind~~ read the stored `kind`, falling back to `legacyMeasureKindGuess` only for a row written before ADR-108 |
| `apps/app` `mappers/mandates.ts` `measureValue` | ~~yes, the only guessing site in the system~~ **fixed** | ~~`isCurrencyCode(currencyOrUnit)`~~ switches on the `kind` the contract now carries |
| `Measure`, `NamedMeasure`, `useMeasureText`, `MandateBar`, the ledger row, the agent row, the approval card | no | consume the discriminated `MeasureValue`; unchanged, correct since the mapper is |
| `apps/app` `actions.ts` | no, it **refuses** a currency-code unit | a write-side rule, not a classification: the form cannot scale money whatever the kind, so this stayed as it was |

**Resolved by #3130 / ADR-108 (2026-09-19).** `mandateLimitSchema` and `mandateAuthoritySchema` (`packages/oxagen/src/mandates/schemas.ts`) both carry `kind: "money" | "count"`. `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` (`packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`) works it out once, from the declaration it already validates, and returns the stamped limits every write handler (`grant_mandate`, `request_mandate`, `update_mandate_limits`) persists instead of the caller's raw input. A limit written before ADR-108 has no stored `kind`; `parseMandateRow` (`packages/rules/src/mandates.ts`) fills it with the documented fallback, `legacyMeasureKindGuess`, the same heuristic this table used to name as the sole guessing site, kept as what it is rather than deleted, and removable once every stored limit carries a real `kind`. The mapper switches on the fact and calls `isCurrencyCode` for nothing.

**Why it was not fixed here (2026-09-16), and what landed instead (#3130 / ADR-108, 2026-09-19).** Carrying the kind meant `mandateLimitSchema` (and `mandateAuthoritySchema`) gaining a `kind`, written by the handlers from the declaration `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` already validates, and read by the mapper instead of `isCurrencyCode`. That was a contract change *and* a change to the stored `mandates.limits` jsonb, which existing rows do not carry: it needed a fallback or a backfill, and was #3024's object at the time. The alternative, resolving declarations at read time in `list_mandates`, was rejected: a join per mandate, and genuinely ambiguous when a mandate's tool patterns match two tools declaring the same measure with different types. `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` now refuses that case outright (`measure_kind_conflict`) rather than picking a tool's declaration silently. ADR-108 records the choice: derive at write time, once, where the declaration was already fetched and validated, and take a documented fallback (`legacyMeasureKindGuess`) for the rows a backfill did not touch, rather than a migration, the same guess this document used to call the sole guessing site, now named, isolated to `parseMandateRow`, and removable once every row carries a real `kind`.

**`isCurrencyCode` no longer decides anything in the mapper.** A heuristic left beside the fact it stood in for was the next reader's trap, and now nothing reads it. Its other use, refusing a currency-code unit on the request form in `actions.ts`, is a different rule and survives.

**2026-09-16 — An exemption from a check needs its own rule, not silence (#2957, `packages/handlers`).** The unit check below exempts `calls`, and the note recording it said the built-in measure "declares nothing to compare against". The first half was right and the conclusion did not follow: there being no *declaration* to compare against does not mean there is no *correct unit*. `calls` has one and it is its own name. So `{ calls: { perPeriod: "250000000", currencyOrUnit: "USD" } }` was accepted by the contract, enforced by the gate as a ceiling of **250 million calls**, and rendered by `toMandateList` as **$250.00** — the same two-halves-a-billion-apart failure the declared-measure check closes, reached through the exemption beside it, and reachable from `api` and `mcp` whatever the form does. The `calls` branch is now an explicit assertion against the constant, with a comment saying why it is checked against a constant where the others are checked against a declaration, so the next reader finds a rule where the gap was.

**The pattern this is the third instance of: a fix written against the case in the finding rather than against the space of cases.** The `length > 1` label condition; the per-call fallback that keyed on "are there any bars" instead of partitioning the measures; the `calls` exemption. The remedy is structural where it can be — `drawsBar` is exported from `MandateBar` and the approvals panel partitions by **the component's own predicate**, so a caller's question and the component's answer cannot drift, which is exactly how the per-call case arose. A sweep of the input *adjacent* to every other fix on this lane turned up one more: **a mandate column in which every measure is unlimited rendered an empty cell**, ambiguous between *no limit* and *not shown*, where the Tools ledger already said "no limit"; Agents › Mandates now says it too. The rest held — no-scaling against `calls` and against an `amount` declaration, the consequence set against a tag duplicated between the boxes and the free field, calls-only against measure-only and both and neither, `overLimit` against a single-component excess and a combined one and no per-period limit at all, `blindSpotOf` against a non-empty narrowed answer and a non-empty truncated one, `isEffective` at both window boundaries, and `NamedMeasure` against one measure, two, the bar and the per-call line.

**2026-09-16 — A mandate limit must be denominated in the unit the tool declares (#2957, `packages/handlers`).** The worst finding in this lane, and the only one whose fix is a handler change. `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` checked a limit's measure **name** and that its type is not `text`, and never looked at the declaration's third field. The gate reads a call *by* that declaration — `readMeasure` takes a count as the tool reported it, in the tool's unit — and compares it against the limit's figure. So a tool declaring `storage` in GB accepted a limit an operator entered and an approver read as **50 bytes** and then admitted a call of **50 GB**. Both halves self-consistent, nothing logged as a conflict, and the approver's understanding and the enforced ceiling a billion apart. The same shape as the app's scaling defect, reached through the one field nobody was comparing. **Fixed where the declaration is in hand**, not at a caller: `_mandate.ts` now refuses `measure_unit_mismatch` when `limits[name].currencyOrUnit` differs from the declared `unit`, so `grant_mandate`, `request_mandate` and `update_mandate_limits` are all covered by one check and no future writer can miss it. `calls` is exempt from *this* check and carries its own, recorded in the entry above. This rides the PR under SCR-004 because it is small and clearly correct: every existing fixture that denominates a limit differently is already a refusal test, so no behaviour anyone relied on changes. The app maps the new reason to a sentence and the dialog's unit hint states the rule. Applied: `packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`, `packages/handlers/src/mandate.handlers.pg.test.ts`, `apps/app/src/features/agents/action-failure.ts`, `apps/app/messages/agents.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A per-call-only mandate is named rather than drawn (#2957 app half).** `MandateBar` answers null without a per-period limit, because a bar needs a denominator. An approval card whose mandate limits every measure per call only therefore rendered no bar at all — and, because the found-mandate branch also suppressed the `mandateUnread` fallback, printed the current-period caveat over that emptiness: a note about figures it was not showing, with the mandate's own id nowhere on the card. The branch now keys off the measures a bar can draw rather than off the mandate existing, and a mandate with none names itself and prints its per-call limits through `NamedMeasure`, saying nothing about a period it is not counting. Applied: `features/fleet/approvals-panel.tsx`, `messages/fleet.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A measure's name travels with its value (#2957 app half).** The multi-measure fix labelled a figure only when a mandate limited more than one, and I argued for that condition: labelling the single figure in a column already headed *Per call* adds noise. The argument assumed the lone measure is the expected one, which is true only when it is `amount` — a mandate limiting `tax` alone drew an unlabelled dollar figure with nothing on the row to say which budget it governed. The bar had the omission in a stronger form: `authority.measure` went only into a `data-measure` attribute, so an approval card whose mandate limits two per-period measures in one currency drew two "Remaining authority" bars indistinguishable on screen and to a screen reader alike. **Both are one thing — the label belongs to the measure, not to the surface rendering it** — so it is fixed where the rendering happens rather than at each caller: `NamedMeasure` in `ui/measure.tsx` prints a figure with its name and takes no option to omit it, and `MandateBar` names the measure in its heading and at the head of its accessible name from the `authority` it already receives, so no caller can decline to pass one and no later caller can inherit the omission. Applied: `ui/measure.tsx`, `ui/mandate-bar.tsx`, `features/tools/mandates-ledger.tsx`, `features/agents/mandates.tsx`, `messages/ui.json`.

**2026-09-16 — Every sentence about a mandate list, against the rows its read can return (#2957 app half).** The third axis, and the one the first two could not catch. The field table asks *whose fact is this?* and the bound table asks *whose bound is this?*; neither asks **does this sentence describe the rows the read actually returns?** An earlier catalogue pass looked for absolute claims — *every*, *all* — and walked straight past `"The mandates this workspace has granted"`, because **"has granted" carries no quantifier**: it is a claim about the *status* of every row, and `list_mandates` returns `draft` rows the table beneath labels "requested". A workspace whose ledger held only drafts was told it had granted authority.

Every string in `messages/{agents,tools,ui,fleet}.json` backed by a mandate read, checked against every status its read can return:

| String | Read | Was it true of every row? |
|---|---|---|
| `tools.mandates.lead` | `list_mandates` (draft/active/expired/revoked) | **no** — "has granted". Now "has recorded — granted, requested, expired and revoked" |
| `tools.mandates.truncated` | same | **no** — "has granted at least that many". Now "has recorded" |
| `tools.mandates.partial`, `emptyListed`, `emptyListedDetail` | same | **no** — inherited "granted" from the lead. Now "recorded" |
| `tools.mandates.empty` | same, zero rows | understated: true of grants, silent on requests. Now "none granted, and none requested" |
| `agents.mandates.title` | `list_mandates` for one agent | **no** — "Mandates held"; a draft is not held. Now "Mandates recorded" |
| `agents.mandates.truncated`, `partial`, `none`, `retired` | same | **no** — "holds" / "held". Now "recorded for this agent" |
| `agents.mandates.noneEffective` | same | **no** — "No mandate this agent holds is in effect". Now "recorded for" |
| `agents.mandates.request.perCallHint` | the write, not a read | **no** — "what is granted is what is asked for", but `grant_mandate` may activate a draft with limits of its own. Now "the limit this request carries is the figure entered" |
| `agents.mandates.noneListed` | `list_mandates` | yes — its claim is about *authority*, and an effective mandate is held authority |
| `tools.mandates.notGranted`, `columns.grantedBy`, `status.*` | per-row cells | yes — `grantedBy` is null on a draft and the cell says so |
| `ui.mandateBar.*`, `fleet.approvals.mandate*` | figures from one authority row | yes — they describe a measure, not a set |

The rule that falls out: **a sentence over a list names the widest state its read returns.** `list_mandates` returns a mandate in every state, so the word is *recorded*; *granted*, *held* and *in effect* are claims about a subset and are used only where the code has narrowed to that subset (`isEffective`, `grantedBy`). Pinned by a test that renders a ledger of drafts and asserts the page says "has recorded" and never "has granted", plus a per-status sweep over all four states.

Checked and left alone: `agents.detail.credentials.empty`, `agents.detail.toolbelt.*`, `agents.detail.enrollment.lead`, `agents.source.lead` and the retire/resume bodies make claims over #2956's reads, not this lane's, and are outside this diff.

With the field table and the bound table this closes the three questions that produced every finding on this PR: *whose fact is this?*, *whose bound is this?*, and *whose rows are these?*

**2026-09-16 — A retired identity is not offered a mandate, and the handler half is filed (#2957 app half).** `retire_agent` sets `agents.status = "archived"` and suspends the principal; it does not set `deletedAt`, which is the only status `resolveAgent` (`packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`) filters on. So `request_mandate` and `grant_mandate` both succeed against a retired identity, putting real rows in the ledger and real authority on the page for something that can never run. The page now declines the request and says why, beside the identity card that already hides its writes on a retired agent. **The handler half was judged not to ride this PR**, and the judgement is the point: a blanket refusal in `resolveAgent` would also break `list_mandates`, `get_mandate` and `revoke_mandate`, which must keep working after retirement — the record has to stay readable and a live mandate has to stay revocable — so the refusal belongs on the three capabilities that create or widen authority, not on the resolver they share. Underneath that sits a question only the maintainer settles: whether an active mandate survives retirement at all, or whether `retire_agent` should refuse or revoke while one is live. That is the same question #3123 raises about `archive_workspace` stranding live API keys, and it is filed as **#3124** with both halves in its DoD. **The family is one shape** — an object that outlives the thing that scoped it, where the parent's handler already enumerates what it refuses over (`archive_workspace` refuses while an agent is registered) and the list is missing a member. Not a missing concept; an incomplete list — which is why #3124's DoD proposes one enumeration of what is scoped to an agent and to a workspace that the retire and archive handlers both consult. Applied: `features/agents/mandates.tsx`, `features/agents/agent.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`. **Resolved 2026-09-19 by ADR-106 and #3124: retirement revokes, it does not refuse.** `retire_agent` now revokes every `active` or `draft` mandate bound to the agent's principal in the same transaction it revokes credentials and host enrollments, rather than leaving them for a caller to find and refuse cleanup while any exist. `request_mandate`, `grant_mandate` and `update_mandate_limits` refuse a retired agent with the typed reason `agent_retired`; `list_mandates`, `get_mandate` and `revoke_mandate` are unchanged, exactly as this entry anticipated. #3123 remains open and unresolved; ADR-106 records the same reasoning as the answer whoever picks it up should apply. Applied: `packages/handlers/src/_mandate.ts`, `agent.retire.ts`, `mandate.request.ts`, `mandate.grant.ts`, `mandate.limits.update.ts`, `lib/agent-identity.ts`; `packages/oxagen/src/contracts/agent.retire.ts`; `features/mandate/action-failure.ts`, `messages/mandate.json`; `docs/adr/ADR-106-retirement-revokes-what-it-scoped.md`.

**2026-09-16 — Every bound the request form applies, and the contract rule it mirrors (#2957 app half).** The field table above asks *does this field make the operator restate a fact the tool declares?* Two findings passed it and failed a second question: **is the app's own bound the same as the contract's bound?** §2 keeps `@oxagen/oxagen/mandates/schemas` out of the app, so every bound on the request path is a copy, and a copy drifts in silence. The bounds are now one block in `data/contracts/mandates.ts`, each naming its rule, each pinned by `mandates.test.ts`.

| App bound | Contract rule | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `CONSEQUENCE_TAG` `/^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{1,63}$/` | `consequenceTagSchema` | matches, pinned at both length ends |
| `MAX_CONSEQUENCE_TAGS` 16 | `consequenceTags.min(1).max(16)` | matches |
| `MEASURE_VALUE` `/^(0\|[1-9][0-9]{0,29})$/` | `measureValueSchema` | matches, and now governs **calls** too — it was `/^\d{1,9}$/`, which refused a cap of a billion calls the ledger would have held |
| `CONSEQUENCE_OTHER_MAX` 1054 | 16 tags × 64 characters + separators | **was 256**, which truncated a legal set in the browser into a syntactically valid shorter one — requested, granted, covering nothing. Now derived, never chosen |
| `MEASURE_NAME_MAX` 64 | `measureNameSchema` | matches |
| `UNIT_MAX` 32 | `currencyOrUnit.min(1).max(32)` | matches |
| `PURPOSE_MAX` 2000 | `purpose.min(1).max(2000)` | matches |
| `DATE` `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` | `validFrom`/`validTo` are instants | **deliberately narrower**: the form collects days and widens each to the start and end of its own, so the window is inclusive at both ends |
| the `unit` refuses an ISO 4217 code | `currencyOrUnit` takes any string | **deliberately narrower**: the form cannot scale money, so it may not write a limit that reads back as money |
| the measure field refuses `calls` | a `calls` limit is legal | **deliberately narrower**: the calls-per-day field is that limit's only writer, so a figure meant for another measure cannot land under the name the gate reads as one per call |
| tool patterns, measure-name format, tag-to-tool coverage | `toolPatternSchema`, `.max(64)`, `measureNameSchema`, `findCoveringMandate` | **delegated**: no app bound at all, so nothing can truncate; the kernel refuses and the dialog shows the refusal |

Together with the field table this is the pair of questions that generated every finding on this PR: *whose fact is this?* and *whose bound is this?* No remaining bound on the request path is untraceable to a rule or to a written reason for being narrower.

**2026-09-16 — A calls-only mandate is a mandate (#2957 app half).** The action refused a request with no per-call or per-period measure limit, but `mandateLimitsSchema` needs one limit of any kind and `calls` is one, and `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` exempts `CALLS_MEASURE` from the declared-measure check precisely so a tool that carries a consequence and declares no numeric measure can still be bounded. That tool had no way through the dialog at all: blank fields refused here, an invented measure refused by the handler. The measure entry is now written only when it is asked for, its four fields stand or fall together, and a request naming neither a measure limit nor a calls limit is the only one refused. Applied: `features/agents/actions.ts`, `features/agents/mandate-request.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A mandate request names every consequence, because one is usually not enough (#2957 app half).** `findCoveringMandate` (`packages/rules/src/mandates.ts`) accepts a mandate only when `tool.consequenceTags.every(t => mandate.consequenceTags.includes(t))`. The dialog offered a single-choice `<select>` and the action wrote `consequenceTags: [consequenceTag]`, so a tool declaring two tags could never be covered: the operator asked, an approver granted exactly what was asked, and every call was still denied — at the moment of use, far from the dialog, with the mandate reading `active` and in effect on every screen. Worse in one respect than the scaling defect, which at least produced something that worked. The tags cannot be read from the app: `consequence_tags` lives on `agent.tool_versions` and appears in exactly one contract, `publish_tool_declaration`, as an input — the same wall as `measures` (INV-05). **Decision: the form expresses the set and states the rule.** The control is a checkbox group over the starter tags, the action takes the whole set trimmed and de-duplicated, and the hint says a mandate covers a tool only when it names all of that tool's tags. Naming too few is the safe way to be wrong — the mandate then covers nothing rather than more than was meant, and the gate's denial names the tags it wanted — which is why the form does not refuse instead: refusing what it cannot confirm would refuse every request, since it can confirm nothing here. `consequence_tags` on `list_tool_declarations`' output is the durable answer and the same lane as `measures`. Applied: `features/agents/actions.ts`, `features/agents/mandate-request.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`.

**2026-09-16 — A mandate surface answers "can this view establish that?" from the evidence, never from a lossy intermediate (#2957 app half).** Review found the same mistake three times on these pages in two rounds, each time a confident claim recovered from something that had already thrown the answer away. **(a)** The over-limit warning summed `settledRatio + reservedRatio`, but `ratioOfIntegers` clamps each to 1 for drawing — 600 settled with nothing reserved against a limit lowered to 500 arrives as `1 + 0`, which is not greater than 1, so the warning the previous fix added could not fire for an excess carried by one component; quantization could round a small combined one away as well. **(b)** "No mandate" was asserted from a truncated page: the read is newest-first and stops at its bound, so a hundred newer drafts push the one mandate still in effect off it, and every row present authorizes nothing. **(c)** It was asserted from a reader-narrowed list, recorded above. **The rule: ratios are for drawing, flags are for saying, and a flag is computed where the evidence still exists.** `MandateAuthority.overLimit` is taken in the mapper by `sumExceeds` (`data/contracts/money.ts`) on the integer strings the ledger recorded, before anything is clamped, rounded or scaled — the deliberate pair to `ratioOfIntegers`, whose clamp is correct for a bar and fatal for a question. `blindSpotOf(list, role)` answers why an empty result cannot establish that no authority exists — `reader_scope`, then `truncated`, then null — from the reader's role and `truncatedAt` rather than from the rows, and both mandate list surfaces take their empty state from it, so the three hedges are one shape with one place to add a fourth reason. **(d)** The warning's own copy then made the same class of claim one level down: `overLimit` is the sum against the limit, so it is true when an open reservation alone carries the excess, and the sentence said the limit was "fully settled" — reporting a completed effect where none had happened, against the distinction this surface teaches everywhere else ("reserved by calls in flight"). The message now names the excess and not where it sits, and points at the bar and the figures, which show the split without asserting it. **(e)** And the abstraction was itself half-applied: `blindSpotOf` was consulted only in the zero-row branch, so a narrowed reader who owns one agent saw that agent's mandates under a lead reading "Every mandate this workspace has granted", with no caveat. The question is not "is this empty" but **"is this the whole set"** — incompleteness is a property of the answer, not of its length, and a narrowed list of fifty rows is as partial as a narrowed list of none. So one line states what the answer is missing whenever anything is, above the rows and above the authority statement both; the empty branch declines to assert absence and the table no longer implies completeness, from the same call. The Tools lead dropped its "Every mandate" claim in the same pass. Applied: `data/contracts/money.ts`, `data/contracts/mandates.ts`, `data/live/mappers/mandates.ts`, `ui/mandate-bar.tsx`, `features/agents/mandates.tsx`, `features/tools/mandates-ledger.tsx`, `messages/agents.json`, `messages/tools.json`, `messages/ui.json`.

**2026-09-16 — The mandate bar draws an over-limit period as full (#2957 app half).** `update_mandate_limits` may lower a limit under authority already drawn, so `settledRatio + reservedRatio` can exceed one. Clamping each segment on its own and letting flex resolve the overflow rescaled both — 500 settled and 500 reserved against a limit lowered to 500 drew an even split, reading as a bar half used. The reservation is now drawn in the room settlement leaves, neither segment shrinks, and the overcommitment is stated in words (`ui.mandateBar.overLimit`, `data-over`) rather than disappearing into a bar that looks balanced. Applied: `ui/mandate-bar.tsx`, `messages/ui.json`.

**2026-09-19 — The mandate detail route has landed; its `e2e/routes.ts` row has not, and cannot yet (#2957 app half).** This replaces the 2026-09-16 entry that recorded the route as owed. `/{org}/{ws}/mandates/{mandate}` now exists with its `page.tsx`, its `pages.mandate` title key, its `get_mandate` port method and the two governed writes, and the mandates table on Agents links through to it. **What is still missing is the row in `e2e/routes.ts` that §1.2 asks every new route to arrive with, and the reason is the seed rather than the lane.** Every row in that table is a static path built from `SEED.orgSlug` and `SEED.workspaceSlug` alone, and `page-load.spec.ts` asserts each one answers 200 with its own title. A mandate's public id is minted by `grant_mandate`, so a row would have to name an id the seed created — and the seed creates none, because `assertToolsDeclareMeasures` refuses a grant whose every tool pattern does not match a declared, consequence-tagged tool in the workspace, and the seed declares no tool at all. A hard-coded id would 404, and a 404 answers 200 with the not-found title, so the row would fail the oracle it is there to satisfy. Adding it therefore means seeding a tool declaration, granting a mandate, writing its public id into `SEED_RECORD` beside `runPublicId`, and making `routes.ts` read that file — which nothing reads today. **The same gap already holds for every other parameterised route:** `/{org}/{ws}/agents/{agent}`, `…/agents/{agent}/source`, `/{org}/{ws}/runs/{run}` and `/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}` are all absent from the table for exactly this reason, so the app has 28 `page.tsx` files and 13 rows, 12 signed in and one anonymous. INV-20's route-set-equality clause is not implemented in `e2e-files.test.ts` (its header says that clause lands with `routes.ts`), which is why nothing fails today and why the count in §1.2 should be read as the page count rather than the row count. The right fix is one change for all five routes — seed the records the parameterised rows need, read `SEED_RECORD` in `routes.ts`, then turn on the equality clause — and it is an e2e-harness lane, not an app half. Each page's own component test asserts its title and its body in the meantime. **One constraint on that harness lane:** a seeded mandate is a grant of bounded financial authority that no person made, so the fixture's grant has to be marked as a fixture and excluded from every rollup and every spend figure before it is written. A smoke test that can load a page is not worth a grant the ledger of record cannot tell from a real one.

**2026-09-18 — Skills goes all in: resolution is in scope and the 2026-09-15 narrowing is reversed (ADR-090).** Source: maintainer decision 2026-09-18, to build the Skills page the Mission Control mockup draws. The narrowing below read spec §2's "no skills engine" as "no skills surface". Those are different claims: running a skill and resolving one are separate acts, and ADR-043 cut only the first. What the narrowing shipped was a page answering which skill names a harness happened to report, with no version, digest, source, cost or decision — an inventory presented as a control, deciding nothing. Decided, per ADR-090: Oxagen governs **resolution** and still runs no skill. `.oxagen/skills.toml` is the config of record, in the main repository on its production branch, read through the #3241 binding and changed only by pull request; its absence means off, and a workspace is created with `skills.enabled` false. Withholding happens before ranking — a held skill is not a low-ranked result, and the agent is told the count and the reason class, never the name. Turning skills on adds exactly one tool, `search_skills`, and moves no tier, grant or budget. A run pins its config version at start and replay resolves that version. An unbound repository with skills on stops the loop before the first model call (`ask`, 30-minute timeout to `deny`; there is no `allow`). Reflection is research-only and quarantined: out of band after the seal, outside the sealed chain, not replayed on a fork, billed as overhead, readable only under an organization `research.read` grant, and expiring. New stores `skills.config_versions`, `skills.resolutions` and `reflections`; new frame kinds `skills.searched`, `skills.loaded`, `skills.resolved`, `control.interject`, `control.answer`, `repo.unknown`, `repo.bound`, `workspace.created` — eight, not nine: a reflection is a post-seal record in `skills.reflections`, never a frame, because §8.2's dense `seq` and hash chain and §8.3's sealed Merkle root leave no way to append one after the seal. `list_skills` keeps its name and its job as a section beside the resolved catalog, because what the harness actually had is a different question from what it was allowed to find; its contract comment stops citing §2 as authority for "does not resolve". Build order is load-bearing: the resolution store and config versioning first, then the off state, then the page — shipping the off state first is what makes the default path right from the first commit. Applied to §0 item 1, §1.2 (the `/{org}/{ws}/skills` row, now a `[[...tab]]` catch-all; the route delta stays +1), §8 (the #3098 lane and its #3241 dependency) and the W13 bullet. The e2e rule is untouched: `apps/app/e2e` stays `login`, `pay` and `page-load`, and the page is proven by component and action tests.

**Superseded 2026-09-18 by ADR-090 and the entry above; kept as history.** **2026-09-15 — Skills ships in rev1 as what is recorded today; the W13 bullet is narrowed.** Source: maintainer decision 2026-09-15 — Skills and Audit ship in rev1 as what is built today, on the new skin (#3098 for Skills). For Skills, what is built today is one record: `tacho.sessions.skills_available`, the skill names a wrapped harness reports when a session starts, written by `ingest_tacho_events` and read by nothing. ADR-043 retired the skill system (`packages/skills`, the `skill.*` and `agent.skill.*` contracts, `agent.skills` and `agent.skill_versions`), so the legacy page has nothing behind it to port. Decided: `list_skills` reads that column and the session columns beside it, with no migration, no new table and no ClickHouse read; it declares `noBillingGate: true` (§1.5, INV-28) and gates the acting user in its handler, org Owner, Admin or Member or workspace Owner or Member (INV-29). A null inventory counts as not reported, and the reported count is null when no session in the window reported one (§3.4). `/{org}/{ws}/skills` renders its one section in the §3.3 states, sits between Tools and Steering in the sidebar and in the phone's More sheet, and appears in ⌘K as `go:skills`; the legacy `/{org}/{ws}/workbench/tools/skills/**` and `/{org}/{ws}/settings/skills` routes redirect to it (§7.3). Every mockup element with no backing renders nothing (§3.6): search, `.oxagen/skills.toml` and its versions, the off gate, the dialogs, digest approval, In the loop, Reflection and the nav count. Spec §2 ("no skills engine") and ADR-043 stay true: Oxagen reports which skills the harness had and does not run, resolve or author one. The route count moves +1: 19 → 20 at WL-33's baseline, and with every lane landed the total is 26 (§1.2, §6.3). Applied in one lane PR carrying both halves, because the backend half had not landed on `app-rebuild` when the app half started. The dead skill-load telemetry (`packages/telemetry/src/skill-telemetry.ts`, its barrel export and its tests) is deleted in the same PR; the `skill_loads` ClickHouse table and its migration stay, because a migration applied in production is never edited.

**2026-09-15 — Audit ships in rev1 as what is built today, on the new skin.** Maintainer decision, 2026-09-15, reversing the Audit half of the 2026-09-14 scale-back below. Source: #3097. Audit ships the page the deprecated app already serves — the `security.security_events` viewer at `/{orgSlug}/security/audit` and its signed export — rebuilt on the rev1 seams, and builds no new backend beyond wiring what exists. The full design (#2966: eight tabs, an `audit.audit_events` store, receipts, legal holds, KEK rotation, assurance, erasure) stays closed, and the rest of the 2026-09-14 cut stands: incidents beyond what is built, Cedar simulation, the assurance suite, Spend reconciliation, agent scores, SSO/SCIM, funding and data-plane UI, two-person mandates, steering effect, retirement and thresholds, Ontology. Four defects on the read path the page binds are fixed with it, because each would make the page print a wrong answer: `query_audit_log` threw a plain `Error` for a refusal, which `classifyKernelFailure` keys on `code` and so read as `unclassified` — the error state, not the denied one (INV-14); for a caller without the role it fell through to filtering on the org-only workspace sentinel and answered an empty record where the answer is a refusal, which is a fabricated value (§3.6); it read `org_users` itself instead of `assertOrgRole` on the acting user (INV-29); and it declared no `noBillingGate`, so an org at 0 GAUs was refused its own audit record (INV-28). The Incidents tab is not ported: `list_incidents` filters on `ctx.workspaceId`, so an org-scope caller carrying the sentinel reads nothing, and an org-wide incident register is new backend. Applied: §0 item 1; §1.1; §1.2 (the `/{org}/audit` row, the shell row's sidebar links and its More-sheet entry, +1 `page.tsx` file and `e2e/routes.ts` entry, and with every lane landed the total is 26); §1.5 and INV-28 (both contracts); §3.6 (Audit has a page, so it has no `UNRECORDED` row); §6.3 (26 steps); §7.1; §7.3 (`security/audit` → `/{org}/audit`, and no row under `/{org}/audit/**`, which would swallow the export download); §8 (the E and H lines and the Gap-lanes paragraph); and `architecture.worklist.json` (the rev1 scope rows, the Not-recorded seam, WL-32, WL-33, WL-47, WL-49, the ratification note and the lane entry).

**2026-09-15 — Maintainer decisions of 2026-09-15.** The entries through "The remaining agent-adopted defaults" record them, one per decision. Decision 1 restores the $5 signup grant in `create_org` and is recorded by #3055. Decision 10 approves the witness runner plane with a $200/month AWS budget cap and is recorded by #3062. Neither is repeated here.

**2026-09-15 — Organization: roles and workspaces are built on every tier, and the org-only workspace sentinel is shared.** Source: the maintainer's scope decisions of 2026-09-15 on #2964, applied by the lane's app half. Decided and applied: (1) the **Roles** page returns at `/{org}/roles` with `org.roles` on `list_iam_roles`, and **Workspaces** becomes a section of `/{org}` on `org.workspaces` (`list_workspaces` with `includeArchived`), so §1.2's "one real rev1 hole" is closed by an in-app `create_workspace` form beside `update_workspace_settings` and `archive_workspace`. (2) **No control is gated on the enterprise licence** (ADR-069, superseding ADR-063 decision 3): `create_role` and `set_role_grants` drop `enterprise_tier_required`, and the page prints `list_iam_roles.enforcement` either way, in the observe-mode words, because a role below the enterprise tier is recorded and not resolved. The delegation ceiling reads no tier (`packages/iam/src/delegation-ceiling.ts`), so the refusal protected nothing and the ceiling and role gate still do. (3) **`ORG_ONLY_WORKSPACE_ID` moves to `@oxagen/oxagen`** and `apps/api`'s `capabilityContext` uses it for an org-only mount, with `TenantScopeError` answered 400 `invalid_tenant_scope` (#3029, ADR-068); §3.2 step 4 is amended, since it said the sentinel lived in the app's builder alone. (4) **Invitations stay a read.** The mockup's Resend and Revoke have no contract on `app-rebuild`, and §1.1(b) lets a lane bind only what its backend half registered, so the People page keeps listing pending invitations and adds no write. SSO/SCIM, model funding and the data plane stay cut.

**2026-09-15 — Credit packs stay the in-app agent's top-up; GAU blocks stay the governed-action product (maintainer decision 2).**
- `purchase_credits` is not retired. The credit packs fund the ADR-053 platform-funded assistant balance.
- GAU blocks remain the product governed actions are bought in.
- `pnpm billing:stripe-sync` catalogues a GAU block product with a lookup key beside the packs (WL-57). The GAU Checkout and invoice lines name that product and price at the organisation's contracted rate.
- Credits never pay for GAUs.

**2026-09-15 — Enterprise is negotiated only, and every feature is on for every tier (maintainer decision 3).**
- **Enterprise.** Its terms come only from a `billing.contract_terms` row. `enterprise-v2` is removed from `SUBSCRIPTION_PLANS` and the Stripe catalogue (WL-56).
- **Gates.** No feature is gated on the enterprise licence, and IAM and the SOC 2 controls are on for every tier. WL-55 removes the gates:
  - the `tier_gate` step of `checkIAM` (`packages/iam/src/check-iam.ts:159`),
  - `canAccessACL`, `canAccessSSO`, `canAccessSCIM` and `canAccessAuditLog` (`packages/billing/src/entitlements.ts:93-118`),
  - `requireTier(…, "enterprise", …)` (`packages/handlers/src/prompt.settings.write.ts:39`).
- **What stays.** INV-29's handler checks stay after WL-55. §0 item 10, §1.5's roles paragraph and §3.2 describe the current tree, in which the resolver runs only for enterprise, until WL-55 lands.

Applied: §0 item 10; §1.5; §3.9 items 3 and 4; ADR-055 §2; metering spec §4.2 and §7.3; the SOP; WL-55; WL-56.

**2026-09-15 — Production takes the additive migrations now and the rest at cutover (maintainer decision 4, revised to option (a)).**
- **Asked.** Migrate the production database, then run `pnpm billing:stripe-sync` and `pnpm billing:price-book-sync` against production.
- **Found.** The `app-rebuild` migrations are not purely additive. Several rebuild CHECKs on existing tables, set NOT NULL on columns `main`'s writers omit, or drop a table `main` reads.
- **Decided, option (a).** Only the three additive ADR-060 cost migrations go to production now: `20260915020000_cost_price_book_and_rollups`, `20260915020100_rls_cost_tables` and `20260915020200_run_totals_operator_key`. #3080 carries them to `main`, and `infra/tools/run-db-migrations.sh` applies them from the post-merge `main` tip. `pnpm billing:price-book-sync --apply` then fills `cost.price_entries`.
- **At the WL-52 cutover.** Every other `app-rebuild` migration, the `billing.plans` upsert and `stripe-sync --apply` against production run with the cutover.
- **Applied.** The three migrations and `price-book-sync` ran in production on 2026-09-15; the entry "The three additive ADR-060 cost migrations are applied in production ahead of WL-52" records the run.
- **No worklist item.** The runs are operations; WL-52 (merge and deploy verification) carries the rest.

Applied: `docs/ops/stripe-product-sync-sop.md` §6.

**2026-09-15 — Switching invoice billing off turns the invoiced overage into purchased units (maintainer decision 5).**
- When `set_org_billing_terms` turns `approved_for_invoice_billing` off, the accrual close (§3.9 item 12) adds the current bucket's `overage_invoiced_gau` to `purchased_gau` and sets `overage_invoiced_gau` to 0, in the same transaction.
- The invoice is the purchase. The prepaid bucket opens at `remaining = 0` with no overdraft, and a later switch back to invoice billing counts nothing twice.
- Zeroing `overage_invoiced_gau` is WL-58's reading of "add"; its note says so.

Applied: §3.9 items 8d and 12; ADR-055 §7; metering spec §4.2; WL-58.

**2026-09-15 — `invoice_gau_max` bounds overage, and the interim invoice fires at unit max+1 (maintainer decision 6).**
- **What the cap counts.** Overage beyond the monthly allowance.
- **When it fires.** When uninvoiced overage exceeds the cap, which is at overage unit `invoice_gau_max`+1. The invoice is for exactly `invoice_gau_max` GAUs, and that extra unit starts the next accrual.
- **Example.** On Build (50,000 included) with the default 100,000 cap, the first interim invoice fires at GAU #150,001 of the month and the second at #250,001.
- **Today.** The recorder compares with `>=` (`packages/billing/src/action-metering.ts:324`); WL-59 changes it.

Applied: §3.9 items 6 and 8d; ADR-055 §7; metering spec §4.2; the closed openQuestion on the threshold; WL-59.

- **Upgrade.** `start_subscription_upgrade` stays. The Billing page's Plan card binds it through Stripe Checkout, resolving the price by its `stripe-sync` lookup key (WL-66).
- **Retirements.** `get_rate_card`, `preview_action_cost` and `get_evidence_retention` retire at cutover. WL-50 deletes their contracts, handlers, API routes, MCP tools and docs.

Applied: §1.2 Billing row; §1.4 Plan row; §3.9 item 11 and the contracts table; ADR-055 §12; metering spec §5.6; the closed openQuestions on the upgrade path, retention and cutover parity; WL-50; WL-66.

**2026-09-15 — The shell keeps the sidebar flyout (maintainer decision 9).**

- The 2026-09-14 bullet "Not changed: the shell's assistant flyout" left adoption open; this decision settles it.
- The §1.2 shell row and the #2968 lane note keep the flyout per ADR-053.

Applied: the §1.2 shell row; the 2026-09-14 bullet.

**2026-09-15 — Ledger-ingested runs can be halted and cancelled (maintainer decision 11).**
- **Today.** A ledger run has no connection point, and `dispatch_command` answers `no_connection_point` (`packages/handlers/src/tacho.command.dispatch.ts:15-18`).
- **The contract.** The ledger ingest contract is built now, with a revocable run token. A producer appends a ledger run's frames under a token that lasts at most fifteen minutes.
- **Cancel.** `cancel` on a ledger run revokes that token in the same transaction as the command row, so every later append is refused.
- **Still refused.** `steer`, `pause` and `resume` on a ledger run, until a producer carries them.

Applied: the §1.2 Run row; §1.3; ADR-056's 2026-09-15 amendment; WL-61.

**2026-09-15 — Invoice-billed orgs are suspended 5 days past due (maintainer decision 12).**
- **Trigger.** An invoice-billed org is suspended 5 days after an interim or period-close invoice is past due.
- **While suspended.** Metering continues: the recorder, the interim threshold and `billing.gau-close` keep accruing, closing and invoicing.
- **Reactivation.** Paying the full outstanding balance reactivates the org.
- **What it replaces.** The second rule §3.9 item 6 adopted from the billing amendment ("no automatic suspension in rev1"). The first rule, that an interim invoice does not move the monthly clock, is confirmed.
- **What suspension refuses.** Whatever the existing `billing_suspended` gate refuses (§3.9 item 9). WL-60's note names the one branch that changes if the maintainer wants a suspended org to keep governing.

Applied: §1.4 Billing mode row; §1.5 `resolve_approval` row; §3.9 item 6; ADR-055 §7; metering spec §4.2; the closed openQuestion on assumption 2; WL-60.

**2026-09-15 — Neo4j stays (maintainer decision 15).** Maintainer decision, 2026-09-15. Neo4j is not retired. It stays as the graph store, no ADR retires it, and no removal work is planned or scheduled. Earlier wording that the rev1 app target retires Neo4j with the ontology engine, and that Neo4j stays "through rev1" pending a retirement ADR written after the witness lane (#2955), is superseded by this entry. Nothing in rev1 removes a Neo4j dependency, schema runner, vector index, data plane or `NEO4J_*` variable: `packages/ontology/src/schema.cypher` and its three vector indexes (ADR-003), the connector dual-write to Postgres and Neo4j (ADR-012), the store boundary the platform manifest parses from that cypher file (ADR-031), the Neo4j plane in `packages/tenancy/src/data-plane.ts` (ADR-042, ADR-058), and the `NEO4J_*` names `instrumentation.ts`'s bootstraps read and `turbo.json`'s `test:e2e` env list carries (§6.3) all stay as they are. The Ontology page stays cut from `apps/app` (the scale-back entry of 2026-09-14, below): the app has no Ontology page, route, sidebar link or `UNRECORDED` row, and graph reads stay on the API and MCP surfaces. That cut removes the page, not the store. Applied: ADR-003 carries the same decision as an amendment. No worklist item changes, because `architecture.worklist.json` names no Neo4j work and no openQuestion asks which store holds a graph.

**2026-09-15 — Dependabot re-pins are compared by what each pin resolves to, and Dependabot gains ignore entries (maintainer decision 16a).**
- For #2989, each re-pin of a DoD caller stub is checked by comparing what the old pin and the new pin resolve to.
- Dependabot gains `ignore` entries for those stubs in the repositories that carry them, so `scr-corpus-check` stops going red on Dependabot's schedule.
- The work is tracked on #2989. It is outside `apps/app`, so no worklist item is added.

- The work is tracked on #2980. It is outside `apps/app`, so no worklist item is added.

**2026-09-15 — A forked run's grade seals when the fork's own attempt seals (maintainer decision 16d).**
- A forked attempt is graded by its own seal, from its own recording.
- The mint-at-fork path is deleted. That is `fork_run` minting an attempt under a ledger seal graded `fork`.
- No recorder writes such a seal, because a ledger seal grades at the `harness` tier (ADR-058 decision 3). Only a fabricated seal in `run.fork.test.ts` reaches the path (`packages/handlers/src/run.fork.ts:21-25`).

Applied: WL-64.

**2026-09-15 — The remaining agent-adopted defaults are confirmed; API key rotation ships in rev1 (maintainer decision, overrides).**
- **Overridden.** API key rotation ships in rev1: the API keys page gains Rotate on `rotate_api_key` (WL-63).
- **Confirmed as designed:**
  - `resolve_approval` is the only billable action, and membership writes are free (§1.5).
  - `set_org_billing_terms` is operator-script only (§3.9 item 12).
  - The action counters are dropped (WL-27).
  - The `billing.invoices` mirror is kept, and the settlement states stand as designed (§3.9 item 7).
  - Steering runs in team mode with N=3, M=2 and confidence 0.7 (ADR-061).
  - Sign-in is verify-by-link, with no SAML button, and `governance_mode` is not asked at signup.
  - The onboarding gate has the CLI installer and a provisional workspace, and the 7-day offer is deferred (#2967).
  - The permission catalogue has seven groups (ADR-063).
  - The agent definition lives in git, and spend budgets are in micros (ADR-057, ADR-060).
  - The fixture adapter is deleted (§0 item 5).
  - There are three notification events.
- **Defect found reviewing the confirmed settlement states.** An `auto_topup` row that the close job marks `failed` for `idempotency_key_expired` keeps the bucket's `open_topup_settlement_id` pointing at it. `packages/billing/src/gau-settlements.ts:625-637` calls `settleGauFailed`, whose `settlePending` (`:257`) changes only the status, so that month got no second automatic attempt. #3073 fixed it on `app-rebuild` (the entry below), so no worklist item carries it.

Applied: §1.2 API keys row; §1.5; §3.9 item 10; `architecture.worklist.json` openQuestions, each closed with its decision; WL-63.

**2026-09-15 — The onboarding gate returns as a rail over the real pages, and Register an agent is one route with three addressed steps.** Source: ADR-065 (the #2967 lane's backend half, #3030), which answers the openQuestion "Confirm dropping the onboarding gate" with the recommendation this document carried. Decided and applied in the app half: the gate is `org.onboarding_state`, read by `onboarding.state` on Fleet, where it draws the three-step rail, the provisional-workspace banner while no main repository is bound, and the first-run banner naming the run the first frame opened; `/welcome/*` stays deleted, and the sign-in flows keep their own pages. The register flow returns as `/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}` with `{step}` one of `name`, `wrap` and `run`, which is the lane's one route (§1.2, +1 on INV-20's count; with every lane landed the total is 26, #3097's audit route and #3098's skills route included). Four decisions the app half took inside that frame, each because the contracts as merged decide them: (1) the wrap step draws the path the agent's recorded harness takes rather than the mockup's three tabs, because `register_agent`'s harness enum carries no `codex` member and fixes the harness at registration, so three switchable tabs would be surface with no contract under them (§3.6); (2) `get_install_instructions` is not bound, because it answers the wrap steps only when handed an enrollment token, and a token exists only after the operator's write, so a read port for it would mint a token on render or take a secret as a read argument — `create_enrollment_token`'s own `enrollCommand` is the wrap command and the step prints it; (3) the first-frame wait is the contract's handler-side long poll read by the page, with the client island re-reading only while a host is enrolled, because WL-35 has not landed `src/server/sse.ts` and a client timer over a 0 ms read would turn the wait into one invoke per tick (§3.5's budget is kept: one invoke per 20 s wait); (4) the seven-day conversion offer is not built, because ADR-065 decision 3 leaves it to the pricing decision and the columns that would feed it are not in the table. `PageKey` gains an `onboarding` row (`onboarding_state_unavailable`, 503; permission `agent.register`). The `e2e/routes.ts` entry and the `page-load` step for the new route land with WL-47, which creates that file; the route's title key is in the catalog and its page test asserts the title and the h1 in the meantime.

**2026-09-15 — Rev1 keeps an in-app Build and Scale upgrade; Enterprise has no Checkout.** Maintainer decision, 2026-09-15, answering the openQuestion "No subscription-upgrade path exists in the app in rev1". Decided: the Billing page offers a Build or Scale upgrade through Stripe Checkout on the Stripe prices `pnpm billing:stripe-sync` writes to `billing.plans`, so `start_subscription_upgrade` is not retired; Enterprise is negotiated on its `contract_terms` row and has no Checkout. `purchase_credits` is bound on the Billing page by WL-67 (the two-meters entry below). The `pay` e2e stays the GAU purchase (§6.3). WL-44 does not carry the upgrade, because its paths are the app's purchase form and action and the upgrade needs its contract and handler changed first: `start_subscription_upgrade` does not declare `noBillingGate`, so the kernel meters an upgrade as a governed action and refuses it for a prepaid org at `remaining ≤ 0` (§1.5, INV-27); it takes absolute `successUrl` and `cancelUrl` where `purchase_gau_bucket` takes app-relative paths its handler prefixes with `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (§3.8); its handler checks only that a principal exists, throws untyped `Error`s (`packages/handlers/src/billing.subscription_upgrade.start.ts:13-28`) and calls no `assertOrgRole` (INV-29); and it passes any `planSlug` to `createCheckoutSession`. Applied: #3078's WL-66 carries the upgrade (contract, handler, the action beside `purchaseGau`, the Plan card's buttons); its acceptance names `noBillingGate`, `assertOrgRole` on the acting user and app-relative return paths, and the handler's untyped `Error`s are WL-66's to replace with `HandlerError`s; the §3.9 contracts row and item 11's subscription row name it; the openQuestion is marked answered. WL-44 restores `src/shared/checkout-url.ts` (`parseCheckoutUrl`, `ExternalCheckoutUrl`), which WL-13 removed while nothing bound it (#3047), and `redirectToCheckout` in `src/shared/navigation.ts`; WL-66's and WL-67's actions reuse both. At WL-50, ADR-068 names WL-66 as the item that restores `start_subscription_upgrade`'s `app` layer and WL-67 as the one that restores `purchase_credits`'.

**2026-09-15 — The three additive ADR-060 cost migrations are applied in production ahead of WL-52.** Source: maintainer decision 2026-09-15, option (a). #3080 (merged at `67b9715d0`) put `20260915020000_cost_price_book_and_rollups.sql`, `20260915020100_rls_cost_tables.sql` and `20260915020200_run_totals_operator_key.sql` on `main`, byte-identical to `app-rebuild`, with the regenerated `atlas.sum` and the four tenant-policy manifest entries (pin 96 on `main`). Applied on 2026-09-15 from `main` at `67b9715d0` by `infra/tools/run-db-migrations.sh --apply` on the app node, `ALLOW_DIRTY` unset. The dry run before it read Current Version 20260914120000, Next Version 20260915020000, 106 executed, 3 pending. The status after it read OK at 20260915020200, 109 executed, 0 pending. `atlas_schema_revisions` holds 20260915020000, 20260915020100 and 20260915020200; `cost.price_entries`, `cost.run_totals` and `cost.daily_totals` exist. `pnpm billing:price-book-sync --effective-from=2026-09-15T00:00:00Z --apply`, run from an `app-rebuild` checkout at `340420f10` through an SSM tunnel, wrote 192 list rows; a second run wrote 0 and left 192 unchanged.

- **The three files are frozen.** Production's `atlas_schema_revisions` records their hashes, so an edit to any of them on either branch is a checksum error at cutover. `20260915020100` is generated from `tenant-policy.manifest.ts` and is not regenerated; a later manifest change ships as a new migration file. The cutover's first step is `git diff --exit-code origin/main <cutover-ref> -- packages/database/atlas/migrations/20260915020000_cost_price_book_and_rollups.sql packages/database/atlas/migrations/20260915020100_rls_cost_tables.sql packages/database/atlas/migrations/20260915020200_run_totals_operator_key.sql`, and a non-empty diff stops the cutover.
- **The remaining `app-rebuild` migrations apply at cutover under non-linear execution order.** Production is at 20260915020200 and `app-rebuild` carries versions that sort before it, so the cutover apply runs with Atlas's non-linear order. The gate before that apply is a production dry run from the cutover ref that reads Next Version 20260914165423 and reports no checksum or history error; any other output stops the cutover.
- **The `billing.plans` upsert from `stripe-sync` runs after the cutover apply**, not before it. Only the price book ran ahead of the cutover.

**2026-09-15 — The steering writes and the agent-role writes gate the acting user; a failed auto top-up ends its episode.** Maintainer decision, 2026-09-15. Source: three defects found on `app-rebuild` after #3063. (1) `open_context_pr`, `propose_record`, `dismiss_proposal` and `append_record` (G2961-be, #3026) passed `ctx` itself to `assertOrgRole`, so an API-key call read `ctx.userId`, which is null: `propose_record` and `append_record` skipped the gate for a key, and `open_context_pr` and `dismiss_proposal` refused every key. #3026 merged before #3063's rule, so `role-check.test.ts` fails on those four calls. (2) `assign_agent_role` and `revoke_agent_role` ran no org role gate; the delegation ceiling runs only for an enterprise org (`agent.role.assign.ts`, `canAccessACL`), so on Free, Build and Scale any member could attach or detach an agent's roles. (3) A `pending` auto top-up the close job marks `failed` with `idempotency_key_expired` left `open_topup_settlement_id` naming it, because `settlePending` writes only the settlement, so the org got no further automatic top-up that month. Decided: the four steering writes follow the entry below: they resolve the acting user with `resolveActingUserId`, gate it unconditionally, and record it as the proposal's updater; the author columns of a proposal and an append stay the signed-in user, null for a key, so a Context PR stamps an agent's proposal `inferred` and the merge's separation-of-duties check reads a person. `merge_context_pr` keeps its reviewer gate on the signed-in user. `assign_agent_role` and `revoke_agent_role` gate org Owner or Admin for the acting user, the gate `create_role` and `set_role_grants` run; their contracts' `defaultRoles.workspace` becomes empty to match, and the acting user is the assigner the ceiling reads, replacing `resolveEffectiveAssigner`. `settleGauFailed` clears `open_topup_settlement_id` wherever it names an `auto_topup` row; `open` keeps the episode, because Stripe is still collecting. Applied: the six handlers and their tests (a key whose creator holds the role, one whose creator does not, one with no creator); `role-check.test.ts` (the four steering contracts join the handler-body array, following an exported handler into its same-file factory; a second array covers `resolve_approval`, `assign_agent_role` and `revoke_agent_role` through packages/agent's `LOADERS`; a probe for the factory shape gated on `ctx` inside a signed-in check); `gau-settlements.ts` and its tests; §3.9 items 7 and 8c; ADR-061; the capability docs of the six contracts. No contract's `surfaces` changes.

**2026-09-15 — A new organisation receives the $5 signup grant, and it funds the in-app agent (#2968).** Source: the openQuestion WL-16 left ("Confirm, or name where the grant should return when that lane ships") and the #2968 lane's NEEDS-MAC on decision 3. WL-16 deleted `grantFreeCredits` from `create_org`, so a new org's ADR-053 platform-funded assistant balance started at zero and `evaluateTurnCreditGate` refused its first platform-paid turn with `insufficient_credits`; `set_org_billing_terms` is an idempotent terms upsert and cannot carry a grant. Maintainer decision: the in-app agent's platform balance is funded by restoring the $5 signup grant in `create_org`. Applied: `packages/billing/src/grants.ts` exports `grantSignupCredits(tx, orgId)` (the `FREE_SIGNUP_CREDITS` path: 500 credits, a `grant_signup` ledger row keyed on the org, a non-expiring `free_grant` lot and the balance mirror, idempotent on the ledger key) and `grantFreeCredits` wraps it for the deprecated onboarding action; `organizationCreateHandler` calls it on the bootstrap transaction, so a failed grant rolls the org back. `create_org` still writes no `contract_terms`, `gau_buckets`, `gau_settlements` or `org_billing_settings` row. The grant is never invoiced and buys no GAU (ADR-055); it is the assistant balance only. §3.9 item 14 and §7's row say so; ADR-055 §13 and ADR-053's #2968 amendment carry the dated line; `org.create.test.ts` and `grants.test.ts` hold the call and the rows, and `org.create.pg.test.ts` asserts one ledger row, one 500-cent lot and one balance row and nothing else under `billing.*`; the openQuestion in `architecture.worklist.json` is answered.

**2026-09-15 — An API key acts as its creator at every role gate.** Source: the open question WL-28 (#3027), WL-29 (#3006), G2953-be (#3013) and G2958-be (#3025) carried to the maintainer: may an API key act as its creator for a role-gated capability? The MCP server builds every `CapabilityContext` with `userId: null` and so does an API-key call on the API (`apps/mcp/src/context.ts`, `apps/api/src/middleware/auth.ts`), so `assertOrgRole` refused both with `forbidden / no_principal`, and a role-gated MCP tool such as `purchase_gau_bucket`, `set_auto_topup`, `list_invoices` or `dispatch_command` had no possible outcome but 403. G2964-be (#3028) added `resolveActingUserId` in `packages/iam/src/org-role.ts`, the key-to-creator mapping the kernel's enterprise IAM path already makes (`fetch-authz.ts`), and used it in six handlers. Decided (the orchestrator, under the maintainer's goal to finish outstanding work, consistent with #3028): an API key acts as its creator (`auth.api_keys.created_by_id`), bounded by the creator's current org and workspace roles, read on every call; a deleted key, a key of another org and a key with no recorded creator are refused `no_principal`. The rule is uniform: every handler that calls `assertOrgRole` passes it the user `resolveActingUserId` returned and records that user as the actor. A key does not carry a role of its own; a dedicated service principal per key stays the durable model `docs/specs/rbac-permissions-plane.md` names. `authorize_cli` keeps its session-only consent guard, so no key's creator consents to a CLI login. Applied: `resolve_approval`, `authorize_cli`, `get_contract_rate`, `get_subscription`, `get_gau_bucket`, `list_invoices`, `purchase_gau_bucket`, `set_auto_topup`, `export_run`, `summarize_run`, `fork_run`, `dispatch_command`, `register_agent`, `rotate_agent_credential`, `suspend_agent`, `retire_agent` and `commit_agent_definition` resolve the acting user (the four agent-identity writes and `commit_agent_definition` drop their session-only pre-guard), each with a test for a key whose creator holds the role, a key whose creator does not, and a key with no creator; `packages/handlers/src/role-check.test.ts` gains the rule over every `assertOrgRole` call under `packages/*/src`, with probes that pass a resolved const, an inline resolve and a shorthand `userId`, and fail `ctx` itself, `ctx.userId`, and a resolve whose result is not passed; §3.2 and INV-29; the capability docs of those contracts. No contract's `surfaces` changes in this entry: the contracts that dropped `mcp` for want of this decision (the G2958-be tools lane, `export_run`, `summarize_run`, `fork_run`, the agent-identity writes) may add the tool back in their own lane.

**2026-09-15 — `instrumentation.ts` imports the four bootstrap entries by name (#3048).** Source: #3048. `instrumentation.ts` is the process bootstrap. It imports `initTracer` and `recordSecurityEvent` from `@oxagen/telemetry`, `makeSecurityEventInserter` from `@oxagen/database/security` and `assertRlsConnectionSafe` from `@oxagen/database`; §2's row named only the kernel gate setters and the five `bootstrap*` entries, so the import graph carried the four as baseline entries. `apps/api/src/bootstrap.ts` and `apps/mcp/src/middleware.ts` wire the same four. One shared bootstrap package for three call sites was #2342's scope, closed as not planned on 2026-09-07, and is not built. Applied: §2's `instrumentation.ts` row names the four bindings; `src/test/arch/layers.ts` admits them by binding (`PLATFORM_NAMED_ROWS`), so any other binding from those three specifiers, a namespace import or a side-effect import is refused; probe `instrumentation-bootstrap-entries.ts` passes at `instrumentation.ts` and fails at `server/kernel.ts` and `data/live/audit.ts`, probe `instrumentation-fifth-entry.ts` (`withSystemDb` beside `assertRlsConnectionSafe`) fails at `instrumentation.ts`, and `tenancy.ts`, `telemetry-namespace.ts` and `telemetry-chselect.ts` are placed at `instrumentation.ts` and fail; the four entries leave `baseline.json` and `baseline.test.ts`'s WL-50 list; `architecture.worklist.json` WL-50 says so. INV-23 holds unchanged: `instrumentation.ts` is not edited and `instrumentation.test.ts` still asserts the five bootstraps.

**2026-09-15 — The invitation page reads through the viewer seam (#3049).** Source: #3049. `features/auth/invitations.ts` imported `systemLookups` from `@/server/tenancy-lookups`, which WL-07's acceptance asked for and §2's features row refuses. `requireInvitee` could not serve the page: it redirects a signed-out visitor, and `/invite/[token]` renders for one. `src/server/viewer.ts` gains `readInvitation(token): Promise<InvitationRecord | null>`: no session, no interrupt, nothing minted; a malformed token is null before any lookup, an unknown one is null, and the record comes back whatever its status or expiry, because `decideInvitation` closes an expired invitation. `requireInvitee` reads through it. The `InvitationView` mapping stays in `features/auth`, because §2's `src/server/**` row does not import `@/data/contracts`; `viewer.ts` re-exports the `InvitationRecord` type for it. §2's features row is unchanged. `resolveInvitee`, which §1.2 and §3.1 named and no code implemented, is replaced in both by `readInvitation` plus `decideInvitation`, and §3.1's `requireInvitee` returns the record, as the code does. Applied: §1.2's `/invite/[token]` row; §3.1; `viewer.test.ts` (a valid token, an expired invitation, an unknown token and a malformed token) and `features/auth/invitations.test.ts` (the expired invitation decides as `closed`/`expired`); probe `features-imports-tenancy-lookups.ts` fails `layer` at `features/auth/invitations.ts` and passes at `server/viewer.ts`, probe `features-imports-viewer-invitation.ts` passes at `features/auth/invitations.ts`; the entry leaves `baseline.json` and `baseline.test.ts`'s WL-50 list; `architecture.worklist.json`'s viewer seam contract and WL-50 acceptance.

**2026-09-15 — The arch baseline empties at WL-50.** Source: the review of WL-13 (#3047). WL-13's acceptance asked for an empty `src/test/arch/baseline.json`. After WL-39 (#3046) its 10 remaining entries are removed by WL-47 and WL-48 (the missing e2e files) and by WL-50: the deprecated e2e suite, the #3048 decision on `instrumentation.ts`'s four platform imports and the #3049 decision on `features/auth/invitations.ts`'s `@/server/tenancy-lookups` import, which WL-07 asks for and §2's features row refuses. WL-13 depends on none of them. Applied: §8's WL-13 and WL-50 lines; `architecture.worklist.json` WL-13 (knip baseline empty; `baseline.json` pinned by `src/test/arch/baseline.test.ts`), WL-34 (no `baseline.json` entry added), WL-47 and WL-48 (each names the entries it removes) and WL-50 (removes the last entries and asserts `[]`).

**2026-09-15 — Oxagen charges on two meters: governed actions in GAUs, and in-app AI usage in usage credits.** Maintainer decision, 2026-09-15. (1) **Governed actions**, in governed action units: list $5 per 1,000 (5,000 micros per GAU), sold in 5,000-GAU blocks at $25; Free includes 5,000 GAU a month, Build is $199 a month with 50,000, Scale is $999 a month with 300,000; Enterprise is negotiated on its `billing.contract_terms` row; every feature is on every tier; the volume bands are $5, $4, $3 and $2 per 1,000 (metering spec §4.1). `resolve_approval` is the billable governed action and membership writes are free (§1.5). (2) **In-app AI usage**, the in-app agent's model calls, in usage credits: 1 credit = $0.01, metered by the credit gate at provider cost times the meter markup (`packages/billing/src/pricing.ts`), funded by the $5 signup grant `create_org` writes (#3055) and topped up with credit packs (`purchase_credits`). Tokens are not passed through at cost. Proven spend stays a report figure. The meters share no balance: credits never buy GAUs, and a GAU block never adds credits. The Billing page shows both meters: the GAU bucket with the contracted rate, and the usage credit balance with a top-up. Maintainer decision 2 of 2026-09-15 as #3078 records it keeps `purchase_credits` as the in-app agent's top-up and off the Billing page; this decision puts the balance and the top-up on the page. Two contract defects surface with it: `purchase_credits` declares no `noBillingGate`, so a credit top-up is a governed action today and a prepaid org at `remaining ≤ 0` is refused it; and its handler checks only that a principal exists, so on Free, Build and Scale any member may start one (`checkIAM` allows every capability below enterprise, §1.5). WL-67 fixes both with the section. Applied: §0 item 2; §1.4 (the In-app AI usage row); §3.9 (the second-meter paragraph); INV-25; §8 (WL-67 in phase F); `architecture.worklist.json` WL-67 and INV-25, WL-67 taking the id after #3078's WL-66; `docs/specs/governed-action-metering.md` §4.7; ADR-055's 2026-09-15 amendment; `docs/ops/stripe-product-sync-sop.md` (Two meters).

**2026-09-14 — Mockup refresh: the design of record is the Mockups repo at `origin/main`, per page, and four things the mockup gained since `mc-baseline-w4` get an architecture presence.** Source: a delta comparison of `mc.html` on Mockups `origin/main` (13,640 lines) against this document, the two canonical `docs/` files and the open `kind:gap` issues. The rule applied: the maintainer's scope decisions of 2026-09-14 win over the mockup, and where the mockup adds or changes a screen inside the kept scope, this document and `architecture.worklist.json` follow it.

- **Source of truth, stated.** The Status block gains a **Design source of truth** row: Mockups `origin/main`; `mc.html` is the one design; `pages/<page>.md` is the per-page design of record a lane reads; `docs/2026-09-11-oxagen-mission-control-spec.md` and `docs/2026-09-12-mission-control-app-implementation-plan.md` are the canonical spec and plan, and `~/Documents/Oxagen/Specs` is stale and is not read. §5 says why a page file and not a line number: `48f5e8a` (the Ontology removal) shifted `mc.html` by 502 insertions and 900 deletions on 2026-09-14, so every `mc.html:NNNN` written before it lands roughly 557 lines off mid-file and 613 off late-file. Every `#2950`–`#2970` body was re-cited onto `pages/<page>.md` in the same change.
- **Fleet gains the stat strip, as two tiles.** WL-34 ships Live runs and Waiting on a human, both countable from `list_runs` and `list_approvals` with no new contract; Spend today and Cache hit rate land with #2962's app half, which renders them on the Fleet route ahead of WL-34 (maintainer decision, 2026-09-15: the Spend lane ships with Fleet's two tiles). The alternative was WL-34 shipping a half-strip with no rule, or omitting the strip a design partner sees first.
- **The runs table draws the agent identity without the two scores.** `agentCard` (`mc.html:11583-11596`) is the mockup's only agent-identity component, in three layouts, and carries a Trust and a Spend pill on every one, coloured by a nearest-rank percentile of the whole Oxagen population. The pills and the percentile machinery are cut (#2969 closed), so rev1 renders avatar, key and harness label and no `scorePair`; the §1.2 Fleet row says so, because copying the component in wholesale is the obvious way to bring the cut half back.
- **`list_runs` excludes in-app-agent runs from its first version.** Mockups `71bc546`: the assistant is Oxagen's, the customer talks to it but never owns or manages it, so its turns are recorded as runs and never listed as the tenant's — the mockup keeps them in a separate `ASST_RUNS` list that Fleet's `RUNS.filter` (`mc.html:3990`) never reads. WL-18 lands in phase D, long before the #2968 lane; without the rule in its acceptance, #2968 would have to change a shipped contract's result set. Added to WL-18's acceptance and to the §1.2 Fleet row.
- **The phone shell is named.** `bcc7049` added a full phone treatment; the word `MobileNav` appeared three times in this document and nowhere as a thing with parts. The §1.2 shell row and WL-32 now carry the five-slot thumb bar, the More sheet, bottom-sheet dialogs, card tables, the drawer scrim, 44 px targets, 16 px inputs and safe-area insets, and say that the Agents, Tools and Spend slots point at `NotRecorded` pages for the whole of rev1 **deliberately** — the bar is the phone's only navigation and a four-slot bar would change again at every lane. The mockup's `audit` More-sheet entry is dropped with the page.
- **Every Run tab has an owner.** `runTabs` (`mc.html:4783-4790`) gives a non-witness run seven tabs and four had no lane. Assigned: Transcript and Governed actions/Player → #2952, Proof → #2955, Cost → #2962, Policy → #2958, Chain and seal → #2951, Context → cut (#2954 closed). A witness run renders its own four-tab set (Ladder, Frames, Cost, Chain and seal, `mc.html:4776-4781`) on the same `/runs/{run}` route, so #2955 adds a variant and no route.
- **Route counts move by a stated amount per lane.** INV-20 pins 19 `page.tsx` files and 19 `e2e/routes.ts` entries and said only that a lane adding a route adds its entry. §1.2 now states each lane's delta before it starts: #2956 +2 (agent detail `pAgent`, and the `.oxagen/agents/<slug>.toml` definition source editor `pAgentSource` that `bcc7049` added with its own loading, error and denied states), #2957 +1 (mandate detail `pMandate`), #2964 +1 (`/{org}/roles`, deleted by WL-33 and re-added by the lane), #2967 +1 (`/{org}/{ws}/register/{step}`, ADR-065 decision 1), #3097 +1 (`/{org}/audit`), #3098 +1 (`/{org}/{ws}/skills`), every other kept lane +0. With every lane landed the total is 26. Stated absolutely rather than as a chain of moves, because these lanes land in any order and a chain reads correctly in only one of them.
